# Bug audit — 2026-11-14

Current inventory: **427 numbered concerns**. Entries distinguish source-confirmed defects, qualified hypotheses/contracts, and known residuals; this is not a claim of 427 new or runtime-reproduced bugs.

## Scope and method

Read-only parallel source audit. Production code is not modified. Findings are appended as they arrive and checked against current source; no builds, tests, or formatters are run for this investigation.

Audit slices:
- Rust RPC and stream lifecycle: cancellation, deadlines, ordering, cleanup.
- Persistence and replay: durability, recovery, offsets, retention.
- TypeScript and Python SDKs: conversion, lifecycle, error propagation, binding contracts.
- CLI: command routing, state handling, exit status, input/output contracts.
- Parent pass: event-bus shutdown and consumer lifecycle.

Tooling limits: `codegraph` is not installed on the current PATH; no language servers are configured. Evidence comes from current source and focused literal searches.

## Evidence standard

Each concern records severity, confidence/status, source locations, trigger, consequence, and verification or remaining uncertainty. Source-confirmed findings are not runtime reproductions. Possible defects remain explicitly unconfirmed; rejected candidates and duplicates are retained separately when relevant. Security-sensitive concerns stay in this local internal report.

## Findings

First pass complete: four subagents plus the parent bus/shard investigation produced 31 numbered entries in this section. BUS-03 retains an unproved data-loss impact; SDK-02 and SDK-05 retain contract uncertainty; RPC-04 is linked to a previously documented related concern. Material rejected candidates are preserved in the provenance notes. Verification was current-source tracing only; no runtime reproductions, tests, builds, or production changes.

### BUS-01 — Cancelled shutdown cannot be resumed

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed control-flow defect, not runtime-reproduced.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/bus.rs:1388-1455` permanently changes `shutdown` from false to true and makes every later caller wait only for `shutdown_completed`. The winning future then awaits producers (`1497-1533`), the scaling monitor (`1571-1573`), drain workers (`1576-1606`), batch workers (`1628-1631`), and adapter operations (`1644-1652`) before publishing completion (`1735`). There is no cancellation guard or independently owned shutdown task.
- **Trigger:** Keep an `Arc<EventBus>`, begin `shutdown_via_ref()`, then drop/abort that future at a pending await (for example an application-level timeout). Invoke shutdown again.
- **Consequence:** No caller can take over the remaining teardown. Subsequent calls time out after the fixed wait, and completion never arrives. Cancellation before `drain_finalize_ready` is published can leave drain workers parked until their own 10-second fallback (`2452-2458`); cancellation after taking worker handles can detach workers while bus-owned senders remain open.
- **Repair direction:** Give teardown an independently owned task and shared terminal result, so callers may cancel their wait without cancelling teardown.
- **Duplicate check:** Focused search of earlier bug audits and code reviews found related shutdown fixes, but no matching cancellation-safety finding.

### BUS-02 — Adapter shutdown failure becomes success for later callers

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/bus.rs:1650-1652` retains the adapter's inner `Result`; `1734-1736` sets `shutdown_completed = true` and returns that result even when it is `Err`. The losing-CAS path at `1438-1455` returns `Ok(())` as soon as that flag is true. The public completion documentation at `1747-1749` instead says “success path only.”
- **Trigger:** `Adapter::shutdown()` returns an error before its timeout. A concurrent or subsequent caller invokes `shutdown_via_ref()`.
- **Consequence:** The first caller receives the failure, but other callers receive success for the same teardown. `is_shutdown_completed()` also reports true despite its success-only contract.
- **Repair direction:** Store and replay a shared terminal outcome; distinguish teardown-finished from teardown-succeeded in the completion contract.
- **Duplicate check:** Earlier review `CODE_REVIEW_2026_05_01_BUGFIXES_7.md` CR-25 covers premature success on the waiting deadline, not loss of the first caller's terminal error.

### BUS-03 — Explicit flush errors are silently discarded during shutdown

- **Severity / status:** Medium concern; discarded result source-confirmed, concrete built-in-adapter data-loss scenario not established.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/bus.rs:1644-1649` checks only the outer timeout result with `.is_err()`. `Ok(Err(adapter_error))` is neither propagated nor logged; even an elapsed flush timeout is only logged. The final return comes solely from `adapter.shutdown()` (`1650-1652`, `1736`). `net/crates/net/src/adapter/mod.rs:166-175` defines explicit flush as persistence of buffered data and also asks shutdown to flush pending data.
- **Trigger / consequence:** A flush operation fails, followed by a successful shutdown. The explicit flush failure disappears from the API and logs. Data loss depends on whether that adapter's shutdown successfully repeats the failed persistence work; it is not proved here.
- **Repair direction:** Inspect both timeout and inner flush results, preserve diagnostics, and define whether a successful shutdown supersedes an earlier flush failure.

### BUS-04 — Scale-down timeout races stranded flush against a detached worker

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed race, not runtime-reproduced.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/bus.rs:827-830` removes the shard and captures stranded ring events. At `862-901`, timed-out drain/batch handles are detached, but their sequence mirror is still read and used for a new stranded batch at `919-938`. The detached batch worker can continue receiving queued batches and flushing (`2346-2410`); each future flush obtains its sequence from worker-local state, then publishes the updated mirror (`net/crates/net/src/shard/batch.rs:350-375`). Loading the mirror does not reserve the stranded batch's sequence range.
- **Trigger:** Scale down a shard with both stranded ring events and older batches queued/in progress. Slow adapter operations, enough queued batches, or retries keep the worker alive beyond its `2 × adapter_timeout` join bound. The stranded flush uses the current mirror before the worker forms its next queued batch.
- **Reachability detail:** Finalization observes an empty ring but does not wait for already-selected producers (`net/crates/net/src/shard/mapper.rs:1285-1320`). An ingest that captured the old shard before draining can still enqueue before `remove_shard` captures the stranded ring (`net/crates/net/src/shard/mod.rs:730-745`); there is no shard-local retirement check.
- **Consequence:** The stranded batch and a later detached-worker batch can use the same `sequence_start`. On JetStream their message IDs collide and distinct events can be deduplicated away; even without dedup, newer stranded events can overtake older queued events.
- **Repair direction:** Do not independently dispatch a stranded batch while the old worker may still allocate sequences. Preserve one owner for ordered draining and sequence allocation through the timeout path.
- **Prior related finding:** `BUG_AUDIT_2026_05_03.md` item 28 marks this pattern fixed in the **add-shard rollback** path. This finding concerns the current **remove-shard** path, which still dispatches after timeout.

### BUS-05 — Late producer can successfully enqueue into a removed shard

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed race, not runtime-reproduced.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/shard/mod.rs:730-745` resolves and retains a routing-table snapshot before taking the shard lock, then pushes without revalidating membership. `remove_shard` drains under that lock (`1128-1135`) but releases it before publishing the table without the shard (`1137-1160`). `Shard::try_push_raw` (`180-216`) has no retired/closed state. The old table keeps the removed shard object alive.
- **Trigger:** Producer P selects an active shard and retains its old table, then pauses before locking. Scale-down marks/finalizes that shard, performs the final ring drain and removes it; its drain worker observes removal and exits. P resumes, locks the old shard object, and pushes successfully.
- **Consequence:** Ingestion reports success, but neither the new routing table nor any remaining drain worker will visit that ring. The accepted event is silently lost when the stale table/shard is released.
- **Repair direction:** Mark the shard retired under the same shard lock as the final drain, and make writers reject/re-resolve that state before accepting data. A table swap alone does not quiesce snapshot holders.
- **Duplicate check:** The May 18 phase-3 bus/shard review discusses stale snapshots in an add-shard, self-healing scenario (F-5); it does not establish safety of late writes after remove-shard's final drain.

### PERSIST-01 — Empty retention compaction resets sequence allocation on restart

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed by PersistenceAudit and parent source checks; not runtime-reproduced.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/file.rs:155-156` derives `next_seq` solely from the last recovered index entry, defaulting to zero. Retention publishes only surviving entries/timestamps via `compact_to` (`1392-1397`), without an independent allocation watermark.
- **Trigger:** Persist events, evict all of them through retention, then close/reopen before another append.
- **Consequence:** The reopened channel reuses sequence numbers. Resume cursors, tokens and derived-state checkpoints can skip new events or refer to different content under an old sequence.
- **Contract / repair:** The in-memory `tail_signals_lagged_when_all_retained_dropped` witness preserves a nonzero allocation watermark; persist that watermark independently of surviving records, including empty generations.

### PERSIST-02 — Missing timestamp sidecar can trigger destructive empty-log recovery

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed; requires external deletion/corruption of the timestamp sidecar.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/disk.rs:1859-1862,1999-2036` rejects even a manifest-selected generation unless `idx`, `dat` and `ts` all exist. With no complete candidate it creates generation 1; the subsequent open cleanup sweeps other generations (`629`). Missing timestamps otherwise have an explicit fallback (`disk.rs:2243-2245`; `redex/file.rs:159-175`).
- **Trigger:** Compact to generation 2 or later, allow predecessor cleanup, lose only the live `ts` file, then reopen.
- **Consequence:** Intact index and payload files are abandoned and then deleted in favor of a new empty generation.
- **Repair direction:** Distinguish committed generations from staging candidates. Recover an optional sidecar or fail closed without replacing/sweeping existing data.

### PERSIST-03 — Append after combined-corruption recovery disappears on next reopen

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed recovery/append ordering; not runtime-reproduced.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/disk.rs:514-544` trims a non-monotonic suffix only in memory when checksum filtering already removed entries. The actual index remains unchanged; its writer is reopened append-only (`583-588`). The existing `recovery_seq_trim_is_in_memory_only_after_checksum_drop` fixture (`3182-3250`) establishes surviving in-memory sequences `[0,2]` with the bad suffix still on disk.
- **Trigger:** Recover that combined checksum/non-monotonic corruption, append sequence 3, sync, and reopen again.
- **Consequence:** The old non-monotonic suffix is encountered before the newly appended index entry, so recovery discards the acknowledged new append as part of the suffix.
- **Repair direction:** Durably publish the recovered index/timestamp pair before allowing new appends, preferably in a fresh generation; otherwise reject writes until recovery is committed.

### PERSIST-04 — CortEX accepts an incomplete retained replay as full catch-up

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/cortex/adapter.rs:883-925` reads `[resume, head)` after `Lagged` but rejects only an entirely empty result. `RedexFile::read_range` omits evicted entries; the loop does not require the first returned event to equal `resume`, nor check sequence continuity.
- **Trigger:** Append sequences 0–4, retain only 3–4, then create a from-beginning materializer. Tail signals lag; direct catch-up returns the nonempty suffix `[3,4]`.
- **Consequence:** State folds only the suffix and `folded_through_seq` advances to 4. `wait_for_seq(4)` can succeed despite omitted history; the stricter applied watermark cannot cross the missing prefix and applied waits can remain pending.
- **Repair direction:** Check the full expected sequence range before folding catch-up results; surface unrecoverable history loss instead of advancing past it.

### PERSIST-05 — Full live-tail buffer permanently stops a recoverable materializer

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/file.rs:1693-1709` handles a full watcher channel by trying to put `Lagged` into that same full channel, then drops the sender. Without an intervening receive, the error is lost and the receiver eventually gets EOF. CortEX unconditionally exits on EOF (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/cortex/adapter.rs:863-866`), bypassing catch-up.
- **Trigger:** A producer overflows the live tail buffer while the fold task is busy or unscheduled.
- **Consequence:** After draining buffered events, materialization stops permanently even while its source remains open and the missed events are still readable.
- **Repair direction:** Treat unexpected EOF on an open source as lag and enter continuity-checked catch-up/resubscription. `RedexIndex` already distinguishes this case using `is_closed()`.
- **Qualification:** A receiver racing the second send can make explicit `Lagged` delivery succeed; the no-intervening-receive case still deterministically loses the signal.

### PERSIST-06 — Append can commit after close's final durability barrier

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed lifecycle race.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/file.rs:470-475` checks closure before acquiring the state lock and does not recheck inside it. `close` sets closed, disconnects watchers under that lock, releases it, then syncs and returns (`1564-1599`). PersistenceAudit found the same check ordering in the other append/skip mutators.
- **Trigger:** Pause an append after its initial closure check, let close finish, then resume the append.
- **Consequence:** The append succeeds on a closed file after subscribers have been disconnected and after the advertised final sync. With `FsyncPolicy::Never`, no later automatic durability barrier remains.
- **Repair direction:** Recheck closure under the state lock in every mutating path so close and append share a lifecycle linearization point.

### PERSIST-07 — Manager close/reopen can race two independent disk handles

- **Severity / status:** High; source-supported concurrency defect; detailed corruption interleaving not runtime-reproduced.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/manager.rs:945-946` removes the map entry before closing the file, allowing `open_file` to construct another disk segment for the same directory while the old one is still closing/appending. The neighboring `close_and_unlink_file` explicitly holds an entry guard for its lifecycle sequence (`958-965`).
- **Trigger:** An old append writes payload bytes but has not written its index record. Concurrent close removes its entry and waits for the append; concurrent reopen recovers the old index and truncates its apparent unreferenced payload tail. The old append then writes its index and reports success.
- **Consequence:** Acknowledged data can reference truncated bytes; the reopened handle also starts with an independent stale index and cached lengths.
- **Repair direction:** Serialize close/removal with reopening through the same per-channel entry guard, retaining exclusive lifecycle ownership until close finishes.

### PERSIST-08 — Checksum recovery misaligns persisted timestamps on the next restart

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/disk.rs:555-574` rewrites timestamps to include checksum survivors only, while retaining the unfiltered on-disk index. On the next open, `read_timestamps` expects the original index count and rejects the shortened sidecar (`2243-2261`); `redex/file.rs:159-175` then substitutes current timestamps.
- **Trigger:** Corrupt the second payload of a four-record log, recover once, then reopen again without compaction. The existing `checksum_filter_preserves_ts_pairing_on_mid_file_drop` fixture covers only the first recovery.
- **Consequence:** Original ages of surviving events are lost. Repeated restarts can keep resetting their ages and delay age-based retention indefinitely.
- **Repair direction:** Publish the recovered index and timestamps together, or retain original timestamp positions until the index is durably rewritten.

Persistence duplicate/provenance note: Focused searches of prior internal audits found related retention/recovery topics but no exact matches for these eight findings. The subagent explicitly rejected post-rename manifest-fsync error swallowing as intentional, documented policy; it is not counted as a bug.

### SDK-01 — Python file context manager suppresses durability failures

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed; not fault-injected.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/sdk-py/src/net_sdk/redex.py:66-74` catches and discards every `RedexError` from `f.close()`. Native close propagates core errors (`bindings/python/src/cortex.rs:1061-1067`); core close already handles repeated closure and performs a fallible final sync (`src/adapter/net/redex/file.rs:1564-1599`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** A persistent file's final sync fails on context exit. The `with open_file_cm(...)` statement reports success although its durability barrier failed.
- **Repair direction:** Propagate close failures; preserve/chaining any simultaneous body exception rather than treating every storage error as harmless idempotence.

### SDK-02 — Stream stop does not stop buffered-batch delivery

- **Severity / status:** Medium concern; behavior source-confirmed, exact intended treatment of fetched events undocumented.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/sdk-ts/src/stream.ts:65-97` checks `aborted` only before poll, then yields the whole response without another check. Python has the same pattern in both iterators (`sdk-py/src/net_sdk/stream.py:89-108,133-156`).
- **Trigger:** Fetch a multi-event batch, stop after consuming the first event, then request another item; or stop while an async poll is pending.
- **Consequence:** Additional events still arrive after stop, including the entire pending response in the second case. Typed/channel wrappers inherit this behavior.
- **Repair direction:** Specify whether stop drains buffered events; for immediate-stop semantics, check after polling and before each yield.

### SDK-03 — Python custom channel parsers receive transport envelopes

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed mismatch with the helper's payload-only contract.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/sdk-py/src/net_sdk/channel.py:139-154` wraps scalars/lists and escapes sole-`_value` objects. Custom parsing only removes `_channel` (`157-177,264-272`), whereas default parsing also unwraps once (`282-289`).
- **Trigger:** Publish `42`, `[1,2]`, or `{"_value":42}` to a channel using `parse=json.loads` or a scalar/list validator.
- **Consequence:** Custom parsing gets the `_value` envelope instead of the original payload, yielding wrong types/content or rejecting a valid event.
- **Repair direction:** Decode routing metadata and exactly one transport envelope before serializing the payload for a custom parser.

### SDK-04 — Python slot serialization includes synthetic slots and loses private ones

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed from implementation and Python slot semantics; not executed.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/sdk-py/src/net_sdk/channel.py:116-130` iterates declared slot names verbatim with `hasattr/getattr`, without excluding `__weakref__` or resolving per-class private-name mangling.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Publish a slotted object declaring `("value", "__weakref__")`: an active weak reference makes the serialized dictionary contain a non-JSON weak-reference object; otherwise an unwanted field is added. A declared `__value` slot is silently omitted because the stored attribute is name-mangled. Channel publish/batch and node emit paths reuse this conversion.
- **Repair direction:** Exclude synthetic slots, resolve private slots per declaring class, and consistently merge real instance fields.

### SDK-05 — Supplied Python channel filters replace channel isolation

- **Severity / status:** Potential medium; behavior source-confirmed, **intent ambiguous—not a definite bug**.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/sdk-py/src/net_sdk/channel.py:260-262,299-301` installs the channel filter only when no custom filter exists. `sdk-py/tests/test_channel_subscribe_opts.py:82-95` explicitly requires replacement behavior. The TypeScript channel combines the predicates instead (`sdk-ts/src/channel.ts:241-247`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Subscribe to one channel with a generic payload filter; matching events from other channels can enter the typed stream, which strips their channel tag.
- **Decision needed:** Decide whether custom filters refine channel scope or intentionally override it; align/document the cross-SDK contract before changing this behavior.

SDK provenance note: Focused prior-audit searches found no exact matches. Rejected candidates: watch-iterator rejection/break cleanup exists; retained TypeScript subscriptions use weak native node references; no current fallible Python CortEX close path was established; primary mesh option forwarding and stream-stat conversions produced no additional supported defect.

### CLI-01 — Concurrent channel-gate updates lose persisted access gates

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed race, not runtime-reproduced.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/cli/src/commands/channel.rs:516-520` performs an unguarded read/modify/write/register sequence on persisted channel entries. Control sessions run concurrently (`commands/lifecycle.rs:3058-3076`) and each channel-serve operation independently runs in `spawn_blocking` (`3180-3189`). Startup restores only persisted entries (`3590-3593`).
- **Trigger:** Two `channel serve` operations for different channels read the same original `channels.json`, then each publishes its own updated list.
- **Consequence:** Both live gates can be registered while only one update survives on disk; restart silently loses the other access gate.
- **Repair direction:** Serialize the complete operation through shared node state and use exclusively owned staging files.

### CLI-02 — Blob receives share a truncating staging pathname

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed concurrency/alias hazard.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/cli/src/commands/transfer.rs:759-775` opens the deterministic `<out>.partial` with truncating `File::create`; commit renames that same pathname (`790-805`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Concurrent receives to the same output write through independent handles to one staging file. Verified chunks can overwrite each other; one invocation can publish corrupted bytes while the other still writes. A pre-existing staging symlink is followed and its target truncated even if acquisition later fails.
- **Repair direction:** Use unique, exclusively created sibling staging files; publish only the file owned by that invocation. Continued writes after rename depend on platform open-file rename semantics.

### CLI-03 — Identity no-overwrite check is not enforced at publication

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed TOCTOU on platforms allowing replacement rename.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/cli/src/commands/identity.rs:244-257` checks existence when `--force` is absent, but publication unconditionally renames the staged seed (`667`). Exclusive temporary creation protects only the temporary file (`640-649`).
- **Trigger:** Two processes generate an identity to the same initially absent output path without `--force`.
- **Consequence:** Both can report success while the second replaces the first identity, potentially destroying the only persisted seed already consumed from the first result.
- **Repair direction:** Carry overwrite policy to atomic publication; use the existing no-clobber convention in `org::publish_staged` (`commands/org.rs:2113-2140`).

### CLI-04 — Channel-grant output can overwrite a private seed or unrelated staging file

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed destructive path handling.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/cli/src/commands/channel.rs:369-375` checks only final-path existence, then loads the root. `396-399` truncates a deterministic `.tmp-channel-grant` sibling and renames it without a foreign-seed guard.
- **Trigger / consequence:** `channel issue-grant --root-identity ROOT --out ROOT --force` replaces the loaded private root with public grant JSON. Independently, an existing staging pathname—including a root seed placed there—is truncated even if final output is absent and force is omitted.
- **Repair direction:** Reuse exclusive staging and no-clobber/forced publication with the existing foreign-seed protection (`commands/org.rs:2337-2369`).

### CLI-05 — Capability announcement output can truncate its signing identity

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/cli/src/commands/cap.rs:378` loads the signing identity; `write_announcement_output` directly truncates an unchecked destination with `tokio::fs::write` (`537-541`).
- **Trigger:** `cap announce --key IDENTITY --out IDENTITY --tag nrpc:echo`, or an output path aliased to a seed file by symlink/hard link.
- **Consequence:** Successful announcement generation destroys the private signing seed without overwrite acknowledgement.
- **Repair direction:** Apply the established foreign-seed guard and publish through exclusive sibling staging rather than writing through an alias.

CLI provenance note: Focused prior-audit searches found no exact matches. Rejected candidates: joined-target inspection explicitly rejects attachment; temporary-supervisor reads are gated/disclosed; unused timeout configuration alone does not contradict the documented deadline policy.

### RPC-01 — Duplex response consumption never replenishes finite credits

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed integration gap.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh_rpc.rs:7471-7475` sends the response-window header. `cortex/rpc.rs:8507-8511,8548-8557` creates a semaphore and spends a permit for each response. `DuplexStream::poll_next` returns consumed chunks without issuing a response grant (`mesh_rpc.rs:3394-3408`); its wrapper supplies no grant bookkeeping either.
- **Trigger:** Open duplex with `stream_window_initial: Some(1)`, produce two response chunks, and consume the first.
- **Consequence:** The second chunk stalls even though the caller is consuming responses. The terminal can also stall behind the pump.
- **Repair direction:** Add response-window/grant bookkeeping to the duplex receive half, preserving it across split calls. Unbounded windows avoid the path but do not repair finite-window calls.

### RPC-02 — Remote terminals leave upload credit waiters blocked

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed.
- **Evidence:** Client-stream and duplex grant pumps exit when their grant receiver closes without closing the upload semaphore (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh_rpc.rs:6826-6833,7482-7489`). Upload waits acquire that semaphore (`2831-2839,3201-3208`). Terminal dispatch removes pending entries and drops grant senders (`cortex/rpc.rs:9569-9589,9618-9647`).
- **Trigger:** Use `request_window_initial: Some(1)`. Send the initial request; the server rejects or times out without consuming it. Attempt another send.
- **Consequence:** The terminal arrives, but the upload waiter can remain blocked indefinitely. For client-streaming, the outstanding mutable send borrow also prevents reaching `finish()` to observe that terminal.
- **Mitigation / repair:** Explicit cancel-token watchers close the semaphore; ordinary remote termination does not. Propagate terminal/deadline state to send waiters and reject further sends.

### RPC-03 — Failed publication permanently spends upload credit

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed pre-publication failure path.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh_rpc.rs:2831-2840` forgets the acquired permit before fallible admission signing and initial publish (`2856-2870`). Duplex repeats this ordering (`3201-3208,3225-3240`).
- **Trigger:** With upload window one, lazy admission signing fails or initial publication fails before delivery; retry `send` on the still-`JustOpened` handle.
- **Consequence:** The only credit is already consumed, so retry waits forever; the server never received a call from which to grant replacement credit. Cloning initial headers preserves state but not the spent permit.
- **Repair direction:** Keep the owned permit until publication commits, refunding on preparation/pre-publication failure. Cancellation during partial publication requires a separately defined commit contract.

### RPC-04 — Reopening a stream ID resets only the local sequence lifetime

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed asymmetric-close defect; **related known concern, not wholly new**.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:49740-49754` closes local stream state without a peer lifetime transition. A new stream starts `tx_seq` at zero (`wire/src/session.rs:2498-2511`), while an existing peer receive stream retains its frontier and rejects lower sequences (`wire/src/reliability.rs:1212-1216`). Already-signalled receive boundaries are not reset (`wire/src/session.rs:2638-2657`).
- **Trigger:** Deliver reliable data, close/reopen the same stream ID on one side of the same session, then send again while the other side retains its stream.
- **Consequence:** New packets can be classified as old duplicates and acknowledged without consumer delivery. Local epochs/quarantined grants do not synchronize the remote receive lifetime.
- **Repair direction:** Preserve sequence continuity or introduce a fenced wire-level lifetime transition.
- **Prior evidence:** `SDK_AUDIT_2026_10_11.md:105` already documents unsafe native close/reopen and a higher-level workaround that deliberately keeps the stream open. The audited core API still exposes the underlying behavior. The reopen test cited by the subagent (`tests/three_node_integration.rs:4102-4129`) checks successful send, not successor delivery.

### RPC-05 — First reliable boundary can exclude an earlier reliable send

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed concurrency race.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/wire/src/session.rs:970-982` holds a shared stream lookup while separately reserving sequences and claiming the reliable boundary. Reservation uses `fetch_add` (`3380-3383`); boundary promotion uses a distinct compare-exchange (`2736-2739`). The receiver concedes sequences below the stated boundary (`2659-2662`).
- **Trigger:** A reserves sequence 0 and pauses before promotion. Concurrent B reserves sequence 1, wins boundary promotion, and its packet arrives first.
- **Consequence:** The receiver concedes sequence 0; A's genuinely reliable packet is later dropped as a duplicate. Atomic sequence ranges and a once-only boundary claim do not make their combined transition atomic.
- **Repair direction:** Serialize allocation with first reliable promotion, or combine them into one authoritative admission transition.

### RPC-06 — Reliable descriptor headroom is checked but not reserved

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed concurrent-admission gap.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:50086-50109` checks available retransmit descriptors without reserving them. Admission reserves byte credit/sequences only (`wire/src/session.rs:973-982`); descriptors register after delivery (`mesh.rs:50412-50427`). Full-window insertion evicts an unacknowledged descriptor (`wire/src/reliability.rs:1143-1159`).
- **Trigger:** On a supported reliable stream with `window_bytes = 0` and 32 descriptor slots, concurrently admit two calls of 17 packet-sized events before ACKs free capacity. Each independently sees enough initial headroom.
- **Consequence:** Together they exceed capacity, evicting an unacknowledged packet's retransmit state. If that packet was lost, reliable recovery cannot recover it.
- **Repair direction:** Atomically reserve descriptor capacity at admission and release unused reservations on failure. Whole-call checks protect serialized callers, not concurrent ones.

### RPC-07 — Public duplex pump draining outlives deadlines and cancellation

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed; independent of RPC-01.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/cortex/rpc.rs:8627-8648` applies the deadline to handler execution only, then awaits the response pump without a deadline (`8649-8653`). That pump can block on a response semaphore (`8548-8557`). Public cancellation removes its map entry but does not close the pump's cloned semaphore (`8794-8800`).
- **Trigger:** With a response window of one, queue two responses, let the handler return, and withhold further credit.
- **Consequence:** The pump and supervising task can remain alive past the deadline without terminal emission or cleanup. CANCEL also need not unblock the credit wait.
- **Repair direction:** Supervise handler and pump under one deadline/cancellation lifetime; close/abort and join the pump before terminal emission. Protected calls already use a bounded supervisor; this finding concerns the public branch.

### RPC-08 — Completed public duplex calls leak flow-control entries

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/cortex/rpc.rs:8507-8511` inserts a per-call response semaphore. Completion removes `in_flight` and request senders but not `flow_control` (`8727-8729`). The public CANCEL branch removes it (`8800`), but consuming the server terminal marks a clean close (`mesh_rpc.rs:3409-3425`) and suppresses cancel-on-drop.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Repeatedly complete public duplex calls with finite positive windows and response counts within the granted window. Each completed call retains its key/semaphore until the serving fold itself is dropped.
- **Repair direction:** Remove the exact call's flow-control entry on every public completion path.

RPC provenance note: Focused searches found the prior stream-close warning recorded under RPC-04; other reviewed matches concerned different grant/streaming paths. Rejected candidates: authenticated target/session routing guards are present for responses/grants; local handle epochs fence stale operations; current sends inherit promoted reliability; malformed/duplicate manifests are rejected. No unsupported bypass is promoted as a finding.

## Second audit pass

Second pass complete: four fresh subagents audited gang reservations/scheduler lifecycle, MeshDB query/federation correctness, MCP invocation/consent/session boundaries, and native Node/Python/Go binding lifetimes; the parent audited state snapshots and causal/horizon bookkeeping. Added 45 numbered concerns, bringing the report to 76 entries. All returned concerns are retained, including explicitly qualified contract questions, known/residual issues, and unconfirmed hypotheses—not 76 claimed distinct confirmed bugs. Source locations, triggers, consequences, repair directions, uncertainty, and historical relationships accompany the entries. Verification remains source tracing only; no runtime checks or production edits.

### STATE-01 — Snapshot high-water check races retention removal

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed interleaving, not runtime-reproduced.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/state/snapshot.rs:555-559` checks `high_water` before separately acquiring the live snapshot entry (`574-583`). `remove` removes only that live entry (`628-630`). A vacant-entry store overwrites the high-water value with its own sequence rather than rechecking it under the lifecycle lock.
- **Trigger:** Stale store A for sequence 5 passes the initial high-water check and pauses. Store B commits sequence 10; retention removes B's live snapshot. A resumes into a vacant live entry and commits sequence 5, also lowering high-water to 5.
- **Consequence:** Both the latest snapshot and its supposed anti-rewind watermark move backwards despite the documented retention protection.
- **Repair direction:** Serialize freshness validation, live-entry replacement/removal, and watermark publication through one per-entity owner. Rechecking only before the live-entry lock is insufficient.
- **Prior related findings:** `BUG_AUDIT_2026_05_02.md` item 8 identified retention rewind before the high-water fix; `REVIEW_BUGFIXES_8_2026_05_02.md` item 5 discusses a benign equal-sequence race, not this store/remove interleaving.

### STATE-02 — “Exact” cone concurrency ignores event sequences

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed public API result defect; no production caller established in the inspected source.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/continuity/cone.rs:114-120` declares two exact cones concurrent only when neither full horizon contains the other's **origin**, ignoring both stored sequence numbers. There is also no same-origin ordered-chain check. The neighboring `could_have_influenced` correctly uses sequence-aware `has_observed` and handles same-origin ordering (`66-82`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** A's event 10 has observed B through sequence 2; B's event 10 has not observed A. They are concurrent, but origin membership returns false for concurrency. Conversely, two events on the same origin with empty horizons are reported concurrent despite their chain order.
- **Repair direction:** Handle same-origin events explicitly and compare the other cone's actual sequence against each exact horizon. Document the remaining approximation when a full horizon is missing.
- **Prior related finding:** `CODE_REVIEW_2026_05_01_BUGFIXES_7.md` CR-16 records the earlier bloom-saturation fix using `contains_origin`; it did not address sequence-sensitive concurrency.

### STATE-03 — Host snapshots omit the chain-head payload needed by their own restore path

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed chain-continuity defect for stateful daemons whose snapshot differs from their last emitted payload.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/compute/host.rs:262-274` creates a snapshot without filling `head_payload`. Restore falls back to serialized daemon state as the previous output payload (`159-177,303-319`), although the chain builder hashes the real previous event payload (`state/causal.rs:189-197`). Registry snapshot extraction forwards the result unchanged (`compute/registry.rs:311-317`).
- **Trigger:** A stateful daemon emits payload P at a non-genesis head, but its `snapshot()` returns different state bytes S. Snapshot and restore it, then emit another event. The same issue reaches local standby synchronization without any wire transport.
- **Consequence:** The restored producer computes the next `parent_hash` against S instead of P. Observers retaining the original head reject the successor as a fork even though the daemon's application state restored successfully.
- **Repair direction:** Populate host-generated snapshots from the chain builder's actual head payload, and preserve that context across migration rather than substituting application state.
- **Prior related finding:** `BUG_AUDIT_2026_04_30.md` item 25 identifies missing snapshot head context. Adding an optional runtime field did not wire the current host producer to populate it.

### STATE-04 — Cross-node snapshots erase previously observed causal dependencies

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed migration information loss; wire omission is explicitly documented, but no reconstruction path was found.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/state/snapshot.rs:269-272,329-347` omits the full horizon; decoding installs an empty one (`460-466`). `compute/host.rs:201,320` adopts that empty horizon. Migration sends only serialized snapshot chunks (`subprotocol/migration_handler.rs:519-540`; `compute/orchestrator.rs:111-122`) and passes the decoded snapshot directly to restore (`migration_handler.rs:1064-1073,1138-1149`).
- **Trigger:** A daemon observes source A, migrates, then processes an event from source B without observing A again.
- **Consequence:** New outputs' encoded horizons contain B but omit A, despite retained application state depending on A. Causal queries can incorrectly return “definitely not influenced,” violating the advertised no-false-negative direction of encoded observed horizons.
- **Repair direction:** Carry the full horizon in the snapshot/migration protocol, or reconstruct it explicitly before allowing restored output. Preserving only the previous link's compressed horizon does not restore the full per-origin sequence map.

### STATE-05 — Pruning beyond the head advances the snapshot marker beyond actual history

- **Severity / status:** Low/medium contract concern; source-confirmed marker inconsistency, no current production consumer of `snapshot_seq()` established.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/state/log.rs:199-214` gates marker advancement on removing at least one event, but still assigns the requested prune sequence rather than the actual last-pruned sequence. The base link advances only to the last real event (`236-240`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** A log contains events 1–3; call `prune_through(100)`. The resulting head is 3 and next valid append is 4, but `snapshot_seq()` reports 100. Consumers treating that marker as covered history can skip events 4–100.
- **Repair direction:** Advance the marker to the actual last-pruned link, not an unvalidated requested upper bound.
- **Prior related finding:** `BUG_AUDIT_2026_04_30_CORE.md` item 129 fixed marker advancement when nothing was pruned; the nonempty, upper-bound-beyond-head variant remains.

Parent-pass qualification: `ObservedHorizon::observe(origin, 0)` does not increment logical time for a newly inserted zero-sequence origin, but the origin itself is retained correctly and no consequential production logical-clock use was established; this is not promoted as an additional defect. Snapshot framing/version checks and sequence/origin consistency checks are present.

### BIND-01 — Async Python RPC results do not retain their source reactor

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed ownership gap; exact failure timing not runtime-reproduced.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/bindings/python/src/mesh_rpc.rs:3065-3074` retains the parent `Arc<GuardedRuntime>` on `AsyncMeshRpc`, but unary futures capture only the node (`3293-3304`) and returned streaming/client-stream/duplex objects omit runtime ownership (`3366-3374,3405-3413,3450-3456,3513-3528`). Split descendants likewise omit it (`3980-3988,4067-4074`). The Python async bridge runs futures on a separate process-global runtime (`bindings/python/src/async_bridge.rs:31-41`), not the source mesh reactor.
- **Trigger:** Return an opened async RPC stream from a helper, release the local mesh/RPC factory objects, and continue using the stream; or release them after constructing an awaitable but before awaiting it.
- **Consequence:** Last-owner destruction can shut down the mesh's source socket reactor/background tasks while live RPC work retains only `Arc<MeshNode>`.
- **Repair direction:** Retain the source `Arc<GuardedRuntime>` in independently owned futures and resulting handles, including org constructors and split halves.
- **Prior related finding:** `SDK_AUDIT_2026_10_11.md:809-823` covers the analogous `AsyncNetMesh` ownership defect; the current async RPC descendants are a separate remaining surface.

### BIND-02 — Async Python close does not wake already-pending RPC operations

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed cancellation gap; duration depends on response/deadline.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/bindings/python/src/mesh_rpc.rs:3565-3573` holds the receiver mutex across `stream.next().await`. `close` only sets a flag and attempts `try_lock` (`3617-3622`); `aclose` immediately resolves (`3627-3629`). It does not fire the stored cancel token, and the pending pull neither selects on close nor rechecks the flag before returning data. Client-stream and duplex close/operation paths repeat the pattern (`3694-3702,3721-3737,3754-3759,3961-3966,4052-4057,4117-4122`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Close an idle stream from another asyncio task while a pull is pending. Close reports completion but the pull remains parked and CANCEL is not emitted; a later chunk can still be returned after closure. Credit-blocked sends have the same wakeup gap.
- **Repair direction:** Signal independent cancellation/closure without taking the operation mutex, and have parked operations observe it and release the inner handle.
- **Prior related finding:** The October audit's sync-close defect (`SDK_AUDIT_2026_10_11.md:782-795`) has a current sync wakeup fix; its assumption that async siblings were fully fixed does not cover pending-operation wakeup.

### BIND-03 — Explicit async Python stream grants lack Tokio runtime entry

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed panic path on a normal Python asyncio thread.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/bindings/python/src/mesh_rpc.rs:3594-3599` synchronously invokes the core grant without entering a runtime. Core grant reaches `spawn_grant_publish`, which directly calls `tokio::spawn` (`src/adapter/net/mesh_rpc.rs:2456-2469,2512-2521`). The sync binding explicitly enters its runtime for this operation (`bindings/python/src/mesh_rpc.rs:1188-1196`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Call `grant(1)` on an idle, flow-controlled `AsyncRpcStream` from the ordinary asyncio thread. Instead of publishing credit, it panics because no Tokio runtime is entered. Whether this surfaces as a Python panic exception or abort depends on the build's panic strategy.
- **Repair direction:** Publish via an explicitly selected runtime and retain the grant handle independently of the receiver mutex.
- **Prior related finding:** `SDK_NRPC_CONSISTENCY_AUDIT_2026_08_08.md:15-54` covers runtime entry for registration, not this grant operation.

### BIND-04 — Finite but oversized cache TTLs panic across native APIs

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed local-input availability defect; no remote path established.
- **Evidence:** Sign/finiteness checks accept `1e30` seconds, then call panicking `Duration::from_secs_f64`: `net/crates/net/bindings/go/meshdb-ffi/src/lib.rs:1398-1404`, `bindings/node/src/meshdb.rs:325-334`, and `bindings/python/src/meshdb.rs:382-391`. The C ABI conversion occurs before its `catch_unwind` (`meshdb-ffi/src/lib.rs:1412`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Supply a finite nonnegative TTL beyond `Duration`'s representable range. Rather than an argument error/default, native code panics; escaping the non-unwinding C ABI aborts the process. Abort-profile builds cannot recover at unwind catchers either.
- **Repair direction:** Use `Duration::try_from_secs_f64` and map failure to each binding's argument policy; include conversion inside C panic containment as defense in depth.
- **Prior related finding:** `CODE_REVIEW_2026_05_13_MESHDB.md` covers negative/nonfinite validation and execution containment, not finite out-of-range conversion.

### BIND-05 — Async Python grants are discarded while a pull owns the mutex

- **Severity / status:** Potential medium; behavior source-confirmed, **intent ambiguous** because skipping is explicitly documented.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/bindings/python/src/mesh_rpc.rs:3589-3599` silently skips a grant when `try_lock` fails; pending pulls hold the same lock throughout their data wait (`3565-3573`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** A flow-controlled server needs explicit credit while a pull is already waiting. Another task calls grant, but it is discarded; the server can remain credit-blocked while the client waits for data.
- **Repair direction:** Retain an independent `RpcStreamGrant`, as the sync implementation does, or explicitly reject/report grants that cannot be applied. This is separate from BIND-03's runtime-entry panic when the lock is available.

### BIND-06 — Combined async Python duplex handle serializes sending behind receive

- **Severity / status:** Potential medium; source-confirmed serialization, combined-handle concurrency contract unclear.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/bindings/python/src/mesh_rpc.rs:3828-3841,3883-3892` holds one Tokio mutex across send and receive awaits. Public `into_split` provides independently owned halves (`3913-3945`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Begin receiving before sending the request needed for a response. Receive holds the mutex and prevents a concurrent sender from supplying that request.
- **Repair direction:** Split internally into independent operation locks, or explicitly require `into_split()` before concurrent bidirectional use.

Native-binding provenance note: Material rejected candidates include supported Go MeshDB Free/use races (caller locks exist), MeshDB payload-length/free mismatches (checked lengths and owned slices), Node compute callback buffer lifetime (copied into owned bytes), and Node method receiver loss through supported SDK registration (bound-method adapter exists). Native snapshot-error-to-None behavior is explicitly documented and was not promoted without a contradictory contract.

### MCP-01 — Cross-provider failover ignores at-most-once invocation safety

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed opt-in path.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/adapters/mcp/src/serve/mesh_gateway.rs:624-628` uses `InvokeSafety` for same-provider retries, but any transport error triggers equivalent-provider failover (`644-654`). Paid invocation selects `AtMostOnce` (`serve/gated.rs:199-204`); equivalence fingerprints omit pricing (`serve/grouping.rs:103-122`), allowing a matching free alternative. Free handlers do not redeem payment headers (`wrap/invoke.rs:369-416`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Enable trusted-equivalent providers with matching paid/free tools. The paid primary executes but its response times out; the free alternative executes the same arguments again despite at-most-once safety. Default configurations without equivalence enabled avoid this path.
- **Repair direction:** Do not fail over ambiguous at-most-once outcomes; include pricing compatibility in equivalence eligibility.
- **Prior related finding:** July payments SDK review M6 fixed selection of `AtMostOnce`; this is a remaining consumer that ignores it during failover.

### MCP-02 — Cancelled publication leaves ownerless advertised contributions

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed cancellation gap.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/adapters/mcp/src/wrap/session.rs:549-562,701-713` inserts shared contributions before awaiting mesh synchronization; the cleanup-owning registration is constructed only on success (`624-628,772-776`). Explicit error rollback does not execute when the future is dropped. Refresh similarly swaps before awaiting (`896-908,1074-1083`).
- **Trigger:** Timeout/drop publication during announcement. The CLI actually applies an optional startup timeout around `publish_server` (`cli/src/commands/wrap.rs:353-362`).
- **Consequence:** A contribution remains without its publication owner/handlers. Later sibling synchronization can announce that orphaned tool set again.
- **Repair direction:** Arm a cleanup owner before inserting/swapping shared state, and make announcement/catalog reconciliation cancellation-safe.
- **Prior related finding:** July SDK refresh rollback finding F5 covered returned errors, not cancellation.

### MCP-03 — Withdrawing wrapped tools need not terminate a hung child

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed lifetime gap for nonresponding calls.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/adapters/mcp/src/wrap/session.rs:987-998` relies on dropping the publication's client to stop the child. Served handlers retain client Arcs (`580-584`), and outstanding handler tasks survive service withdrawal. The actual `StdioMcpClient` owns the kill-on-drop child (`wrap/stdio.rs:33-42`); an Arc release is not necessarily its destruction.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Invoke a tool that never responds, then withdraw/drop its publication while the mesh stays alive. Withdrawal can succeed while the child, pending request, handler, and configured credentials remain resident. External `client()` clones also extend this lifetime.
- **Repair direction:** Explicitly shut down the subprocess/transport independently of reference count, fail pending requests, and complete reader teardown.

### MCP-04 — Cancelling a partial stdio write corrupts framing for later requests

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed cancellation hazard; not runtime-reproduced.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/adapters/mcp/src/wrap/stdio.rs:60-67` writes the JSON body and newline through cancellable `write_all` calls within the request future. The mutex serializes writers but is released on future drop without completing the frame.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Send arguments larger than pipe capacity while the child is not reading, cancel after a partial write, then issue another request. The next JSON message is appended to an unterminated fragment; the child sees a malformed combined line and requests can remain unanswered.
- **Repair direction:** Own full-frame writes in an independent writer task, or close the transport permanently when an interrupted frame cannot safely be completed.

### MCP-05 — Abandoned stdio requests retain pending entries

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed resource leak.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/adapters/mcp/src/wrap/stdio.rs:204-217` inserts a pending sender without drop/cancellation cleanup. Returned write errors remove it, but future cancellation bypasses that branch; only matching responses or connection shutdown subsequently reclaim it.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Repeatedly timeout/abort calls to a live child that never answers those requests. Pending entries accumulate without a bound, including calls cancelled before acquiring stdin and never transmitted.
- **Repair direction:** Give each pending registration cancellation-safe ownership that removes it on every exit. Remote MCP cancellation is a separate concern.
- **Prior related finding:** July bridge “leak/hang-safe” assessment covered I/O errors and EOF, not abandoned futures.

### MCP-06 — Integer schema validation rejects integral JSON number representations

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed valid-input rejection.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/adapters/mcp/src/serve/validation.rs:168-171` accepts integers only when serde stores them as signed/unsigned integers. JSON Schema's integer type also includes finite numbers without a fractional part, such as `1.0` and `1e3`.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Invoke an integer-schema tool with `{"count":1.0}`. The shared gate rejects it as `ValidationFailed` before invocation despite schema-valid arguments.
- **Repair direction:** Recognize integral finite floating representations without losing integer-range handling. Existing literal/fractional tests do not cover this representation difference.

### MCP-07 — Requests accepted after stdout closure can never complete

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed terminal-state race.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/adapters/mcp/src/wrap/stdio.rs:268-276` clears pending entries once and records closure when the reader exits. `request_raw` (`204-217`) neither checks nor synchronizes registration with this terminal state.
- **Trigger / consequence:** The child closes stdout but retains stdin, or stdout reading fails while the child stays alive. A new/racing request successfully writes after final pending cleanup, then waits forever because the only reader has exited.
- **Repair direction:** Serialize closed-state transition with pending registration and reject every post-close request. Ordinary process exit often yields a write error, but does not cover this half-closed case.

### MCP-08 — Forwarding secret copies are not scrubbed on all error exits

- **Severity / status:** Low; source-confirmed memory-hygiene concern, not demonstrated credential exfiltration.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/adapters/mcp/src/forward/keychain.rs:70-80` copies plaintext but `entry(...)?` can return before explicit zeroization. `forward/header.rs:249-252` also drops owned rejected bytes before constructing their zeroizing wrapper.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Keychain entry initialization fails, or owned secret bytes fail header validation. Plaintext copies are released unsanitized contrary to the stated all-outcomes cleanup intent.
- **Repair direction:** Acquire zeroizing ownership when allocating/accepting owned secret buffers, before any fallible operation.
- **Prior related finding:** July forwarding F5 claimed all-outcomes scrubbing; these early exits remain.

### MCP-09 — In-memory secret backend accepts values it cannot retrieve

- **Severity / status:** Low; known residual, not new.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/adapters/mcp/src/forward/secret.rs:104-107` stores bytes without validation; retrieval validates the copied value (`140`) and can reject it.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Store an oversized/control-character secret. Setup succeeds and `contains` is true, but subsequent resolution fails instead of returning the configured value.
- **Repair direction:** Validate consistently at entry or explicitly document a weaker test-backend contract.
- **Prior related finding:** July forwarding F2 explicitly named this backend; the recorded resolution documents keychain validation only. Practical impact is lower for this ephemeral/test-oriented backend.

### MCP-10 — Truncated pinned-name hash can collide and remap dispatch

- **Severity / status:** Unconfirmed medium concern; **no concrete colliding capability pair established**.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/adapters/mcp/src/serve/shim.rs:640-649` retains 24 hash bits after a lossy/truncated base; dispatch picks the first matching name (`358`). The comment at `624-627` acknowledges name collisions but claims they cannot cause remapping.
- **Conditional consequence:** If distinct IDs share both base and suffix, one tool is unreachable under that name, and changing the approved set can change which capability the cached name invokes.
- **Repair direction:** Detect collisions in the approved set and refuse ambiguous names, or use a sufficiently collision-resistant reversible naming scheme. A reproducible pair is needed before promoting this to a confirmed defect.
- **Prior related finding:** July bridge F9 fixed ordinary pin-set-dependent names; this is its qualified hash-collision residue.

### MCP-11 — Credential absence is treated as proof of duplicate-safe invocation

- **Severity / status:** Potential medium; **intent ambiguous / existing chosen policy**.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/adapters/mcp/src/serve/backend.rs:143-154` chooses duplicate-safe retry solely for wire credential status `"none"`. A credential-free local filesystem append tool can still be stateful; descriptor lowering explicitly marks tools non-stateless (`wrap/descriptor.rs:307`).
- **Conditional consequence:** An executed append whose response times out can be repeated. Absence of credentials does not establish idempotence.
- **Decision / repair:** Decide whether providers must independently declare idempotence, rather than deriving it from credentials. Historical bridge F1 deliberately chose the present policy, so this is not presented as a novel accidental regression.

MCP provenance note: No live shared-stdio credential-injection consumer was found; forwarding rejects stdio targets. Consent still gates wire `"none"` tools; pin requests do not self-approve; ordinary ID-local promoted names retain their prior fix. Documented pagination/cancellation-forwarding limitations and intentionally owner-scoped delegated describe are not promoted as new defects.

### MDB-01 — Concurrent federated join inputs reuse one wire call ID

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed production transport mismatch.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/meshdb/federated.rs:296-300` derives each atomic request ID from the query handle. Both join children execute concurrently with clones of that same handle (`387-402`). `meshdb/transport.rs:407-418` correctly rejects an already-inflight ID.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Execute a remote-on-remote join while the first input request remains pending. The second input fails duplicate registration even when both sources are available.
- **Repair direction:** Preserve shared query cancellation identity but allocate distinct wire IDs per atomic remote request. Loopback join coverage does not exercise wire demultiplexing.
- **Prior related findings:** May 13 M3 / June 18 finding 26 address allocation across executors and duplicate rejection, not recursive join reuse.

### MDB-02 — Row-count batching exceeds the actual packet-size limit

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed wire-size mismatch.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/meshdb/transport.rs:838-853` batches at a row-count threshold, then sends each encoded batch as one subprotocol event (`940-948`). `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:45614-45640` builds one nonfragmented packet. `wire/src/protocol.rs:32-55,577-581` limits packets to 8192 bytes, with a smaller event ceiling.
- **Trigger:** Return 64 rows containing 128-byte payloads; payload alone fills 8192 bytes before encoding/framing overhead.
- **Consequence:** The full batch cannot pass the receive boundary. A later small final batch can produce a successful incomplete query; an actual send error returns from the server loop without a terminal error, potentially leaving the caller pending.
- **Repair direction:** Bound batches by encoded bytes against the true event ceiling; fragment through a supported path or reject oversized rows explicitly, and terminate calls on failed response sends.

### MDB-03 — Response inbox overflow can silently truncate successful results

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed wrong-result path.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/meshdb/transport.rs:339-348` drops frames when the bounded response inbox fills. Mesh integration only logs the routing error (`mesh.rs:32025-32029`). Translation accepts a later final batch as successful completion (`meshdb/federated.rs:1089-1101`); response framing has no batch ordinal/expected total (`meshdb/protocol.rs:95-102`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Slow consumption fills downstream rows and the 64-frame response inbox. An intermediate batch is dropped, then a later final batch is admitted. Count/sum/join results can be wrong and incomplete results can enter the cache.
- **Repair direction:** Latch overflow as terminal call failure through a path that does not require room in the full inbox. Missing-terminal detection cannot detect a missing middle batch.

### MDB-04 — Remaining outer-join implementations drop preserved missing-key rows

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed residual across local/federated variants.
- **Evidence:** Local full-outer uses a hash-table builder that filters missing keys (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/meshdb/executor.rs:541,1018-1021`). Federated hash/full-outer use the same dropping builder (`federated.rs:820-821,861-862`); federated sort-merge filters missing keys on both sides (`898-905`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Join on field `k` with preserved-side payload `{"other":1}`. Local FullOuter loses missing-key right rows; federated LeftOuter/RightOuter lose preserved build rows; FullOuter loses missing-key right rows; federated sort-merge loses preserved missing-key rows on either side.
- **Repair direction:** Retain nonmatching rows separately and emit them on preserved outer sides, sharing the corrected semantics across implementations.
- **Prior related findings:** June 18 findings 20/32 and current local one-sided/sort-merge regression coverage establish the intended behavior; these remaining mirrors were not covered.

### MDB-05 — Explicit JSON nulls become matching join keys

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed behavior contradicts the join's stated null contract.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/meshdb/executor.rs:1084` and the federated extractor use the distinct-projection helper, which serializes null as key bytes `"null"` (`meshdb/row.rs:177-201`). The join documentation says a NULL key matches nothing (`executor.rs:1106-1109`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Join inputs both contain `{"k":null}`. Null rows match one another, potentially creating a Cartesian product instead of remaining unmatched.
- **Repair direction:** Exclude null in join-specific extraction, without globally changing the intentionally different distinct-count projection behavior.
- **Qualification:** No explicit-null join test establishes an alternative intended contract; missing-field rejection alone does not cover explicit null.

### MDB-06 — Payload fields can shadow intrinsic filter fields inconsistently

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed wrong-filter results.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/meshdb/row.rs:57-64` inserts intrinsic origin/sequence before flattening payload into the same namespace. `77-83` appends duplicate tags but overwrites metadata. Tag predicates can match any duplicate while metadata predicates see only the payload replacement.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Envelope sequence 42 with payload `{"seq":1}` can satisfy a tag filter for `seq == 1` and fail a metadata filter for `seq == 42`. The two supposedly mirrored predicate views disagree and differ from intrinsic-first numeric extraction.
- **Repair direction:** Reserve intrinsic names consistently or namespace payload fields unambiguously; test collisions on both predicate surfaces.

### MDB-07 — Cache hits ignore the requesting call's freshness policy

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed policy-transition gap.
- **Evidence:** Cache-hit paths return entries without considering `options.cache_policy` (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/meshdb/executor.rs:269-274`; `federated.rs:183-190`). The key contains only plan hash/version (`cache.rs:94-100`); expiry checks only the policy stored when the entry was inserted (`166-170,293-304`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Populate a plan under a long TTL, then request that plan with a shorter or zero TTL while the old policy still permits the entry. The caller receives data older than its requested bound.
- **Repair direction:** Apply request freshness to cached age as well as stored expiration, or isolate incompatible policies. Explicit local bypass avoids the issue but should not be necessary to honor a requested TTL.

### MDB-08 — Evicted cache values retain row allocations outside byte accounting

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed retained-memory defect.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/meshdb/cache.rs:393-399` removes lookup/accounting and frees the slot index but leaves `nodes[idx].value.rows` allocated. Storage is dropped only when that slot is reused (`369-372`) or the entire cache is cleared.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Evict a large result under byte pressure or lazy TTL expiration. The cache can report zero entries/bytes while retaining payload allocations; two near-budget inserted results can remain resident though accounting reports only the survivor.
- **Repair direction:** Drop/empty the removed value while keeping reusable node metadata.
- **Prior related finding:** May 13 pass-2 NEW-M4 concerned oversized insertion, not release of removed storage.

### MDB-09 — Federated cache bypass is not propagated to remote execution

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed end-to-end options gap when remote executors cache.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/meshdb/federated.rs:174-178` applies bypass only to local federation cache. Outbound Execute contains only ID/plan (`296-300`); the actual server calls `executor.execute(plan)` with default options (`meshdb/transport.rs:800`). Loopback restores defaults too (`federated.rs:1198-1200`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** A cache-enabled remote server already holds an older result; execute a federated authoritative read with `bypass_cache: true`. It can still return the remote cache and can populate that cache on a miss.
- **Repair direction:** Carry cache execution options over the wire, or explicitly disable remote caching for federated leaf reads. Cache-less servers avoid the path.

### MDB-10 — Exact aggregates lack their promised working-memory budget

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed resource-boundary gap.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/meshdb/query.rs:346-348,362-365` promises bounded exact aggregates and a budget error. Reduction bags grow without accounting (`executor.rs:763-766`), as do exact distinct sets (`838-841`). Local execution first materializes its full range without an equivalent input cap (`361-370`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Valid large ranges with many distinct values or percentile samples continue allocating instead of returning `QueryBudgetExceeded`, risking process exhaustion.
- **Repair direction:** Account and enforce aggregate working memory before growth and bound local materialization.
- **Prior related finding:** May 18 MD-1 led to a present federated raw-input cap of 256 MiB; that does not implement the remaining local/per-group aggregate budget.

MeshDB provenance note: Projection/order/limit and pagination/resume are explicitly rejected/deferred, not silently ignored. Single-claim coverage selection and ordinary TTL/Permanent staleness are documented policies. Percentile's `floor(p × (n−1))` is test-pinned, so nearest-rank terminology remains an intent question rather than an additional confirmed defect. Historical adjacent-window overflow allegations were not adopted without a current normal-input failure.

### GANG-01 — Active exclusive ownership expires independently of the running job

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed default-pipeline lease gap.
- **Evidence:** Reservation signing always uses default envelope metadata (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/gang/claim.rs:132-150`). `ReservationFold::DEFAULT_TTL` is 30 seconds, distinct from reservation deadlines (`behavior/fold/reservation.rs:188-193`); fold expiry deletes the whole entry without checking Active state (`fold/mod.rs:996-1010`; `fold/expiry.rs:130-155`). The live mesh installs a swept reservation fold (`mesh.rs:14367-14369`); `GangClaimPipeline` has no running-claim renewal path (`cortex/workflow/step.rs:230-323`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** A 60-second reservation expires around 30 seconds; a running Active job also loses exclusion after that TTL while its daemon/workflow/claim handle remain live. An absent entry can admit another claimant.
- **Repair direction:** Tie retention/renewal to the actual lease lifecycle, revoking/stopping old execution before granting a successor.
- **Qualification:** External callers can manually heartbeat, but the supplied pipeline does not.

### GANG-02 — Scheduler lifecycle mapping does not match registered daemon identities

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed integration mismatch.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/scheduler_bridge/lifecycle.rs:51-52,73` looks up a complete synthesized daemon ref. Its ID is a task-domain splitmix value (`scheduler_bridge/daemon_ref.rs:43-44,61-65`), while real daemon identity is the signing key's origin hash (`compute/host.rs:379-382`; `compute/registry.rs:91-104`; `behavior/meshos/sdk.rs:754-765`). Driver translation preserves the received ID (`scheduler_bridge/driver.rs:57-74`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Register a normal worker named `task/1` through the shipped registry/SDK and install the bridge observer. Matching names do not fix the ID mismatch; real lifecycle events do not resolve to the task and confirmation/failure handling is skipped.
- **Repair direction:** Maintain an explicit mapping from task/logical ref to registered daemon identity and normalize both desired execution and lifecycle events through it.
- **Qualification / provenance:** A custom adapter could translate IDs, but none was found in the shipped bridge. Driver tests fabricate `daemon_ref` events. June review N1 discussed identity collision wording, not this mapping gap.

### GANG-03 — Crash handling removes exclusive placement before failure is applied

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed asynchronous authority window.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/scheduler_bridge/driver.rs:120-122` discards the result of `workflow.fail` and immediately removes the claim. `tick` projects materialized state (`235-241`); still-Running tasks receive baseline `Run/node=None` (`projection.rs:38-48`) without the removed claim's pin (`runtime.rs:58-68`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Tick runs before the appended failure folds, or the failure append errors. It publishes unrestricted Run for an exclusive-resource task. MeshOS can drop the old pin and restart unpinned (`meshos/state.rs:547-560`; `meshos/reconcile.rs:123-147`).
- **Repair direction:** Keep exclusive placement authority until terminal application is confirmed and handle failure-write errors.
- **Prior related finding:** June bridge M1 fixed Run/Stop overlay clobbering, not premature pin removal.

### GANG-04 — Workflow publication failure strands an already acquired claim

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed error-path ownership loss.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/cortex/workflow/step.rs:127-130` acquires an active claim, then propagates a fallible workflow start without releasing it. `StepError::Workflow` carries no claim (`103-110`), so the caller cannot recover ownership from the error.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Workflow append fails after successful activation. No Running gate is returned, but the island remains held until independent cleanup/expiry.
- **Repair direction:** Compensate acquisition before returning the workflow error, preserving any cleanup failure.
- **Prior related finding:** Historical orphan-reservation #14 covers failed quorum/lost reservation, not successful activation followed by workflow-write failure.

### GANG-05 — Successful event append is mistaken for authorized task start

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed workflow/claim handoff mismatch.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/cortex/workflow/step.rs:128-130` returns `StepGate::Running` on append success. The workflow fold deliberately ignores transitions for unknown or terminal tasks (`workflow/fold.rs:71-83`), so append success does not prove a Running transition occurred.
- **Trigger / consequence:** A task is deleted/settles while resource acquisition is underway, or the helper is called for an already-settled task. Acquisition succeeds but start is a no-op; the helper nevertheless authorizes execution while workflow/bridge state remains absent or terminal.
- **Repair direction:** Make claim-to-workflow authorization conditional on current task/lifecycle state and compensate when the handoff does not apply.
- **Prior related finding:** The historical terminal guard is present; this finding concerns its consumer ignoring no-op semantics, not terminal resurrection inside the fold.

### GANG-06 — Delayed release of a copied claim can free its successor

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed same-owner acquisition-fencing gap.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/cortex/workflow/step.rs:35-38` makes an active handle Copy with only an island ID. Release signs a fresh generation (`308-320`), checking current node ownership but not acquisition/job identity (`behavior/gang/claim.rs:204-209`). Same-holder Active→Free is valid (`fold/reservation.rs:341`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Acquire A, release A, acquire B on the same island through the same pipeline, then run delayed/duplicate cleanup for copied handle A. Its newly generated release frees B's ownership while B's consumer still runs.
- **Repair direction:** Carry acquisition/job/epoch identity in the handle and atomically compare it during release.
- **Prior related finding:** Historical #5 fixed absent/foreign-holder release, not same-node successor fencing.

### GANG-07 — Rejected nonholder proposals can poison the legitimate owner's fence

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed **in-process commit API** behavior, not a deployed-RPC exploit claim.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/gang/active.rs:114-134` advances replica fences before the reservation fold validates proposer authority. Voting calls `FenceLedger::accept_active` (`active.rs:69-72`; `quorum.rs:180-185`), retaining the higher epoch even if the later reservation update is rejected.
- **Trigger / consequence:** A holds Reserved at generation 1. Nonholder B proposes epoch 1,000,000, gains fence acknowledgements, then gets LostReservation. A's legitimate epoch 2 receives NoQuorum despite retaining the reservation; ordinary incremental epochs cannot promptly recover.
- **Repair direction:** Validate reservation authority as part of replica acceptance and define a higher-term recovery path for the rightful owner.
- **Qualification / provenance:** Live acknowledgement RPC remains deferred. Historical #4 discussed epoch/cohort integration; this rejection-side fence mutation is distinct.

### GANG-08 — Observed Started events can authorize parked tasks without a claim

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed after lifecycle identity mapping is supplied.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/scheduler_bridge/lifecycle.rs:73-76` converts every known task's Started signal to ConfirmRunning without checking task/claim state. The observer appends start (`driver.rs:117-118`); nonterminal Waiting/Blocked states accept it (`cortex/workflow/fold.rs:71-80`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** A late/replayed Started arrives after a task is parked for unsuccessful acquisition. It becomes Running and baseline projection requests execution without an active claim.
