- **Repair direction:** Confirm only an already-authorized execution lifetime; require current task and claim authority rather than treating observation as an independent start command.
- **Qualification:** GANG-02 currently masks ordinary registry delivery. Terminal resurrection itself is guarded and is not re-reported.

### GANG-09 — Missing topology removes an exclusive claim's placement restriction

- **Severity / status:** Potential high safety concern; **intent ambiguous / explicitly documented and tested**.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/scheduler_bridge/projection.rs:89-92` drops unresolved claim pins; `runtime.rs:58-68` retains the Running task's unrestricted baseline.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Island topology expires while an active claim remains. The desired state can change from pinned execution to run-anywhere without a claim-revocation/reacquisition transition.
- **Decision / repair:** Decide whether unresolved exclusive topology should stop/park execution rather than fail open. The current fallback test (`projection.rs:198-205`) prevents classifying it as unquestionably accidental.

### GANG-10 — Quorum witnesses do not arbitrate owners across independent AP views

- **Severity / status:** Known high-level safety/integration limitation; **not a newly reproduced deployed double-run**.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/gang/quorum.rs:160-185` votes only on island/epoch, not owner or proposal identity; equal epochs are accepted. Separate reservation folds can each admit a local owner and obtain Committed against a shared cohort at equal or increasing epochs. `ActiveClaim` carries no execution fence (`cortex/workflow/step.rs:35-38`).
- **Consequence / qualification:** The in-process model alone does not establish cross-partition exclusivity. Existing partition witnesses share one reservation fold whose write lock supplies exclusion. Live quorum integration remains an explicitly deferred Phase-D boundary, related to historical #4.
- **Repair direction:** Bind acceptance to one authorized owner/proposal per term and require execution fencing through the live protocol before asserting partition-safe exclusivity.

### GANG-11 — Registry process errors have no in-tree crash-event emitter

- **Severity / status:** Medium integration concern; source-supported missing emitter, terminal-error policy undecided.
- **Evidence:** The subagent's source-wide search found `DaemonLifecycleEvent::Crashed` constructors only in tests. `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/compute/host.rs:220-224` increments an error counter and returns; `compute/registry.rs:292-303` forwards the error without notifying the lifecycle observer.
- **Consequence:** Installing the bridge observer alone does not implement process-error→Failed handling; the crash-reaction code is not reached through that production path.
- **Decision / repair:** Define which process errors are terminal and emit the corresponding lifecycle transition, or document the external emitter requirement.

### GANG-12 — Later acquisition errors bypass multi-island rollback

- **Severity / status:** Low/medium rare error-path concern; **ordinary-load apply-error trigger not established**.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/gang/multi.rs:89-109` propagates a later `single_island_claim` error via `?` before the rollback branch, which runs only for a Lost result.
- **Conditional consequence:** If claiming a later island errors after earlier claims succeeded, those prior islands remain held despite all-or-none semantics.
- **Repair direction:** Route acquisition errors through the same best-effort full rollback, preserving the original failure.
- **Qualification / provenance:** Historical #8 fixed errors during rollback, not errors before entering it. Current fixed-size signing/NoIndex paths offer no established common failure; wheel exhaustion is extreme (approximately `u32::MAX` entries).

### GANG-13 — Successful acquisition is allowed after the supplied deadline

- **Severity / status:** Low contract concern; **intent ambiguous / attempt-first behavior is test-pinned**.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/gang/multi.rs:143-157` returns Held before checking the deadline. `schedule_single` uses the same attempt-first policy.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Supply an already-past deadline while capacity is free. Acquisition can succeed rather than report expiry.
- **Decision / repair:** Decide whether a deadline bounds all successful acquisition or only contention retries; document/test that contract consistently instead of calling the chosen policy an accidental implementation bug.

Gang/scheduler provenance note: Rejected candidates include foreign-holder release races (merge rejects them), duplicate/nonmember ACKs (filtered), empty-cohort quorum (unreachable), classic hold-and-wait deadlock (ordered acquisition with contention rollback), and formerly fixed deletion/Run-Stop overlay issues. Explicit low-level activation semantics, deferred liveness integration, and fixed top-N candidate policy are not presented as additional accidental defects.

## Third audit pass

Third pass complete: four subagents plus the parent channel-lifecycle investigation added 47 numbered concerns (CHAN, SUBNET, BLOB, AGG and REPL). All returned concerns are recorded, including qualified/known limitations rather than treating every entry as a new confirmed bug. The additional requested partition/traversal coverage is tracked separately below. Source-only investigation; no runtime checks or production edits.

### CHAN-01 — Concurrent roster updates leave permanently inconsistent forward/reverse indexes

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed interleavings, not runtime-reproduced.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/channel/roster.rs:376-390` updates forward membership and the peer reverse index under separate guards. `remove` similarly updates them separately (`398-422`), and `remove_peer` relies exclusively on the reverse entry to find channels (`429-452`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** An add publishes forward membership, a concurrent remove clears it and its reverse entry, then add inserts the reverse entry: `is_subscribed` can remain true for an absent forward member. Conversely, removal can clear the reverse entry after a concurrent add has restored forward membership, leaving a live forward member that peer cleanup and channel enumeration cannot find. These are lasting mismatches, not merely inconsistent snapshots during mutation.
- **Repair direction:** Serialize each peer/channel lifecycle across both indexes, or use authoritative per-membership records and generation-aware cleanup.
- **Impact boundary:** Reverse-index-based caps, token sweeping (`mesh.rs:5299-5300`), and failure cleanup can miss/misclassify membership. Per-publish exact ACL/token rechecks remain present; no unauthorized payload delivery is claimed from this race alone.

### CHAN-02 — Expiry cleanup can erase a newly accepted replacement subscription

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed stale-cleanup race.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:5324-5332` checks a retained chain, releases its map guard, then unconditionally revokes/removes the peer/channel and chain. A successful re-subscribe replaces that chain (`44188-44191`) and grants/rosters the subscriber (`36764-36783`). Inline publish cleanup follows the same check-then-revoke pattern (`44825-44895`).
- **Trigger:** Cleanup sees an expired old chain and pauses after deciding it is invalid. A valid replacement subscribe completes and is acknowledged. Cleanup resumes and removes the new chain, ACL and roster entry.
- **Consequence:** A successfully renewed subscriber silently loses delivery until it subscribes again.
- **Repair direction:** Associate cleanup with the exact retained-chain/membership generation it inspected; conditionally revoke/remove only that version, or serialize revalidation and replacement through one per-membership owner.
- **Prior related finding:** Historical channel C-3 covers failure/disconnect chain cleanup, not stale expiry cleanup racing renewal.

### CHAN-03 — Fail-fast reports count unattempted recipients as attempted

- **Severity / status:** Low; source-confirmed public-report inconsistency.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:44902-44905` initializes `PublishReport.attempted` to every eligible recipient, but FailFast returns on the first failed send (`44919-44934`). `channel/publisher.rs:119-120` defines the field as the number actually attempted.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Three eligible recipients, first send fails. Only one send runs, but the returned partial report says three were attempted, overstating delivery attempts and obscuring which recipients were skipped.
- **Repair direction:** Increment attempted as each send is started in FailFast, or expose distinct eligible/attempted counts.
- **Contract check:** Returning an `Ok(partial_report)` is intentional in `docs/CHANNELS.md:127`; the older module-level “return Err” prose is stale and is not separately promoted as a runtime bug.

### CHAN-04 — Delayed successful membership requests can undo a later acknowledged operation

- **Severity / status:** Medium source-supported protocol concern; full reordered-datagram scenario not runtime-reproduced.
- **Evidence:** Membership requests are retransmitted as independent fire-and-forget datagrams with one correlation nonce (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:36603-36641`). Accepted Subscribe requests are deliberately reprocessed, not remembered as terminal operations (`36748-36762`); Unsubscribe unconditionally removes membership (`36806-36819`). The wire carries correlation nonces, not a per-membership generation (`wire/src/channel/membership.rs:55-85`), and membership dispatch directly applies each received event (`mesh.rs:31653-31672`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** On a fire-and-forget receive stream (`default_reliable = false`, with no reliable promotion), the first Subscribe datagram is delayed; its retry arrives and is acknowledged. A later Unsubscribe is acknowledged, then the previously unseen first Subscribe arrives and re-adds the member. Conversely, a delayed Unsubscribe can remove a newer subscription. Stream accounting does run before membership dispatch (`mesh.rs:31197-31227,32985-33025`), but `FireAndForget::on_receive` always accepts (`wire/src/reliability.rs:460-465`); authenticated-counter replay protection does not order distinct retransmission datagrams. Reliable-mode ordering can prevent this particular reordering path.
- **Repair direction:** Fence desired membership state with a monotonic per-peer/channel generation or equivalent operation ordering, while still rechecking current authorization on accepted retries.
- **Impact boundary:** This concerns subscription lifecycle/availability, not bypass of token verification or admission gates.

Channel parent-pass provenance: Exact ACL checks, inline token expiry/root/revocation validation, authenticated ACK-peer binding, and pending-ACK drop cleanup are present. Two-tier ACL updates alone were not promoted as a payload authorization bypass because fan-out cross-checks the exact name and retained token. Queue-group filtering can drop a selected copy, but no additional undocumented guarantee was established in this pass.

### Additional requested coverage

Additional requested coverage complete: partition/failure recovery contributed 11 PART entries and native NAT/port-mapping traversal contributed 15 NAT entries. All six subagent results from this continuation are recorded. Standalone utilities, production-connected defects, existing limitations, and unresolved contract questions are explicitly distinguished. No production files changed and no builds/tests/runtime reproductions were run.

### SUBNET-01 — Revoked credentials can acquire the new authority epoch

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed admission race, not runtime-reproduced.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/subnet/auth.rs:1568-1581` checks generation floors before separately reading `auth_epoch`. Admission installs the resulting context without revalidating that pairing (`mesh.rs:20894-20948`); later currentness compares the captured epoch (`20974-20981`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Admission validates generation 0 against floor 0, pauses, then a generation-1 floor updates the epoch and completes invalidation. Admission resumes, captures the new epoch and installs an already-revoked credential that passes currentness until expiry/another invalidation.
- **Repair direction:** Verify floors and epoch coherently, then fence context publication against revocation. Valid roots/signatures/challenges do not repair the race.

### SUBNET-02 — Subject revocation misses contexts installed after its invalidation scan

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed race.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/subnet/auth.rs:2714-2740` checks subject floors before returning a context. Subject updates deliberately do not advance the authority epoch (`1205-1211`); enforcement scans existing contexts (`subnet/admission.rs:247-273`; `mesh.rs:21059-21074`). Context installation at `mesh.rs:20948` is not synchronized with that scan.
- **Trigger / consequence:** A valid presentation passes subject checks; the subject is then revoked and its scan completes; the old verified context installs afterward. The removed subject retains subtree admission until expiry/another invalidation.
- **Repair direction:** Serialize verification/publication with subject-floor updates, or add subject-scoped currentness fencing that does not churn unaffected siblings.

### SUBNET-03 — Concurrent revocation commits can replay in the wrong order

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed durable-ordering defect.
- **Evidence:** Subject registry updates serialize independently (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/subnet/auth.rs:1242-1258`) from journal append (`subnet/floor_store.rs:90-102`). `mesh.rs:21059-21065` does not preserve registry acceptance order across those operations.
- **Trigger:** Revision 1 raises ATTACH and pauses before journaling. Revision 2 raises EXPORT and journals first; revision 1 then journals. Both calls succeed.
- **Consequence:** Live state has both restrictions, but restart applies revision 2 first and rejects revision 1 as stale, losing acknowledged ATTACH revocation.
- **Repair direction:** Give registry acceptance and durable ordering one owner, or persist equivalent complete monotonic state with authenticated per-right provenance.

### SUBNET-04 — Failed revocation persistence cannot be repaired by retrying the fact

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed failure path, not fault-injected.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:21016-21028,21059-21074` updates memory before journaling and invalidates contexts only after successful append. Journal failure returns `StateNotPersisted` (`subnet/floor_store.rs:95-99`), but retry sees unchanged registry generations and skips both append and invalidation. Readback reports persistence from store presence alone (`mesh.rs:21114-21134`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Storage transiently fails during a valid floor update, then recovers. Retrying the same signed floor returns unchanged without persisting it; restart loses revocation. Subject contexts can also remain live because both attempts bypass invalidation, while signed readback can claim `persisted: true`.
- **Repair direction:** Track dirty/pending durable commits, retry persistence, ensure revocation invalidation cannot be bypassed by I/O failure, and attest actual durable coverage.

### SUBNET-05 — Subject floors are not enforced on local gateway export authority

- **Severity / status:** High enforcement concern; source-confirmed gap, intended local-gateway coverage needs confirmation.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/subnet/auth.rs:2483-2541` compiles gateway authority using `verify_credential_set`, which does not consult subject floors. Their check exists only in session admission (`2714-2723`). Applying a subject floor invalidates remote contexts only (`mesh.rs:21059-21074`), without changing the authority epoch. Export checks therefore retain the existing gateway aggregate (`auth.rs:2255-2285`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Apply a subject floor removing a gateway entity's EXPORT right. Existing exported registrations remain authorized and recompiling its old credential still succeeds. Organization admission still constrains callers; this is continued subnet export after withdrawal, not anonymous RPC access.
- **Repair direction:** Define and enforce subject-floor semantics for local ROUTE/EXPORT rights during compilation and invalidate affected published authority/export stability stamps.
- **Qualification:** No documented local-gateway exemption was found; if intentional, successful removal/readback must disclose that limitation.

### SUBNET-06 — Invalidation snapshots can delete newly valid replacement contexts

- **Severity / status:** Medium availability concern; source-confirmed stale-cleanup race.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/subnet/admission.rs:229-237,253-271,279-287` collects stale node IDs, then unconditionally removes whatever context now occupies each key.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Re-admission installs a valid replacement after the stale-ID snapshot but before removal. The invalidator deletes the new context, making successfully admitted authority disappear.
- **Repair direction:** Conditionally remove the sampled context/version, rather than a bare node ID. No unauthorized-access consequence is claimed.

### SUBNET-07 — Non-raising floor revisions are not durably retained

- **Severity / status:** Low/medium replay/availability concern; source-confirmed.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/subnet/auth.rs:1245-1247` advances a subject revision even if no right generation rises, returning false; `mesh.rs:21059-21065` journals only true changes. Subtree updates (`auth.rs:1143-1149`) do not retain newer non-raising revisions even in memory.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Accept a newer revision with unchanged generations, then restart. Its revision watermark disappears, allowing an older signed fact formerly rejected as stale to apply. Generations still cannot decrease; the demonstrated risk is unexpected extra revocation, not re-admission.
- **Repair direction:** Preserve authenticated revision watermarks separately from whether enforceable generations changed.
- **Prior related discussion:** The earlier subnet-auth review covers non-revoking floors/revision anchoring, not this durable-state gap.

Subnet security provenance: No exact duplicates were found in the inspected report/historical reviews. Root/signature checks, one-use presentation challenges, subject/session binding, attenuation, topology containment, exact-boundary EXPORT, and organization admission remain present. PublicAuthenticated export registration is refused. Issuer/leaf validity nesting prevents a leaf outliving its issuer; broader issuer-envelope lifetime policy remains ambiguous, not a demonstrated bypass. Signed policy distribution without a live policy reader was not promoted as an immediate forwarding mutation defect.

### BLOB-01 — Opposite-direction transfers allocate the same stream ID

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed collision scenario, not runtime-reproduced.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/dataforts/blob/transfer.rs:82-88` starts each process's transfer counter at one; pending reassembly expects sequence zero (`289-307`). Each peer's request consumes sequence zero on the same stream (`mesh.rs:47974-47994`), so simultaneous serving reuses the stream and starts response data at one (`49602-49630`). Requests route to request handling, not data reassembly (`31981-32000,32724-32730`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Two separate processes concurrently fetch their first chunk from each other. Each buffers the response waiting for data sequence zero, which will never arrive, then times out despite healthy peers/content.
- **Repair direction:** Allocate disjoint initiator namespaces within each peer pair and preserve peer identity in pending-transfer routing.

### BLOB-02 — Cancelled chunk fetches leave engine-owned pending transfers

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed cancellation leak.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:47822-47824` registers pending engine state without a drop guard; cleanup occurs only in later executed result/timeout branches. Discovery wraps the fetch in a 10-second timeout (`47922-47942`), shorter than its own 15-second idle timeout.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Discovery or an application abandons a fetch to a silent connected peer. Pending buffers/senders/stream state remain without an owner polling timeout; repeated probes accumulate entries. Stream eviction does not remove the separate engine map.
- **Repair direction:** Return cancellation-safe registration ownership that removes pending state and closes the stream on drop.

### BLOB-03 — Directory fetch detaches workers before staging cleanup

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed worker-lifecycle gap.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/dataforts/dir.rs:687-723` spawns file workers; the join loop returns on the first error (`725-735`), dropping/detaching remaining handles. Earlier file-reference decoding can also fail after workers were spawned. The caller then removes their staging tree (`587-596`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** One file fails while another worker is transferring/writing. Fetch returns while detached workers continue against a deleting/deleted tree; cleanup may leave partial output on open-file-restricting platforms. Cancelling reconstruction also bypasses ordinary cleanup.
- **Repair direction:** Stop and join workers before cleanup; give staging lifetime cancellation-safe ownership.
- **Prior related repair:** Store-side drain-on-error was documented in the June performance audit; this is the separate fetch-side path.

### BLOB-04 — GC releases exclusion before physically deleting the chunk

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed historical residual.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/dataforts/blob/mesh.rs:1189-1205` atomically takes the deletable refcount entry, then performs physical deletion after releasing that ownership. `blob/refcount.rs:147-160,184-198` permits concurrent reference/pin recreation.
- **Trigger / consequence:** A reference/pin succeeds after GC takes the old entry but before unlink. GC deletes content now represented by a positive/pinned entry; later fetch fails.
- **Repair direction:** Serialize acquire/pin/store/delete through one per-hash lifecycle through physical deletion.
- **Prior related finding:** May 18 D-1's repaired pre-take race remains after the take; the newly positive refcount survives but its content does not.

### BLOB-05 — Greedy eviction does not release manager-owned backing files

- **Severity / status:** High; source-supported memory-bound violation.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/dataforts/greedy/cache.rs:392-402` removes accounting and returns the entry. Eviction processing withdraws announcements/releases references but never closes/removes its backing file (`greedy/runtime.rs:918-987`); the RedEX manager retains an independent file clone (`redex/manager.rs:183,694-720`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Churn distinct channels beyond the aggregate cache cap. Reported usage decreases while evicted payloads remain resident; re-admission reopens retained files with fresh zero-based accounting.
- **Repair direction:** Define backing-file ownership and reclaim evicted storage without closing unrelated consumers.
- **Prior related findings:** D-3/D-26 concerned counters, not retained manager ownership.

### BLOB-06 — Old greedy eviction cleanup can remove a new generation's references

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed asynchronous generation race.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/dataforts/greedy/runtime.rs:862,909,963` separates eviction from reference cleanup with an announcement await. Cleanup removes shadow buckets by channel name alone (`1083-1108`); later withdrawal is likewise not generation-fenced (`975`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** A evicts B and awaits; another dispatch re-admits B and records fresh references; A resumes and releases B's new bucket/withdraws its fresh announcement. GC can collect blobs belonging to the newly live channel.
- **Repair direction:** Detach old generation buckets atomically at eviction and fence asynchronous announcements/withdrawals by incarnation.

### BLOB-07 — Refcount table replacement races shadow-set mutation

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed residual concurrency gap.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/dataforts/greedy/runtime.rs:1002-1026` snapshots the table before locking shadow state. Replacement drains shadows while holding the table write lock (`433-472`); eviction has the same snapshot/lock separation (`1084-1108`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** A recorder captures table A; replacement installs B/drains shadows; recording then increments A and publishes a shadow hash later cleaned against B. A leaks a positive reference and B can lose another source's legitimate count.
- **Repair direction:** Bind each shadow generation to its table or hold one consistently ordered lifecycle lock across both.
- **Prior related finding:** May 12 blob B-9's sequential swap fix is present; this is its concurrent residue.

### BLOB-08 — Cancelling heat publication permanently pins in-flight markers

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed cancellation gap.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/dataforts/gravity/counter.rs:555-578` skips in-flight hashes and marks new emissions. `blob/mesh.rs:914-934` clears/rolls back only after the awaited sink returns, not when the future is dropped.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Timeout/drop `tick_blob_heat` during batch publication. Those hashes stop emitting heat indefinitely even if reads continue.
- **Repair direction:** Use a drop guard for uncommitted emission markers.
- **Prior related repairs:** D-17 and May 18 M-4 handle candidate/commit and returned errors, not cancellation.

### BLOB-09 — Lossy directory names can collide and silently overwrite content

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed Unix filename case.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/dataforts/dir.rs:471-479` uses `to_string_lossy`; resulting duplicate manifest paths are not rejected. Fetch workers independently truncate the same reconstructed pathname (`687,1218`). Symlink targets also convert lossily (`341`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Distinct valid non-UTF-8 names collapse to one replacement-character string. Store/fetch can succeed while losing a filename/content or reconstructing the wrong symlink target.
- **Repair direction:** Preserve raw names in the format or reject names/targets not faithfully representable before storing.

### BLOB-10 — Small-reference range reads trust advertised size over actual bytes

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed input-triggered panic path.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/dataforts/blob/mesh.rs:3703-3718` checks range against reference size, then calls unchecked `Bytes::slice` on fetched content. Small decoding bounds but does not bind advertised size to content (`blob/blob_ref.rs:749-780`); fetch verifies hash only.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Decode a Small ref for an existing short chunk with an inflated advertised size; request a range within that size but outside actual bytes. Range fetch panics instead of returning typed size disagreement.
- **Repair direction:** Check actual length/use checked slicing and return `ShortChunk`.
- **Prior related findings:** D-11/M-6 fixed the Manifest sibling, not this Small arm.

### BLOB-11 — Directory manifest fetch preallocates an unverified advertised size

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed allocation hazard, outcome allocator/OS-dependent.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/dataforts/dir.rs:900-911` reserves the entire Manifest size before fetching/verifying its first chunk. `fetch_dir_with` uses this path before decoding the directory manifest.
- **Trigger / consequence:** An accepted reference advertises up to 16 GiB with unavailable hashes. A fetch that will fail can first request a 16-GiB allocation, exhausting constrained/non-overcommitting hosts.
- **Repair direction:** Cap initial reservation, grow with verified bytes, and bound directory-manifest materialization.
- **Prior related findings:** Sibling of October transport preallocation and May 12 B-12, not a wholly new allocation pattern.

### BLOB-12 — Directory modes do not round-trip

- **Severity / status:** Low/medium known residual, not novel.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/dataforts/dir.rs:625-646` discards directory entry mode.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Store/fetch private or read-only subdirectories; reconstructed directories do not preserve those internal permissions.
- **Repair direction:** Apply supported mode semantics during reconstruction, or explicitly document their absence.
- **Prior finding:** August 10 Linux deployment audit lines 248–264. A private staging root prevents exposure outside the tree, not internal mode loss.

### BLOB-13 — Greedy Tree references protect only their root

- **Severity / status:** Known/qualified lifecycle limitation; not classified as an unintentional new defect.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/dataforts/greedy/runtime.rs:1011-1020` explicitly records only a Tree's root hash.
- **Conditional consequence:** Descendant chunks remain independently GC-eligible even while the root is referenced.
- **Decision / repair:** Define recursive-reference protection and walk/pin descendants if that is the supported lifetime contract. The implementation expressly acknowledges this limitation.

Blob provenance: Transfer completion checks declared length and BLAKE3; Manifest stride validation/range guards are present. Directory extraction uses a private root, lexical validation and symlinks-last ordering; no new traversal bypass was established. No byte-semaphore deadlock was proved. Documented dedup-on-store versus fetch validation, cold-start stripe gaps and whole-file store buffering are not promoted as new accidental defects.

### AGG-01 — Partially delivered summaries are deduplicated without retry

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed eventual-state delivery gap.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/aggregator/daemon.rs:540-550` discards `PublishReport`. Default BestEffort succeeds when any subscriber receives (`mesh.rs:44978-44987`); the summary then enters the novelty baseline.
- **Trigger / consequence:** One subscriber receives a changed summary while another send fails. Unchanged future ticks are suppressed, leaving the failed subscriber stale until another fold change; the partial error is also hidden.
- **Repair direction:** Consume per-peer results and preserve retry obligations or bounded refresh.
- **Prior related finding:** October AG-1 concerns publication/dedup interaction, but its repaired query-without-publication case is different.

### AGG-02 — Later publication errors discard earlier successful query-state updates

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/aggregator/daemon.rs:340-345,370-381` accumulates successful summaries, but `publish_summary(...).await?` skips committing that prefix when a later kind fails.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Capability publication succeeds, reservation publication fails. Queries see an empty/stale successful prefix, and repeated later-kind failure republishes earlier state indefinitely.
- **Repair direction:** Commit the successful prefix before returning failure; `summarize_now` already does so (`415-429`).

### AGG-03 — Exported summary targets never reach channel/query authorization

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed embedded-API integration gap.
- **Evidence:** Config stores `summary_targets` (`behavior/aggregator/config.rs:76-82`), but channel registration installs visibility only (`daemon.rs:581-585`). Fan-out reads destinations from the gateway table (`mesh.rs:44744-44753`); v2 query admission always passes no export targets (`query_v2.rs:183-185`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Configure an embedded exported aggregator with nonempty targets. Intended subscribers/query callers are refused or filtered; an empty successful publish can still become deduplicated.
- **Repair direction:** Install configured export targets and share resolved policy with query access.
- **Qualification:** Standalone daemon config rejects exported mode; the public embedded/core API remains affected.

### AGG-04 — Scale validates one group incarnation and mutates another

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed registry ABA race.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/aggregator-daemon/src/lib.rs:1171-1207` validates a retained entry, then scales by a fresh name lookup (`1217-1219`; `behavior/aggregator/registry.rs:397-404`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Unregister group A and replace its name with different-kind B between validation and scaling. A's factory grows B, creating mixed-kind replicas that first-replica query/ownership checks misclassify.
- **Repair direction:** Validate and resize one retained, incarnation-checked entry under its lifecycle lock.
- **Prior related review:** Earlier lock-duration findings did not address replacement-name reuse.

### AGG-05 — Registry drain races still-admitted Spawn operations

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed teardown race.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/aggregator-daemon/src/lib.rs:1262-1273` drains one names snapshot. Mutation service handles remain alive until afterward (`489-497`); dropping them does not cancel already executing handlers.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Authorized Spawn creates a replacement/new group while drain awaits an old monitor's shutdown. The new group was absent from the inventory and remains registered/running after teardown returns.
- **Repair direction:** Close mutation admission, settle in-flight operations, then drain a stable inventory.

### AGG-06 — Summary history eviction temporarily removes the current value

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed query availability gap.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/aggregator/daemon.rs:516-528` uses one historical FIFO rather than retaining a current value per fold/subnet identity.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Frequent capability changes evict the only stable reservation summary. LatestSummary/v2 return empty success for a known fold until the next tick republishes its missing baseline, potentially extended by client caching.
- **Repair direction:** Keep current values keyed by identity independently of bounded change history.
- **Prior related discussions:** Historical H2/E1 concern dedup/FIFO cost, not eviction of a still-current value.

### AGG-07 — Audience validation ignores ordered-rule shadowing

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed residual of October AG-5.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/aggregator-daemon/src/scope.rs:145-152` accepts any mapping containing the desired level value. Real subnet assignment overwrites matching levels with later rules (`subnet/assignment.rs:249-295`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** A subnet-3 daemon has `region:eu→3` followed by the same predicate/level mapping to 4. Startup accepts an audience that no peer can actually reach; summaries and nonoperator query access are unavailable.
- **Repair direction:** Validate effective reachability under actual ordered/correlated rule semantics, not the union of declared values.

### AGG-08 — Subnet summaries count the common fold and merely relabel it

- **Severity / status:** Known correctness/integration limitation; high source confidence, not novel.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/aggregator/summarizer.rs:118-134,169-184` counts every common-fold entry and labels the result with the configured source subnet, without filtering ownership.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Subnet 3's common fold includes subnet-4 providers; those providers inflate subnet-3 capability counts. Reservation counts have the same mismatch.
- **Repair direction:** Establish source-subnet ownership filtering or refuse unsupported nonglobal aggregation.
- **Prior documentation:** `AGGREGATOR_MULTI_HOST_PLAN.md` G3/D4 and Phase-3 S1 explicitly defer this; D4 also requires refusing nonglobal reservation aggregation, which current validation does not enforce.

### AGG-09 — Accepted custom fold kinds silently produce no summaries

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium API/support mismatch; future support is explicitly acknowledged.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/aggregator/daemon.rs:229-233` accepts a custom summarizer/kind, but production dispatch returns an empty batch without invoking it (`500-505`).
- **Consequence / repair:** Valid accepted configuration yields empty queries indefinitely. Reject unsupported kinds or provide the required fold handle/invocation path rather than imply custom execution is available.

### AGG-10 — Publisher shutdown timeout detaches the task

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium lifecycle concern; source-confirmed mechanism, production slow-send trigger not reproduced.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/aggregator/daemon.rs:642-659` moves its JoinHandle into timeout; expiration drops/detaches it instead of aborting/joining.
- **Conditional consequence:** A publication exceeding the backstop outlives successful unregister/scale teardown.
- **Repair direction:** Retain ownership through timeout, then abort and join; establish a concrete slow-send witness before claiming observed availability impact.

### AGG-11 — On-demand ticks mask stalled background publishing

- **Severity / status:** Medium health-accounting concern; source-supported.
- **Evidence:** On-demand work increments the same generation used by `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/aggregator/daemon.rs:682-689` as an elapsed/interval progress clock.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Many successful SummarizeNow calls accumulate generation surplus; subsequent background stall is not deemed unhealthy until elapsed expected ticks consume that surplus.
- **Repair direction:** Track last background progress independently of total/on-demand generations.
- **Prior related review:** May 23 pass-2 S3 recommended the formula without accounting for independent on-demand ticks.

Aggregator provenance: Known unfinished cross-host spawn, leases, persistent identities, bounded refresh and v2 ordering metadata are not new accidental defects. No nonexistent pagination protocol was inferred. Registry session-peer authorization is present. Startup limits not applied by `spawn_group` remain an operator-config contract concern, not an established unauthorized resource-exhaustion path.

### PART-01 — Stale failure callbacks remove recovered peers' fresh state

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed production cleanup race.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/failure.rs:418-445` captures verdicts then invokes callbacks after releasing detector guards. Only rerouting is incarnation/verdict-fenced; the surrounding callback continues unconditionally (`mesh.rs:14760-14839`), removing roster, admission, identity and capability state, then withdrawing routes/graph edges.
- **Trigger / consequence:** A failure callback pauses while recovery/replacement installs fresh state. Its reroute policy can correctly reject the stale verdict, yet later callback mutations still destroy the new peer's authority/discovery/subscriptions.
- **Repair direction:** Fence each cleanup mutation against the failed incarnation/current verdict and serialize it with replacement. A callback-entry check alone is insufficient.

### PART-02 — Delayed old-session heartbeats can replace the detector's new incarnation

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed production race.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:30104-30145` clones a session, releases peer lookup, authenticates its heartbeat, then records its ID. `failure.rs:108-118` accepts any nonzero epoch, without checking current peer ownership. Heartbeat verification does not check receive-lifetime retirement (`wire/src/session.rs:1889-1932`), unlike the normal data path.
- **Trigger / consequence:** E1 is captured; E2 replaces it and updates liveness; delayed E1 then overwrites detector incarnation. Later verdicts name E1, so guarded rerouting refuses to invalidate the current failed session while PART-01's unfenced cleanup still runs.
- **Repair direction:** Publish liveness under the peer-transition fence with exact session ownership; random session IDs cannot be treated as ordered epochs.

### PART-03 — RTC endpoint close leaves failure-detector records behind

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-supported production leak.
- **Evidence:** Endpoint eviction removes peer/address/session indexes but not detector state (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:1332-1374,29156`). Later timeout cleanup skips absent peers (`34372-34412`), bypassing detector removal (`34575-34576`). The stale-record cleanup API (`failure.rs:544-581`) has no production caller in the subagent's trace.
- **Trigger / consequence:** An anchor repeatedly admits distinct identities whose RTC endpoints close. Historical detector records and scan cost grow, and removed peers can still generate failure callbacks.
- **Repair direction:** Remove the exact evicted detector incarnation during endpoint close or schedule guarded stale cleanup.

Partition API boundary: The following contested/recovery utility findings are source-level API concerns, **not demonstrated production partition data loss**. The subagent found no production callers of `reconcile_entity`, `PartitionDetector`, `CorrelatedFailureDetector`, or `RecoveryManager`; accounting for contested subprotocol IDs is not a dispatcher. This missing integration was already documented in May 18 carried-forward line 602.

### PART-04 — Reconciliation omits horizon bytes from causal-link equality

- **Severity / status:** Medium standalone-API defect; source-confirmed.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/contested/reconcile.rs:105-117` compares parent hash, sequence and payload, but not `horizon_encoded`.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Branches have identical payload/parent/sequence but different observed horizons. Equal lengths yield AlreadyConverged despite different head bytes; a remote successor can yield Catchup but fail local parent validation because the parent's horizon participates in hashing.
- **Repair direction:** Compare complete links and include every differing field in deterministic tie-breaking. Append validation prevents accepting incompatible successors; it does not repair the incorrect outcome.

### PART-05 — Valid pruning is misclassified as divergence

- **Severity / status:** Medium standalone-API defect; source-confirmed.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/contested/reconcile.rs:77,103-117,153-156` positionally compares retained local suffixes to the remote suffix without verifying local baseline coverage.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Split at 3; local has pruned through 5 and retains 6–7; remote supplies original 4–7. Local 6 is compared to remote 4 and remote wins a false conflict by apparent suffix length, despite identical underlying history.
- **Repair direction:** Require the baseline range or reconcile by sequence with explicit snapshot anchors; report unavailable history instead of conflict.
- **Distinction:** Ordinary pruning triggers this, independently of STATE-05's over-pruning marker.

### PART-06 — Zero tracked peers leave mass-failure mode stuck

- **Severity / status:** Medium standalone-API state defect; source-confirmed.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/contested/correlation.rs:184-188` returns Independent without clearing `in_mass_failure`.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Enter mass-failure mode then call `record_failures(&[], 0)`. The returned verdict is independent but recovery remains throttled, even after window expiry through this path.
- **Repair direction:** Reset mass-failure state before the zero-tracked return.

### PART-07 — Recovery queue entries have no drain lifecycle

- **Severity / status:** Medium standalone-utility leak; source-confirmed.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/failure.rs:1051-1059` appends each no-alternate failure to a queue; recovery only removes failed-node state (`1063-1070`). No queue consumer/drain method was found.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Repeated fail/recover cycles accumulate entries for the manager's lifetime.
- **Repair direction:** Remove unused queue state or implement owned/deduplicated recovery consumption.
- **Prior related repair:** May 3 #51 preserves retry state, not queue ownership.

### PART-08 — Empty alternate updates retain obsolete reroutes

- **Severity / status:** Medium standalone-utility defect; source-confirmed.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/failure.rs:1038-1043` replaces alternates only when the new list is nonempty; `get_action` always prioritizes retained alternates (`1075-1079`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Fail with an alternate, then fail with an empty list after that route disappears. The new call returns Queue, but later actions repeatedly reroute through the obsolete alternate and bypass retry/drop handling.
- **Repair direction:** Refresh supplied alternates including empty lists while preserving failure time/retry count.
- **Prior related repair:** Adjacent residue of May 3 #51.

### PART-09 — Conflict results do not preserve usable losing-fork material

- **Severity / status:** Qualified standalone API/support mismatch; not demonstrated live data loss.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/contested/reconcile.rs:219-229` discards the newly generated fork keypair/builder and returns only a winner/ForkRecord, not losing events. Fork construction assigns a fresh random identity each call (`continuity/discontinuity.rs:118-147`).
- **Conditional consequence:** A consumer cannot rely on this result alone to preserve/replay one canonical losing fork, contrary to “losing chain preserved” prose.
- **Repair direction:** Define a fork-retention owner and return usable fork identity/material; no production outcome consumer was established.

### PART-10 — Remote suffix validation does not anchor its first parent

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium standalone-API concern; replacement impact unproved.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/contested/reconcile.rs:264-285` validates origin/start and links between remote events, not the first link against the agreed split event.
- **Conditional consequence:** An invalid-base suffix can produce misleading Catchup/Conflict. Local append rejects incompatible catch-up; no production conflict consumer was found that proves history replacement.
- **Repair direction:** Require and verify the split anchor before deriving reconciliation outcomes.

### PART-11 — Repeated partition detection creates overlapping records

- **Severity / status:** Known/qualified API behavior; not a newly proved production defect.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/contested/partition.rs:177-187` appends each mass-failure verdict, while correlation returns a rolling window's complete failed set.
- **Conditional consequence / repair:** Repeated detection can accumulate overlapping records; coalesce/deduplicate at detection if one record per incident is required.
- **Prior relation:** May 2 #32; current recovery intentionally updates overlapping records, so that behavior is not relabeled as a fresh recovery bug.

Partition provenance: Current payload/link tie-break fixes, remote origin/start rejection, sorted correlation and callback-outside-lock repair are present. Superposition readiness ordering hypotheses were not promoted: production does not call the questionable source-advance seam and immediately collapses on replay completion. No ordinary caller was established for the additional circuit-reset concurrency hypothesis.

### REPL-01 — Replication responses exceed the unfragmented transport ceiling

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed wire-size mismatch.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/replication_runtime.rs:156-161,176-190` submits each whole SyncResponse as one event. `mesh.rs:45614-45640` builds one unfragmented packet; `wire/src/protocol.rs:32-55,577-581` enforces the smaller-than-8192-byte event ceiling.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Two retained 4-KiB payloads plus response framing exceed that ceiling, well within the default 256-KiB catch-up budget. Retry keeps generating the same undeliverable response; larger batches can also trip packet-builder/UDP limits.
- **Repair direction:** Chunk by encoded transport size and provide supported fragmentation/reassembly for single oversized events.
- **Relationship:** Same transport-boundary class as MDB-02, but a separate replication producer and recovery path.

### REPL-02 — Bandwidth classes can permanently reject an indivisible valid event

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed progress failure under valid small-budget configuration.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/replication_budget.rs:289-292,321-352` imposes class-specific per-grant limits. Response construction does not adapt to those limits or communicate a smaller acceptable chunk.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Configure `replication_budget_fraction = 0.0001` and Background/Realtime replication; a 7-KiB event fits the wire but exceeds the class allowance even with a full bucket. The same event is refused forever.
- **Repair direction:** Couple response sizing/admission and define progress-preserving handling for one event above class allowance.
- **Prior related finding:** May 11 R-62's oversize escape covers Foreground only; related to May 19 B-11/B-12 caps.

### REPL-03 — Advertisement recovery never replays the lost role notification

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed observer-state gap.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/replication_coordinator.rs:494-520` advances role then returns before observer notification on sink error. `reconcile_advertisement` retries publication/clears stale state without notifying (`344-364`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Bootstrap/promotion/withdrawal advertisement transiently fails then recovers. Discovery heals, but MeshOS's observer-derived holder/leader state can remain missing or phantom indefinitely (`behavior/meshos/sources.rs:144-187`).
- **Repair direction:** Retain pending transition notifications or publish observer state according to committed local role, with explicit advertisement status.
- **Prior related finding:** May 18 R-31 addressed advertisement divergence, not observer replay.

### REPL-04 — Replicated managers overwrite each other's mesh router

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed ownership conflict.
- **Evidence:** Each `Redex::enable_replication` installs its private router in one mesh slot (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/manager.rs:308-317`). Wiring Drop unconditionally clears that slot (`113-121`). Unknown channels are rejected by the active router (`replication_router.rs:106-110`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Two replicated managers share one mesh with different channels. The second breaks inbound routing for the first; dropping either can then break the survivor. Manager comments otherwise acknowledge multiple managers sharing a mesh.
- **Repair direction:** Use a mesh-scoped channel registry with ownership-aware registration/removal; one manager per mesh only mitigates the current path.

### REPL-05 — Fully evicted leaders report the wrong catch-up floor

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed live heap replication stall.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/replication_catchup.rs:202` maps an empty retained index to floor zero despite a nonzero allocation frontier. BadRange handling then advances only one sequence per retry (`replication_runtime.rs:1947-1951`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Retention removes every event from a leader with a large allocated history; a fresh replica joins with no new writes. Catch-up performs potentially days of empty retries instead of jumping to the known frontier.
- **Repair direction:** Return `next_seq` when retained history is empty.
- **Prior related finding:** May 18 R-40's fully evicted residual; distinct from PERSIST-01 because no restart/watermark loss occurs.

### REPL-06 — File publication precedes replication registration

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed first-open race.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/manager.rs:692-695,711-744` exposes the file before registering its replication runtime. Reopen configuration validation derives original replication options from that registry (`1049-1067`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Concurrent identical first opens observe the inserted file before its runtime. The second falsely reports it was originally opened without replication, despite identical requested config.
- **Repair direction:** Publish file and replication registration within one per-channel initialization lifecycle.
- **Prior related review:** May 11 R-53 covers configuration comparison, not the publication window.

### REPL-07 — Leadership convergence does not reconcile divergent log prefixes

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed historical residual.
- **Evidence:** Competing leaders converge by tail length/node ID (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/replication_runtime.rs:1440-1461`), then catch up only from the current frontier (`replication_step.rs:250-255`). Append verifies placement at that frontier, not earlier content (`replication_catchup.rs:470-505`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Partitioned leaders write different sequence-1 events; one also writes sequence 2. After healing, the shorter side appends only sequence 2. Equal tails then mask different sequence-1 payloads; equal-tail divergence requests nothing at all.
- **Repair direction:** Reconcile content/frontier with an authoritative rebuild policy, or fence divergent writers before accepting partition writes.
- **Prior findings:** May 11 R-5/C-2 and May 18 R-21; repaired role convergence does not establish history convergence.

### REPL-08 — Failed sync can still advance advertised progress

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed failure path under `UnderCapacity::EvictOldest`.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/replication_catchup.rs:492-526` advances the file tail before fallible sync. EvictOldest can leave the role active after a no-op retention sweep (`replication_runtime.rs:2077-2084`); the production tail provider reads raw `next_seq` (`manager.rs:843-844`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Persistent append succeeds, explicit sync fails, and retention has nothing to evict. The next tick advertises unsynced progress; if now equal to leader tail, catch-up need not retry durability. Crash before another barrier can lose advertised events.
- **Repair direction:** Track/advertise a separately committed durable frontier and retry failed sync.
- **Qualification / provenance:** Default Withdraw avoids continued replica heartbeats here. This is May 18 R-22 residue; no claim is made that current leader retention consumes heartbeat acknowledgements.

### REPL-09 — NotLeader clears the discovery timer without rearming it

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed recovery-state gap.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/replication_runtime.rs:1920-1929` clears leader belief/tokens; tracker clearing also resets its leader-wait timer (`replication_heartbeat.rs:239-241`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** A pinned leader gracefully relinquishes while catch-up is pending and replies NotLeader. Without a replacement heartbeat, the replica neither requests nor times out into discovery/election, remaining Replica indefinitely.
- **Repair direction:** Rearm bounded leader-discovery hysteresis after rejection.
- **Prior finding:** May 11 R-16, through the current NotLeader path rather than bootstrap.

### REPL-10 — Persistent replicas cannot recover retention gaps via skip-ahead

- **Severity / status:** Known operational limitation; explicitly heap-only/v1, not newly accidental.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/replication_runtime.rs:1785-1844` retries gap recovery through `skip_to`, while persistent files reject it (`redex/file.rs:1489-1497`).
- **Consequence / repair:** A retained-history gap remains unrecoverable by heartbeat retries. Document/require rebuild or implement an explicitly durable reset/anchor protocol.

### REPL-11 — Backpressure rejection does not install the documented retry backoff

- **Severity / status:** Qualified low/medium performance concern; source-confirmed state omission.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/replication_runtime.rs:1915-1916` describes exponential backoff, but Backpressure only logs (`1967-1972`).
- **Consequence / repair:** Subsequent requests continue at heartbeat cadence, not the described exponential deferral. Cadence is still bounded; workload impact was not reproduced. Add the promised retry state or correct the contract.

### REPL-12 — Cancelling cancellation can detach runtime ownership

- **Severity / status:** Qualified lifecycle concern; no ordinary manager caller cancelling this wait established.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/replication_runtime.rs:388-422` takes its join handle before awaiting it.
- **Conditional consequence:** Dropping that cancellation future detaches the join; a later cancel sees no handle and can return early.
- **Repair direction:** Keep shutdown ownership independent of any caller's cancellable wait if the public API promises a join barrier.

Replication provenance: Current append/rollback guards prevent the alleged partial-batch corruption; response authorization retains membership, believed-leader and peer-keyed token checks. These were not re-reported. All entries are source-derived; no runtime packet-size or failure injection was executed.

### NAT-01 — UPnP renewal allocates a different external port

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed renewal/API mismatch.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/traversal/portmap/upnp.rs:258-262` renews via install, which calls `add_any_port` (`232-235`). The subagent traced installed `igd-next 0.18.0` allocation behavior: it chooses a fresh external port. Mapping task replacement forgets the old mapping and shutdown removes only the latest (`portmap/mod.rs:650-652,677`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Normal renewals create additional mappings rather than extending the advertised one. Old advertisements lose reachability on expiry and abandoned mappings remain open until then.
- **Repair direction:** Refresh the existing external port with `add_port`; explicitly clean up intentional replacements.
- **Prior relation:** April 30 #20/June 21 addressed initial allocation's returned port, not renewal semantics.

### NAT-02 — NAT-PMP renewals retain a stale WAN IP

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed stale-address path.
- **Evidence:** Only probe updates the cache (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/traversal/portmap/natpmp.rs:550-563`). Install uses that cached address (`618-632`); renewal calls install only (`640-643`), while the production task probes once (`portmap/mod.rs:572`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** The router changes WAN IP but continues accepting mappings. Successful renewals indefinitely advertise the old IP, so address-change handling never sees the actual change.
- **Repair direction:** Refresh external-address discovery during renewal and construct the result from current data.

### NAT-03 — Automatic mapping uses configured port zero instead of the bound port

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed ephemeral-bind defect.
- **Evidence:** Both startup and explicit mapping-loop setup use `self.config.bind_addr.port()` (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:26142,26985`), not the socket's actual bound address.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Bind `0.0.0.0:0` and enable port mapping. The mesh listens on an allocated nonzero port, but the mapper receives zero; it cannot publish a mapping to that socket.
- **Repair direction:** Use the actual bound UDP address after bind.

### NAT-04 — Mapping destination IP is selected independently of the listening interface

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed multihomed configuration mismatch.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/traversal/portmap/sequential.rs:272-280` picks the default-route source IP without receiving the mesh's bound IP (`mesh.rs:26145`). NAT-PMP's request socket also binds wildcard (`natpmp.rs:517`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** The mesh listens only on interface B while discovery chooses interface A. UPnP forwards to A and advertises Open although no mesh socket listens there.
- **Repair direction:** Derive gateway/source selection consistently with the bound socket or refuse incompatible explicit binds.
- **Prior relation:** June 21 A1 required a correct LAN IP; being concrete/nonloopback alone does not make it correct for this socket.

### NAT-05 — UDP-to-TCP relay fallback reuses an endpoint-bound registration proof

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed transition failure.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/traversal/blind_relay.rs:1464-1479` obtains/signs a challenge and observed endpoint; exchange fallback retransmits the same signed request over a new tunnel (`1356-1358`). Relay verification binds proof to the now-different endpoint (`640-645`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** UDP HELLO succeeds, REGISTER times out, TCP fallback succeeds at connecting. Registration deterministically gets BadProof rather than recovering.
- **Repair direction:** Restart HELLO/challenge after changing transports.
- **Mitigation:** Enrollment CLI retries initial failures after 15 seconds; direct `relay_register` surfaces the failed exchange.

### NAT-06 — Relay-wide TCP switching invalidates existing UDP-bound joiner channels

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed endpoint-lifetime mismatch.
- **Evidence:** Tunnel installation changes the relay's send route globally (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/traversal/blind_relay.rs:1389`; `transport.rs:530-538`). Existing channel entries retain their original joiner endpoint and accept only it (`blind_relay.rs:728-734,759-765`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Channel A is established over UDP; a later bind to B activates TCP fallback. B can work, but A's sends arrive from the synthetic tunnel endpoint and are dropped while reverse traffic targets old UDP. Tunnel replacement has the same issue.
- **Repair direction:** Migrate/rebind channels and peer endpoint state when switching transport, not merely the relay-wide sender.

### NAT-07 — Stopping the relay accept loop does not stop accepted tunnels

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed child-task ownership gap.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/traversal/blind_relay.rs:1039-1043` detaches accepted TCP connections with Arcs owning relay state/socket. Cancelling `run()` does not cancel or join them.
- **Trigger / consequence:** Library caller cancels relay run but retains the runtime. Established tunnels continue registering/forwarding and keep the UDP port bound; activity before idle expiry can sustain this.
- **Repair direction:** Own connection tasks under the server lifetime and cancel/join them on exit.
- **Qualification:** CLI process exit masks the lifetime issue; no runtime reproduction was run.

### NAT-08 — Coordinator forwards forged PunchAck origin claims

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed authenticated-peer optimization abuse, not Noise-authentication bypass.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:43601-43605,43666-43668` forwards ACK contents without binding `ack.from_peer` to the authenticated sender. The destination trusts its recorded coordinator (`32323-32330`); WebRTC admission gates do not compare claimed origin.
- **Trigger / consequence:** A session peer sends the coordinator an ACK claiming to be B for A. Forwarding authenticates the coordinator and consumes B's pending waiter, starting a premature direct handshake; the real ACK then has no waiter.
- **Repair direction:** Validate the original sender before forwarding.
- **Prior relation:** May 18 S-2 fixed direct forged ACKs at the final recipient but left coordinator laundering.

### NAT-09 — Old punch timeout removes a newer observer

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed stale-cleanup race.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:4746-4758` unconditionally removes by reflex address on timeout; a new scheduler can replace that entry (`43412-43414`) after timeout selection.
- **Consequence:** Old cleanup removes the new observer, so valid keepalives cannot produce its ACK.
- **Repair direction:** Generation-stamp observers and conditionally remove the sampled entry.
- **Prior relation:** Current cancellation handling protects replacements on receiver closure, not timeout/removal interleaving.

### NAT-10 — Train budget covers only the observer, not the sender

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed global-ceiling ownership gap.
- **Evidence:** Sender tasks continue to deadline (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:43470-43518`); only observer tasks retain the concurrent-train slot (`43525-43530`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Early keepalive or observer replacement releases the slot while its sender still runs. Repeated introductions can accumulate live send trains beyond the configured global ceiling; per-source limits do not enforce that ceiling.
- **Repair direction:** Hold the reservation across the complete sender/observer lifetime or cancel the sender when the observer ends.
- **Prior relation:** June 21 global-limit and July 11 CAS reviews did not cover the ownership mismatch.

### NAT-11 — Reflex probes lose named-peer identity through address resolution

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed routed-peer failure.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:52453-52456` probes B using address-based send, which now resolves that address's owner (`45545-45555`). B's routed send address belongs to relay R; the waiter remains keyed by B, while R's reply resolves only an R waiter (`32176-32179`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** Classification samples routed peers (`53030`). Their probes time out even on healthy routes and can obstruct usable classification despite suitable direct anchors.
- **Repair direction:** Select direct UDP observation anchors explicitly and preserve node identity through the send.

### NAT-12 — Automatic mapping can overwrite or clear a manual reflex override

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium ownership/precedence concern.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/traversal/portmap/mod.rs:315-328` overwrites any differing current reflex on renewal; revoke clears whichever override is active (`335-344`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** An operator changes the override after mapping install; later renewal/revoke replaces or clears that newer manual value. Clearing while mapping stays live can also let renewal publish an address without restoring override flag/class.
- **Decision / repair:** Define automatic/manual precedence and track explicit ownership/generation if concurrent use is supported.

### NAT-13 — Positive subsecond NAT-PMP TTL encodes the delete operation

- **Severity / status:** Medium direct-client API defect; source-confirmed historical residual.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/traversal/portmap/natpmp.rs:585-593` rejects only zero Duration, then truncates seconds.
- **Trigger / consequence:** `Duration::from_millis(500)` passes validation but encodes lifetime zero, the NAT-PMP removal operation.
- **Repair direction:** Validate encoded seconds before sending.
- **Qualification / prior relation:** Production mesh/TCP callers use whole-second constants. This is April 30 Core #113's positive-subsecond residue, not the already-fixed literal-zero case.

### NAT-14 — Enrollment TCP mapping ignores a shortened granted lease

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed adjacent production caller.
- **Evidence:** `net/crates/net/sdk/src/enrollment/portmap.rs:120-124` sleeps configured renewal time instead of the returned `current.ttl`; its production interval is 30 minutes (`29`).
- **Trigger / consequence:** A gateway grants a 60-second lease. Enrollment's mapping expires long before renewal, leaving the advertised endpoint unavailable.
- **Repair direction:** Schedule renewal before the granted TTL, preserving the configured ceiling.
- **Prior relation:** April 30 #21 was fixed in native PortMapperTask, not this separate keeper.

### NAT-15 — Discovery state is pinned across network changes

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium lifecycle limitation; one-shot policy constrains recovery expectations.
- **Evidence:** UPnP can rediscover its gateway but retains immutable local IP (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/traversal/portmap/upnp.rs:80-91`); NAT-PMP retains its original gateway (`natpmp.rs:437-449`).
- **Conditional consequence:** Moving networks can select a new IGD while forwarding to the old LAN address, or continue probing an obsolete NAT-PMP gateway.
- **Repair direction:** Rebuild gateway/local-interface discovery coherently on network transitions if continued recovery is part of the contract. This strengthens NAT-04's lifecycle case rather than establishing a separate remote attack.

Traversal provenance: Wildcard/Open classification, destination diversity and cached handshake reflex are previously documented tradeoffs. PCP support, unsupported-platform discovery and permanent-only IGDs were not inferred from broader prose. Initial one-shot discovery/retry policy and deferred transaction correlation are explicit. NAT-PMP echoed-port omission was not promoted without a supported wrong-live-request scenario. Relay ingress attribution and inspected codec bounds did not reveal additional concrete defects.

## Fourth audit pass

Fourth pass complete: four subagents audited payments, organization authority/revocation, sensing, and MeshOS; the parent traced cancellation ownership/composition. All returned concerns are recorded below: 41 added numbered entries (CANCEL 3, ORG 5, MOS 15, PAY 10, SENSE 8). Qualified contracts, runtime-disabled/legacy paths and known residuals are distinguished from source-confirmed production defects. Source-only investigation; no production edits, builds/tests or runtime reproductions.

### CANCEL-01 — Unary cancellation is not observed until after REQUEST publication

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed cancellation-contract violation.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh_rpc.rs:8185-8205,8285-8303,8365-8373,8423-8424`; the public pre-cancel guarantee is `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:35660-35672`.
- **Trigger:** Reserve a token, cancel it, then issue an otherwise routable unary call using that token. Alternatively cancel while the first reply subscription or request publish is awaiting.
- **Impact:** Unary setup does not check the token: it awaits subscription and publishes the request before registering its cancellation waiter. A provider can execute the handler even though cancellation preceded the call. A slow subscription/publish also delays cancellation observation. The later best-effort CANCEL cannot undo side effects. The documented promise is specifically that pre-cancel returns without publishing; this is not a claim that cancellation after dispatch can roll back work.
- **Guard check / repair:** Streaming constructors use `until_cancelled`; unary does not. Establish cancellation ownership before setup and race setup/publish waits against it, retaining the normal post-dispatch cancellation guard.

### CANCEL-02 — Cancel-registry entries outlive dropped unary calls and failed streaming setup

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed process-lifetime bookkeeping leak.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/cancel_registry.rs:207-246,263-277`; `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh_rpc.rs:3546-3572,4786-4803,6786-6796,7573-7583,7650-7664,8423-8424,8491`.
- **Trigger:** Drop/timeout a unary future after its nonzero token was registered, rather than letting its internal select finish. Another path is a token-bearing streaming constructor whose `until_cancelled` setup step returns a subscription/transport error or whose constructor future is dropped.
- **Impact:** Registration stores `notify: Some(...)`. Unary cleanup calls `release` only after the await or from the explicit cancellation arm; `UnaryCallGuard::drop` removes RPC pending state but not the token. `until_cancelled` releases only when its cancellation arm wins, not when the step fails or the future is dropped. No stream watcher owns failed setup. GC explicitly retains every entry with a notify, so these entries accumulate for the mesh lifetime under repeated external timeouts/setup failures.
- **Repair direction:** Use a registration RAII lease spanning the constructor/call and explicitly transfer ownership to the returned stream watcher. Do not rely on orphan TTL: it intentionally excludes registered entries.

### CANCEL-03 — Hedged RPC clones one cancel token into independent calls

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed cancellation-composition defect.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk/src/mesh_rpc_resilience.rs:499-560`; `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/cancel_registry.rs:177-194,207-231,241-246`; `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh_rpc.rs:4840-4849,8451-8485`.
- **Trigger:** Call either hedge helper with a nonzero `opts.cancel_token` while multiple candidates are waiting. Cancel the operation once. A second ordinary schedule cancels the primary before a delayed hedge starts.
- **Impact:** Each hedge clones the same options/token, but the registry supplies one shared `Notify` and `cancel` calls `notify_one`. One active candidate can consume the signal and release the shared registry entry; the hedge loop treats its `Cancelled` error as a failed candidate and keeps waiting. Other active candidates can continue indefinitely with no deadline. Delayed candidates can start after release without the cancellation latch and can make the outer operation return success after cancellation.
- **Guard check / repair:** This is library-generated token reuse, not a caller manually misusing a per-call token. Normal winner cleanup still drops losing futures and sends wire CANCEL; that does not make an explicit cancellation terminal for the outer hedge. Give the outer hedge one cancellation owner and drop all child futures on cancellation, with separate child registration where needed.

Cancellation provenance: The historical v3 review records allocation, GC frequency and watcher duplication issues, not these ownership/composition failures. Token wraparound was not promoted (no realistic lifetime trigger). Sharing `Notify` is not independently reported as a generic group-cancellation API bug: CANCEL-03 identifies the SDK's concrete multi-call consumer. No runtime reproduction was run.

### ORG-01 — Revocation-store replacement can discard grant revocations

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed authority rollback through a trusted local operation.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:21683-21692,21821-21835,21949-21960,22213-22224`; `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/org_admission.rs:729-744`.
- **Trigger:** The installed store contains an acknowledged, unexpired dispatcher/capability-grant revocation. Install an older backup or another store with equal/stronger membership floors but without that grant revocation.
- **Impact:** Replacement checks only membership-floor dominance, then publishes the candidate. A withdrawn grant becomes usable for a fresh proof/call ID without a new root signature or ownership change.
- **Guard check / repair:** Owner consistency, poison checks and two-store publication pins are present; remote callers cannot install a replacement. Protected-call requalification (`cortex/rpc.rs:4470-4478,5222-5289`) consults the same replacement state and cannot restore missing denials. Require candidate dominance for all still-relevant grant revocations, including retention horizons, or durably merge them before publication.
- **Provenance:** Distinct from previously repaired membership-floor replacement races: synchronization exists, but comparison omits the grant-revocation dimension.

### ORG-02 — Capability-grant revocation does not withdraw DISCOVER authority

- **Severity / status:** Medium confidentiality; source-confirmed missing early-withdrawal checks.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/org_grant_revocation.rs:1-30`; `org_grant_registry.rs:600-651,667-696`; `org_scoped_ingest.rs:465-557`; `org_scoped_store.rs:1714-1716`; `org_cold_plan.rs:134-141`; `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:23294-23347,23976-24004`. Unprefixed files are under `src/adapter/net/behavior/`.
- **Trigger:** Provider B and grantee A install an ordinary DISCOVER grant and its audience secret. B later applies a valid capability-grant revocation bundle without separately removing the installed audience registry record.
- **Impact:** Emission eligibility checks validity window/issuer/target but not grant revocation. Ingest and query currentness likewise omit that denylist. A can keep receiving **new private-discovery updates** until expiry or explicit registry removal.
- **Guard check / repair:** The former grantee must already possess the bound audience secret; unrelated peers cannot decrypt or widen the capability. INVOKE admission separately checks revocation, so continued RPC execution is not established. Check revocation at emission/cached-send, ingest and query boundaries; invalidate affected projections when revocation changes. Generation checks do not reject a grant already revoked before construction.
- **Provenance:** Existing expiry clamping and credential-replacement checks are present; they do not implement early grant withdrawal.

### ORG-03 — Authority load/install accepts mismatched owner-audience material

- **Severity / status:** Low; source-confirmed omission, qualified local configuration-safety concern.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/org_authority.rs:1072-1091`; `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:22149-22152,23225-23230`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Trusted provisioning mixes an authentic org-B membership configuration with an org-A audience credential. Load and runtime installation verify membership but do not compare the audience's `owner_org`. B's owner-private catalog can be sealed under A's discovery material.
