- **Boundary / repair:** Requires provisioning error or trusted local install authority, not an independent remote exploit. Reject audience/config owner-org mismatch both at loading and installation.
- **Provenance:** July 21 §2 repaired cross-org audience inheritance during adoption; that guard is present. These other entry boundaries retain the mismatch.

### ORG-04 — Revocation retention assumes grants cannot start far in the future

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium; conditional signed-artifact/clock prerequisite.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/org_revocation.rs:225-236`; `org_grant.rs:548-593,637-645,906-933,996-1014` in the same directory.
- **Trigger / impact:** A correctly signed future-window grant exists, for example from a substantially fast issuer clock, and is revoked before its window starts. Revocation retention is clamped to application time plus maximum grant TTL/skew; verification bounds window length rather than distance into the future. The denial can expire before the grant later becomes valid.
- **Qualification / repair:** Public issuance uses current time; explicit-window issuance is crate-private. No ordinary scheduled-issuance API was established. Preserve signed-expiry denial evidence, or enforce a protocol invariant bounding future grant starts before relying on this clamp.
- **Provenance:** October 17 CO-8 advocated the corresponding leaf clamp for resource bounding; this records its unexamined grant-window assumption rather than claiming the clamp is categorically wrong.

### ORG-05 — Clock correction can resurrect pruned grant revocations

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium; requires a large wall-clock fault and subsequent correction.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/org_revocation.rs:255-260,409-431,2621-2655`; `org_grant_revocation.rs:352-354` in the same directory.
- **Trigger:** A forward clock step crosses a revoked grant's expiry-plus-skew boundary. Pruning removes the denial and a changed-state write persists that removal. The clock is then corrected backward into the grant's validity window.
- **Impact / repair:** The original signed grant can become temporally valid again without its revocation. Define rollback behavior and retain durable revocation/high-water evidence sufficient to prevent authority resurrection; ordinary bounded skew alone does not cover this schedule.
- **Related known residual:** Admission proof freshness uses wall time while replay retention uses monotonic expiry (`org_admission.rs:816-885`). Large backward steps beyond the retention margin remain the already documented July 21 I.3 replay residual, not a new replay finding.

Organization coverage: Current caller/session, dispatcher subject/org/capability, capability-grant issuer/grantee/target binding, owner install serialization, same-core durable publication barriers, replay trust-domain quota repair, descriptor narrowing, credential expiry and scoped-store tombstone/capacity checks were traced and not reported as bypasses. Focused historical duplicate checks covered July 20/21/26/29, September 24 and October 11/17. No runtime reproduction was run.

MeshOS locations below are relative to `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/` unless another full path is given.

### MOS-01 — Timed shutdown detaches the task it can no longer abort

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed task-ownership residual.
- **Source:** `meshos/runtime.rs:791-811,847-868`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A loop/executor join exceeds its timeout, or shutdown is cancelled during either join. The handle has been taken from the runtime and moved into `timeout`; dropping that wait detaches the task. Runtime Drop cannot abort the now-absent handle. A pending dispatcher may continue effects after `ExecutorTimeout`; a detached loop may retain event senders through registry observers.
- **Repair / provenance:** Retain handle ownership while awaiting by reference and abort/join on timeout, or use an abort-on-drop owner. The May 13 I5 direct-runtime-Drop fix is present but does not cover ownership moved into shutdown waits.

### MOS-02 — Clean shutdown drops accepted deferred actions

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed false clean completion.
- **Source:** `meshos/executor.rs:378-390,480-484,578-582`; `meshos/runtime.rs:807-844`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Admission or a dispatcher retry hint defers an accepted action. The action channel closes before its retry deadline. `recv() == None` breaks the executor immediately, discarding its heap; runtime can report success without dispatching or recording terminal failure for that work.
- **Repair / provenance:** Separate input closure from completion; drain under an explicit shutdown policy or record cancellation. Per-entry retry counts do not cover channel closure. May 14 I7 describes draining the heap, but current code does not.

### MOS-03 — Freeze silently consumes accepted ICE force operations

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed lost emergency operation.
- **Source:** `meshos/reconcile.rs:64-69`; `meshos/event_loop.rs:1607-1619`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Submit a valid ICE `ForceEvictReplica` or `ForceCutover` during an active freeze, then tick before thaw. Reconcile returns no actions due to freeze, but the loop unconditionally clears forced eviction/placement lists. The accepted, audited operation never reaches the dispatcher, including after thaw.
- **Repair / provenance:** Preserve unconsumed entries or explicitly execute permitted emergency operations through freeze. ICE deliberately bypasses ordinary-admin freeze rejection. Related May 14 Deck S16 discusses admission policy, not loss of already accepted force work.

### MOS-04 — Suppression state commits before action delivery succeeds

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed optimistic acknowledgement.
- **Source:** `meshos/event_loop.rs:1578-1604,1641-1659`; suppression at `meshos/reconcile.rs:361-370`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Emit `MarkAvoid` while the executor queue is full, or let its later dispatch fail. The loop already recorded the avoid entry; later passes suppress delivery until TTL, while the dispatcher never applied avoidance. Snapshots likewise claim avoidance. Backoff and rebalance cooldown writebacks have the same ordering shape.
- **Repair / provenance:** Distinguish pending/applied state and commit suppression with successful handoff/acknowledgement. Drop counters are not rollback. Residual beyond May 13 C4 / May 18 O-7: queue loss is observable and excluded from recent emissions, but suppression state remains.

### MOS-05 — SDK dispatcher swallows cluster-backpressure transitions

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed known integration residual.
- **Source:** `meshos/sdk.rs:336-345`; producer `meshos/executor.rs:405-423,443-458`; no-op default `meshos/executor.rs:67`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Use `MeshOsDaemonSdk::start` and cross a configured cluster-backpressure threshold. `SdkRoutingDispatcher` implements only `dispatch`, so the trait's default callback consumes on/off transitions. Neither the user's dispatcher nor SDK daemon-control broadcasts receive them.
- **Repair / provenance:** Implement the wrapper callback, broadcast through the router and forward to the inner dispatcher. `RouterControlSink` works if reached. May 13 C2 fixed executor-side callback production, not this consuming boundary.

### MOS-06 — Default maintenance deadline is advertised but never enforced

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed inconsistent default handling.
- **Source:** `meshos/state.rs:374-386`; `meshos/reconcile.rs:401-414`; `meshos/event_loop.rs:1507-1516`; public contract `deck.rs:1366-1382`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Call `enter_maintenance(node, None)` with a daemon/replica that cannot drain. Public API says None selects the configured default; `DrainStart` advertises that finite deadline, but authoritative state stores None. Reconcile never emits `DrainFailed`.
- **Repair / provenance:** Resolve the default when constructing authoritative state so controls, snapshots and reconciliation share it. Explicit duration works. May 14 Deck S18 addresses the clock anchor, not the missing default.

### MOS-07 — Daemon reconciliation can undo maintenance draining

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed contradictory action, dispatcher-dependent restart effects.
- **Source:** `meshos/reconcile.rs:76,104-155,401-405`; `meshos/state.rs:374-387`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A daemon has desired Run intent. Enter maintenance; it exits after `DrainStart`. Maintenance does not change effective daemon intent, and `diff_daemons` does not check local maintenance. The next pass emits `StartDaemon` during draining or steady maintenance, potentially alongside the transition to Maintenance.
- **Repair / qualification:** Actual restart depends on the installed dispatcher; the start emission itself is confirmed. Suppress/derive daemon intents appropriately during drain and isolation. Node-pin/backoff checks do not cover maintenance.

### MOS-08 — Rejected maintenance transitions already changed the public mirror

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed state projection regression.
- **Source:** `meshos/state.rs:287-311`; snapshot consumer `meshos/snapshot.rs:729-751`.
- **Trigger / impact:** While local state is Recovery, receive delayed Maintenance, or delayed DrainFailed after ExitingMaintenance. The peer mirror is overwritten before the successor guard rejects the local transition. Snapshot peer and local states contradict each other until another update.
- **Repair / provenance:** Validate before mutating both projections; define stale-update ordering for remote mirrors if required. Residual of May 14 N2: its forward guard protects only the local machine.

### MOS-09 — Lifecycle folding treats an empty fallback display name as another daemon

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed stable-identity mismatch.
- **Source:** `meshos/state.rs:328-345`; identity equality `meshos/event.rs:237-243`; converter `meshos/sources.rs:64-72`; fallback producer `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/compute/registry.rs:213-236`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Unregister a daemon while its host mutex is held, for example through a re-entrant supported `with_host` closure. Registry intentionally emits Unregistered with an empty name if `try_lock` fails and tells observers to correlate by ID. Folding instead keys the full `{id,name}`, creating an empty-name Stopped entry while leaving the original named Running entry. This can suppress restart, block maintenance completion and collapse contradictory entries when snapshots key numeric IDs.
- **Repair / provenance:** Key lifecycle state by stable ID; retain name as metadata or resolve fallback events to the existing ID. Related registry observer work exists; no exact historical fallback-identity match was found.

### MOS-10 — Deferred actions are not revalidated against changed desired intent

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed supported retry-contract concern.
- **Source:** `meshos/executor.rs:429-464,517-520,578-582`; admission `meshos/backpressure.rs:157-173`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Dispatcher returns `DispatchError::retry` for StartDaemon. Before retry, desired intent becomes Stop or its node pin moves. The heap later dispatches the old action without intent/generation validation. While actual state is Stopped, current reconciliation produces no stop action to cancel the pending start; withdrawn work can execute before a later corrective tick.
- **Qualification / repair:** A custom dispatcher could reject stale work independently; executor/SDK does not guarantee that. Attach desired-state generations and reject superseded deferred work before dispatch. Deferred replica intents have the same lifetime question; no unimplemented migration adapter is assumed.

### MOS-11 — Failure sequence allocation and publication are not ordered together

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed lost failure-stream record.
- **Source:** `meshos/executor.rs:625-647`; concurrent writer `meshos/event_loop.rs:1444-1477`; consumer `deck.rs:2319-2330`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Writer A allocates N then pauses before ring insertion. Writer B publishes N+1 and a snapshot exposes it. FailureStream advances its watermark; A subsequently inserts N and the stream permanently skips it.
- **Repair / provenance:** Serialize sequence allocation plus publication across both writers. Individual ring locking and a shared atomic prevent duplicates but not commit-order gaps. Distinct from restart/reset epochs and the already repaired separate-counter collision.

### MOS-12 — Publicly acknowledged admin commands can terminate in inert catch-all folds

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium; behavior confirmed, support/defer status ambiguous.
- **Source:** `meshos/state.rs:355-465,575-587`; `meshos/event_loop.rs:1250-1260`; public `deck.rs:1350-1363,1388-1446`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Call Drain, Cordon, Uncordon, RestartAllDaemons or InvalidatePlacement. Public methods describe operational behavior and return a ChainCommit; the loop audits them, but no applicable desired/actual fold or separate dispatcher invocation performs their operation. Drain does not enter maintenance or emit its advertised DrainStart.
- **Qualification / repair:** Some state comments explicitly defer cordon/drain to a later phase. Do not treat deliberate scaffolding as a newly broken completed feature; either implement the advertised consumer or explicitly reject unsupported commands rather than acknowledge inert success.

### MOS-13 — Shutdown publication can stall before its timed joins begin

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium responsiveness concern; total-deadline contract not established.
- **Source:** `meshos/runtime.rs:790`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A full event queue and stalled loop block the initial unbounded `publish(Shutdown)`. Neither join timeout has started, so the method may never reach its bounded waits.
- **Qualification / repair:** Documentation promises bounded joins, not an overall deadline. Use bounded publication or an independently owned shutdown signal if total responsiveness is intended. Historical May 13 I11 added `publish_timeout`; this internal caller still uses unbounded publication.

### MOS-14 — The deferred heap bypasses bounded action-channel capacity

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium capacity residual; workload/retry-duration dependent.
- **Source:** `meshos/executor.rs:480-484,578-582`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A continuous stream of distinct actions receives long retry/defer durations. Actions leave the bounded input channel for an uncapped heap, permitting accumulation before any retry-count budget is exercised.
- **Repair / provenance:** Bound deferred capacity or define coalescing/admission pressure over both stores. Residual of May 13 I7, not the old absence of per-entry retry limits.

### MOS-15 — Startup observation does not inventory an already-populated supplied registry

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium API-contract concern; concrete production startup caller not established.
- **Source:** `meshos/runtime.rs:529-546`; empty actual-state initialization `meshos/event_loop.rs:600-613`; observer registration `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/compute/registry.rs:66-72`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Construct the runtime with an already managed/populated registry, a supported sharing shape. Attaching its observer does not replay existing registrations, so those daemons remain absent until later signals. A desired Run feed can generate duplicate starts; maintenance/snapshots can treat the node as empty.
- **Repair / qualification:** Establish a race-safe initial inventory plus subsequent observation. The missing reconstruction is confirmed; use of a populated registry by a production startup caller was not established.

MeshOS exclusions: ClearAvoidList's existing global semantics and ForceRestartDaemon's backoff-release-only contract were not called isolation/restart bugs. Snapshots are observational, not a documented restoration API. Explicitly deferred migration aborters/action adapters/capability scaffolding, deliberate control-channel at-most-once delivery, and previously logged gang/scheduler-bridge issues were not re-reported. No tests, builds or runtime reproductions were run.

Payments locations below are relative to `net/crates/net/payments/src/`; binding/adapter paths are relative to `net/crates/net/`.

### PAY-01 — Provider signs caller-selected price and recipient

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed missing provider-price authority.
- **Source:** `flow/mesh.rs:154-158,206-213`; `flow/mod.rs:638-652`; `engine/mod.rs:1031-1047,2544-2604`; `bindings/node/src/payment_provider.rs:401-438,517-545,636-644`; `bindings/python/src/payment_provider.rs:473-506`; consuming gate `adapters/mcp/src/wrap/invoke.rs:376-409`.
- **Trigger:** An otherwise allowed caller requests a quote for the actual paid capability, supplying a smaller positive amount and/or its own `payTo` while retaining a registered asset and supported scheme.
- **Impact:** Mesh quote service forwards the caller's template; the engine checks admission and registry then signs those requirements. Production binding providers use AdmitAll. Published pricing terms are validated/returned but not registered as quote authority. A settlement of substituted terms plus a valid invocation binding can pass redemption without paying the published merchant/price.
- **Guard check / repair:** Signatures, possession, tool identity and independent chain verification remain effective over the substituted requirements; none restore provider-owned commercial terms. Register authoritative requirements/offers per capability and reject quote templates outside them. This does not assume a forged signature or broken facilitator.
- **Provenance:** Focused July/August authentication/registry/publication-gate reviews did not identify this authority omission.

### PAY-02 — A stale settlement owner can remove or complete its successor's claim

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed stale-owner fencing gap with nondefault lifetime configuration.
- **Source:** `engine/mod.rs:1208-1263,1486-1556,2721-2734`.
- **Trigger:** Configure quote validity longer than claim reclaim TTL and leave attempt A's facilitator operation pending past that TTL. B takes over and starts settlement. A subsequently fails verification and releases its claim before B records completion.
- **Impact:** Reclaim has no ownership generation; release removes any unsettled record for that quote, without checking current payload/owner. A can erase B's state, after which B may move funds and get “record vanished mid-settle.” Completion is likewise unfenced. A different-payload takeover can lose a record not owned by A's payload.
- **Guard check / repair:** Existing chain/billing evidence prevents removal once recorded, but not an outstanding replacement settlement. Default 60-second quote validity is shorter than the default five-minute reclaim TTL; supported longer-validity/shorter-TTL settings enable the live predecessor schedule. Persist and check a claim token/generation on every release/completion.
- **Provenance:** Residual of July M3 TTL crash recovery; the cited regression aborts the predecessor before takeover.

### PAY-03 — Ambiguous settlement errors erase provider recovery material

- **Severity / status:** High; destructive bookkeeping confirmed, receipt recovery depends on facilitator semantics.
- **Source:** `engine/mod.rs:1425-1434,1961-1967,2721-2734`; `facilitator/client.rs:244-251,307-322,353-365`; caller reservation guard `flow/mod.rs:1359-1380`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Facilitator settles the transfer, but its response times out/disconnects or cannot be decoded. Every settlement error releases the claim and removes the quote record/replay entry, including preserved authorization. Checker reconciliation then has no record. A paid purchase becomes unknown to the provider and service/receipt recovery depends on external caching or operator reconstruction.
- **Guard check / repair:** Caller-side ambiguous-outcome reservations remain held, protecting budget accounting but not reconstructing provider evidence. A single-use on-chain authorization prevents spending the identical nonce twice; this finding is not identical-nonce double spending. Preserve exposed/ambiguous attempts durably and reconcile them before admitting replacement settlement.
- **Provenance:** Known-related residual: July LOW notes accepted timeouts based on nonce protection, which does not provide durable evidence or purchased service.

### PAY-04 — Cancelled transaction leaves blocking staging work outside its lock lifetime

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed POSIX cancellation race, not locally reproduced.
- **Source:** `policy/store.rs:172,189-217,236-243,271-280`; ordinary cancellable callers `flow/mod.rs:1062-1066`, `flow/a2a.rs:2345`.
- **Trigger:** Cancel transaction A while its staging-creation `spawn_blocking` task is queued/running. Async owner drops its advisory lock, but blocking work can continue. B acquires the lock and creates/writes the same per-process temporary pathname. A's delayed creation treats B's live pathname as stale, unlinks it and creates an empty replacement.
- **Impact:** On POSIX, B can finish syncing its unlinked file handle and rename A's empty pathname over the policy/engine store, publishing corrupt/empty state despite B holding the transaction lock.
- **Repair / provenance:** Own the complete synchronous filesystem transaction and lock together, and use uniquely owned staging files rather than unlinking another operation's path. August hardening moved creation to blocking work; that does not preserve lock ownership across cancellation. Tokio's non-abortable running-blocking-task behavior is required, not a speculative filesystem interleaving.

### PAY-05 — Acknowledged payment-store rename is not directory-durable

- **Severity / status:** High; POSIX power-loss durability concern, source-confirmed missing directory sync, not reproduced.
- **Source:** `policy/store.rs:207-217,236-243,271-280`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A reservation/redemption transaction returns success, the caller proceeds, then power fails before the containing directory's rename update is durable. Staging content is fsynced, but the parent directory is not. Restart can expose predecessor state, losing acknowledged reservations or redemption evidence and potentially reopening budget/execution admission.
- **Repair / provenance:** Sync the containing directory after rename and before committed success, using the repository's durable-publication convention. Normal-operation atomic replacement is not power-loss durability. The same principle was repaired for RedEX in April 30 / May 3; no exact payment-store duplicate was found.

### PAY-06 — Billing retry appends after a torn record without repairing its tail

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed audit-log framing failure.
- **Source:** `billing/mod.rs:167-173,183-202`; `engine/mod.rs:1934-1941,578-588`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A billing append partially writes then fails (e.g. disk full), or the process crashes mid-write. A later retry appends a complete JSON event immediately after the partial prefix. That retry can succeed and set `billing_published`, but read/export rejects the malformed joined line permanently. Engine compaction can eventually retire its copy assuming usable audit evidence exists.
- **Guard check / repair:** Event-ID dedup runs after decoding and cannot repair torn framing; writer locking prevents interleaving, not interrupted writes. Recover only a provably incomplete trailing record under the append lock, or use recoverable framing/transactional storage; preserve loud errors for corruption of completed records.
- **Provenance:** Residual beyond July M4 complete-record dedup/republication recovery.

### PAY-07 — Concurrent HTTP approval redemption shares one budget identity across payments

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed approval concurrency gap, rail-dependent multiple settlement.
- **Source:** `flow/http402.rs:494-502,516-572,599-615`; `policy/spend.rs:557-560`; opaque authoring `flow/mod.rs:1441-1460`.
- **Trigger:** Two concurrent HTTP fetches read the same approved held pseudo-quote with identical requirements. Each reserves that quote and later authors/sends its own payment. Use approval for a per-day limit, with an opaque signer producing distinct valid Solana transactions.
- **Impact:** Lookup, reservation and approval removal are separate transactions. The second reservation is treated as a retry: holder count rises but spend does not. A delayed second fetch can reuse its saved approved quote even after the first clears approval. Distinct opaque transactions can both settle against one approved amount/reservation.
- **Guard check / repair:** Fresh attempts have unique IDs, but approval reuse bypasses their generation. EIP-3009's same-quote deterministic nonce protects that rail; it does not make wallet-authored opaque transactions identical. HTTP has no provider-side idempotency guarantee. Atomically claim/consume the approved attempt, or single-flight one durably authored payload; another payment needs its own approval/reservation.
- **Provenance:** Residual of August attempt-ID/approval work (`CUBIC_PR737_UNADDRESSED_2026_08_01.md:431-437`), specifically the approved-hold path bypassing fresh-attempt uniqueness.

### PAY-08 — Billing append failure does not close redemption admission

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium policy-contract inconsistency.
- **Source:** `engine/mod.rs:92-97,2575-2588`.
- **Trigger / impact:** An attached log append fails after `rec.billing` is persisted. A caller already holding the quote and signing identity can still invoke because redemption checks `billing.is_some()`, not successful publication.
- **Qualification / repair:** Engine-state evidence remains durable and logless engines are deliberately supported. This conflicts with the Billing error's stated “broken billing stream should stop serving” posture if that is contractual. Require publication for configured-log admission without breaking deliberate logless mode.

### PAY-09 — Re-verification skips lost billing-publication recovery

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium public-API concern; production caller not established.
- **Source:** `engine/mod.rs:1798-1820,2249-2258`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Reconcile an already-billed record whose `billing_published` is unset using either re-verification API. Their already-billed branches return Served without an event to republish, unlike accept-payment's already-served recovery branch.
- **Repair / provenance:** Share the pending-publication recovery path when reconciling completed records. Behavior is confirmed; no production caller of these re-verification methods was established. Related to July M4, not a separate novel accounting mechanism.

### PAY-10 — Live billing reads can see a writer's incomplete append

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium transient read consistency concern.
- **Source:** `billing/mod.rs:183-202`; production consumer `bindings/node/src/payment_provider.rs:576-590`.
- **Trigger / impact:** `read_all` reads without the append lock while another writer is extending the file. It can observe an incomplete final JSON line and return BadRecord to the production read-billing API; the error may disappear once the writer finishes.
- **Repair / boundary:** Take a consistent read snapshot under the appropriate writer synchronization. Unlike PAY-06, permanent corruption is not required; do not silently ignore genuinely damaged committed records.

Payments exclusions: Permanent transaction/redemption tombstones, frozen records and reconciliation queues retain authority/evidence intentionally. Observed-tier retirement and finite re-verification windows are policy choices. Missing EVM receipts mapping to Pending is explicitly documented (`checker/eip155.rs:235-247`). Ordinary HTTP re-fetch after ambiguity is a new payment attempt by contract; fail-closed reservation retention is not independently called a budget leak. No additional supported checker attribution/finality defect was established. No tests/builds/runtime reproductions were run.

Sensing reachability: Sensing is runtime-disabled by default (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:3808`; SDK `net/crates/net/sdk/src/mesh.rs:242`) and requires explicit enablement. Exact-provider organization observation and retained refresh are production SDK paths without rendezvous/RedEX. Legacy rendezvous requires RedEX plus an explicitly installed leader role; election/scope-membership automation is deferred (`mesh.rs:20202-20233`). Provider-free OrgCapabilityRegistration remains dark (`mesh.rs:38601-38612`), and the exact-sensing bridge is test/fixtures-only (`src/adapter/net/mod.rs:79-87`). No SDK language bindings were established. Sensing paths below are relative to `net/crates/net/`.

### SENSE-01 — An expired relay can promote a provisional cached beat into live continuity

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed false-ready projection on enabled production paths.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/behavior/sensing/continuity.rs:307-330`; ingress `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:39714-39748`; downstream feed `39786-39848`; provisional flush `34130-34189`.
- **Trigger:** Upstream relay U retains a newer pending attestation when its provider stops. U eventually expires continuity and flushes the beat provisionally. Relay R receives it after R's deadline but before R's maintenance tick, with unchanged provider incarnation/generation.
- **Impact:** `on_admitted_beat` does not expire an already-passed deadline, leaving stored Established state. Ingress derives `bearing = true` from that state, and downstream/local cells renew deadlines from cached evidence. A projection that should be Unknown becomes Ready without a fresh continuity-bearing provider event.
- **Guard check / repair:** Default maintenance is five seconds (`mesh.rs:3760`), while valid windows can be subsecond. Signature, newer-sequence, solicited-row and epoch checks do not test that elapsed deadline. Read-time `projected_at` cannot prevent a new false-bearing feed from renewing cells. Expire continuity before processing provisional beats/deriving bearing.
- **Provenance:** Earlier interval-anchor/read-time expiry fixes do not cover incoming-cache promotion; no exact focused historical match found.

### SENSE-02 — Retired emission-counter eviction can cause permanent equivocation poisoning

- **Severity / status:** Medium; known design residual with stronger source-confirmed impact.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/behavior/sensing/emitter.rs:56-68,374-379,574-587`; receiver gate `sensing/incarnation.rs:313-338` in the same behavior directory; production admission `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:39404-39430`.
- **Trigger:** Observe interest A, close it, churn enough other provider interests to evict A's retired emission slot, then reopen A in the same provider incarnation after its status/start estimate or capability generation changes. This observer need not have received the other interests and can still retain A's old gate.
- **Impact:** Recreated emission starts at sequence zero. A different signed fingerprint at the observer's retained sequence poisons A's incarnation, rejecting all later beats until a higher incarnation. If the old frontier is larger, initial stale drops can progress to poisoning when reused sequence reaches it. Ordinary close/reopen does not recover.
- **Guard check / repair:** Active slots are protected, retired counters are not; emitter and observer LRU eviction are independent. Preserve non-reusing emission sequences despite slot eviction or refuse resurrection without uniqueness. Source comments acknowledge stale drops, not this permanent-poison consequence.

### SENSE-03 — Rendezvous replacement renews abandoned consumers without a refresh

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed legacy leader-path soft-state violation.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/behavior/sensing/rendezvous.rs:980-989,1017-1030`; `sensing/table.rs:263-270` in the same behavior directory.
- **Trigger / impact:** A consumer stops refreshing but keeps its session. Just before expiry, capability reconciliation replaces its provider. Reconciliation carries original soft-state TTL, not remaining lifetime, and registers the replacement at now plus that full TTL. Repeated replacements can indefinitely retain abandoned demand, cache/capacity and provider work without consumer refresh.
- **Guard check / repair:** Only live rows are copied, but just-before-expiry is sufficient; session-failure cleanup does not apply. Carry absolute expiry through replacement/orphan restoration. This is not the organization SDK watch path.
- **Provenance:** July 15 §6 preserves consumer coverage on replacement, not its expiry.

### SENSE-04 — Leader-addressed deregistration misses the leader's consumer rows

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed legacy leader-path withdrawal failure.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:38523-38535,38378-38392`; frame contract `src/adapter/net/behavior/sensing/frames.rs:155-163`.
- **Trigger / impact:** An authenticated consumer sends whole-interest `Deregister { target: None }` to its explicitly installed leader while keeping its session. Dispatch removes Peer(from_node) only from the mesh interest table, while capability consumers reside in `leader.relay.table` and the mesh table uses a shared Leader row. Relevant demand remains; fan-out/provider work continues until soft-state expiry despite explicit cancellation.
- **Guard check / repair:** Session loss invokes another cleanup path. Existing remove-downstream removes every interest and would over-cancel if used blindly. Add digest-scoped leader consumer removal and propagate aggregate/branch-death effects. The provider-free organization path is still dark.

### SENSE-05 — A short-lived consumer truncates longer-lived shared leader demand

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed legacy leader-path false-unavailable projection.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:38369-38391,33890-33958`.
- **Trigger:** A registers with TTL 30 seconds and refreshes at 15 seconds. At t=1, B joins the same interest with TTL one second and then stops, using the same valid sampling interval.
- **Impact:** Intake derives shared cadence from all consumers but overwrites its Leader row expiry using only B's TTL. That row expires at t=2 while A's leader-relay demand remains valid to t=30. Provider emissions stop and A becomes falsely Unknown until refresh.
- **Guard check / repair:** Sweeping B does not restore shared expiry; equal intervals cause no cadence-change action. Derive the shared deadline from the latest absolute expiry among surviving consumer rows, updating it whenever those rows change.

### SENSE-06 — Re-ranking can discard the only eligible standby

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium public-helper residual; no production expansion caller established.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/behavior/sensing/rendezvous.rs:1093-1097`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Old active=[A], standby=[B]; fresh ranking active=[B], standby=[A]. Reconcile retains A, filters retained active providers out of the fresh standby list, and does not add freshly ranked B because B was not activated. Standby becomes empty; `expand_to_standby` cannot explore B.
- **Repair / provenance:** Derive standby from all freshly ranked eligible providers minus retained active. Adjacent to July 15 §4's repaired active/standby overlap. No current mesh caller of `expand_to_standby` was found, so no demonstrated SDK outage is claimed.

### SENSE-07 — Singular core projection ignores elapsed observation deadline

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium API freshness residual; current callers limited to tests/benchmarks.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:19471-19480`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Call the singular projection after its deadline but before maintenance expires the cell. It uses `projected()` rather than the deadline-aware `projected_at(now)` and can still return Ready.
- **Guard check / repair:** Neighboring branch and organization projections and production SDK snapshots already check current time. Use one captured now for this public core accessor as well. Residual of the neighboring read-time repair, not a newly established SDK consumer failure.

### SENSE-08 — Raw lease acquisition remains unrenewed and can retain registry/table divergence

- **Severity / status:** Known qualified lifecycle residual; retained SDK family path is repaired.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:16700-16708`; `src/adapter/net/behavior/org_sensing_demand.rs:1129-1165`; `sensing/lease.rs:636-641` in the same behavior directory.
- **Trigger / impact:** Raw public acquisition returns a ticket without a renewal owner. Its table row expires or is refused; later same-key acquisition that does not tighten parameters can return Unchanged from the lease registry without recreating a row. A live ticket can therefore represent no installed demand.
- **Guard check / repair:** Retained `OrgSensingFamily::acquire_provider` supplies renewal and immediately adopts previously unarmed installations. The raw API needs lifecycle-owned renewal or an explicit reconciliation/refresh contract, not another refcount increment.
- **Provenance:** Explicitly related to July 23 §6's registry/table divergence and deferred renewal.

Sensing provenance/exclusions: Receiver-enforced installation generations remain explicitly deferred (`sensing/mod.rs:52-58`), and independent legacy sends still spawn tasks (`mesh.rs:9343-9372`); that ordering concern is known, not a new finding. September 10 refusal acknowledgement/population-floor retry is accepted policy. Cached NotReady before establishment is deliberate bounded pessimism. Evaluator mutations/publication share commit_mu, stale registration IDs cannot remove successors, and user evaluators/destructors are outside registry locks. SDK reads requalify visibility/expiry. Fixture-only bridges were not treated as production organization rendezvous. No runtime reproduction was run.

## Fifth audit pass

Fifth pass complete: four subagents audited wire reliability, A2A task lifecycle, Go/C FFI ownership, and CortEX materialization; the parent traced SDK breaker ownership. All returned concerns are recorded below: 40 added numbered entries (RESIL 3, CTX 10, GOFFI 4, A2A 9, WIRE 14). Existing root-cause overlaps are cross-referenced rather than recounted. Source-confirmed defects, conditional configurations, extension-point concerns, known residuals and unverified consumer reachability are distinguished. No production edits, builds/tests or runtime reproductions.

### RESIL-01 — A pre-reset half-open probe can mutate a later breaker generation

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed stale-probe fencing gap.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk/src/mesh_rpc_resilience.rs:859-866,889-906,928-964,982-1003,1033-1039,1044-1056`.
- **Trigger:** A half-open probe A is pending. The operator calls the supported reset override, which closes the breaker and advances its generation. A later completes with a counted error, or its future is cancelled/dropped. Alternatively a new failure/recovery cycle admits probe B before A returns.
- **Impact:** Closed admissions carry/check a generation, but HalfOpenProbe carries none. A's completion/drop can reopen the explicitly reset breaker, clear another probe's in-flight flag, or let stale success close a later recovery cycle. This can reject healthy calls or permit multiple probes during a state intended to allow one.
- **Guard check / repair:** RAII already prevents an abandoned probe permanently wedging HalfOpen, and Closed-call epoch fencing is present. Neither fences stale probe ownership. Carry a generation/unique probe identity through both completion and Drop, applying mutations only while that admission still owns the current probe.
- **Provenance:** October 11 repaired stale Closed outcomes across breaker transitions; this is the remaining HalfOpen/reset ownership boundary, not a repeat of the repaired no-RAII panic defect.

### RESIL-02 — Custom breaker failure predicate executes under its state mutex

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium extension-point re-entrancy concern; no production custom predicate doing this was established.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk/src/mesh_rpc_resilience.rs:679-681,844-852,915-916,939-940,973`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A configured failure predicate queries the same breaker's public state/counters, for example through a captured Weak handle initialized after construction. Failure bookkeeping holds the non-reentrant state mutex while invoking the callback, so the public diagnostic method attempts to lock it again and deadlocks the calling task/thread. A callback taking an application lock can likewise introduce lock-order coupling.
- **Boundary / repair:** The pure default predicate is unaffected, and no re-entrancy prohibition is documented on the extension point. Compute the predicate result outside the breaker lock, then apply it under the same admission-generation checks; preserve stale-outcome fencing. Runtime reproduction was not run.

Parent resilience coverage: Retry-attempt bounds, default terminal cancellation classification, final jitter cap, Closed-generation guards and probe RAII were inspected. Explicitly documented deadline-plus-backoff semantics were not called a total-deadline guarantee. Extreme floating-point duration conversion edges were not promoted without a practical consumer trigger. Prior CANCEL-01..03 remain the cancellation ownership findings and were not duplicated.

### RESIL-03 — A panicking failure predicate bypasses half-open probe cleanup

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium custom-callback panic path; source-confirmed cleanup ordering, no runtime reproduction.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk/src/mesh_rpc_resilience.rs:903-915,939-943,997-1002,1020-1040`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A half-open probe's inner future returns an error, then its configured failure predicate panics. Bookkeeping disarms ProbeGuard before calling the predicate, but clears `probe_in_flight` only after that callback returns. Unwinding therefore leaves HalfOpen with an in-flight flag and no live owner. If the host contains the task panic, future calls keep returning Open indefinitely until an explicit reset.
- **Guard check / repair:** The existing guard handles panics/cancellation inside the inner future; the default predicate does not panic. Keep cleanup armed until outcome bookkeeping commits, and execute user callbacks outside the mutex without allowing their panic to strand probe ownership. Preserve RESIL-01's ownership fencing when fixing cleanup.

CortEX locations below are relative to `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/`; binding paths are given separately.

### CTX-01 — Workflow success dependencies are discarded on retryable failure

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed lost dependency.
- **Source:** `cortex/workflow/trigger.rs:152-155,241-255`; sanctioned retry `cortex/workflow/fold.rs:94-105`; production evaluator consumer `net/crates/net/bindings/node/src/cortex.rs:2887-2895`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Arm B after A, report A's Failed state, then successfully retry A. AfterTask/IfResult dependencies were permanently pruned as impossible on failure and never fire on the eventual Done.
- **Guard check / repair:** Done cannot be retried, but Failed can; AfterTerminal failure handling does not preserve a success dependency. Distinguish irrevocable abandonment from supported retry before pruning. Related failure-aware trigger cleanup exists; no exact current/historical match found.

### CTX-02 — Concurrent joins can reset an already running/completed reducer

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed asynchronous-materialization admission race.
- **Source:** `cortex/workflow/shard.rs:294-310`; submit fold `cortex/workflow/fold.rs:59-66`; native consumer `net/crates/net/bindings/node/src/cortex.rs:2737-2759`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Two try_join calls on one shared adapter observe an absent reduce task before the first submit folds. Both append ordinary submits. A delayed second append can reset a reducer that started/completed in the meantime, including step, attempts and cancellation state.
- **Guard check / repair:** No multi-origin setup is needed. The existing idempotence test waits for folding before its second call (`shard.rs:487-497`) and misses the gap. Use fold-enforced submit-if-absent or serialized pending admission, retaining intentional reset behavior for ordinary resubmit.

### CTX-03 — Dropping the last adapter does not stop its materializer

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed task-owned lifetime leak.
- **Source:** `cortex/adapter.rs:394-403,845-865`; workflow public surface `cortex/workflow/adapter.rs:48-335`; native holder `net/crates/net/bindings/node/src/cortex.rs:2472-2474`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Drop the last adapter without closing its underlying file. The spawned fold task strongly retains AdapterInner while awaiting that file's tail. No adapter Drop cancels it; it retains the file/materialized state/notification sender and can continue folding writes. Watch streams do not end with final external-owner drop.
- **Guard check / repair:** Explicit close signals shutdown for Tasks/Memories; Workflow exposes neither close nor its inner adapter. This is a pending future owning state, not an ordinary Arc cycle. Separate external ownership from worker ownership and cancel on final external drop; expose usable workflow shutdown.
- **Provenance:** April 30 #133 concerns a related NetDB failure-path leak, not this normal ownership lifecycle.

### CTX-04 — Failed direct snapshot restore has already replaced the checkpoint

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed failed-restore side effect.
- **Source:** `cortex/tasks/adapter.rs:520-549`; memories counterpart `cortex/memories/adapter.rs:523-551`; protected NetDB caller `netdb/db.rs:305-313`; unprotected native caller `net/crates/net/bindings/node/src/cortex.rs:1334-1356`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A persistent direct restore receives a valid outer envelope with corrupt embedded state. It publishes the incoming checkpoint before decoding that state, then returns an error. Ordinary later opens fail on the replaced corrupt checkpoint despite a healthy destination log.
- **Guard check / repair:** Envelope version/origin checks do not validate embedded state. NetDB prevalidates both typed models, but direct typed/Node Tasks restore does not. Decode before durable publication and reuse the validated value. This is a residual publication-order boundary beyond earlier validation work.

### CTX-05 — Linking a child under another parent leaves contradictory lineage

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium; requires supported link calls for one child with distinct parents.
- **Source:** `cortex/workflow/fold.rs:115-126,141-145`; public API `cortex/workflow/adapter.rs:168-172`.
- **Trigger / impact:** link(A,C), then link(B,C). The new reverse parent overwrites A, but A's forward children list still includes C. Deleting A removes C through that stale edge, while B keeps a dangling child reference because deletion detaches only through the current reverse parent.
- **Guard check / repair:** Same-parent duplicate links are guarded; conflicting-parent links are neither rejected nor documented invalid. Reject conflict or atomically update both directions. Related subtree-deletion repairs do not repair reparenting.

### CTX-06 — Related parent IDs produce deterministically overlapping shard streams

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium numeric-ID relationship; exact algebraic collision, not probabilistic.
- **Source:** `cortex/workflow/shard.rs:28-35,249-256`.
- **Trigger:** Let C=0x9E3779B97F4A7C15 and Q=P.wrapping_add(C), both accepted parent task IDs. Shard 0 of Q and shard 1 of P use the identical pre-mix value and therefore identical task ID.
- **Impact / repair:** Fan-out resets the shared task; lineage spans groups, completion can satisfy both joins, and deleting one parent deletes the other's shard. No cross-group collision guard was established. Derive from an unambiguous parent/index encoding and define collision handling. No exact focused historical match found.

### CTX-07 — Reconstructed task-lease actor restarts below its retained generation

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium helper reconstruction concern; live scheduler use not established.
- **Source:** `cortex/workflow/lease.rs:81-96`; consuming generation gate `behavior/fold/reservation.rs:224-235`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Reconstruct TaskLease against retained/restored reservation state using the same publisher identity. Its generation starts at one; renew/release are rejected until it overtakes the old generation, even if the old deadline expired. Cross-publisher expiry takeover does not help the same publisher.
- **Repair:** Seed from retained generation or supply a durable generation owner. No seeding constructor was found. Distinct from the already logged claim TTL mismatch.

### CTX-08 — Lag catch-up ignores shutdown until the entire backlog ends

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium liveness concern; sustained backlog prerequisite.
- **Source:** `cortex/adapter.rs:858-862,883-925`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Following recoverable lag, another file user writes fast enough to keep direct catch-up behind the head. Inner catch-up loops do not check closed/shutdown state or yield. Explicit close cannot stop folding until catch-up finishes; sustained backlog can prevent that indefinitely.
- **Guard check / repair:** The biased outer select does not preempt synchronous inner work. Check cancellation/yield between bounded batches. Existing PERSIST-04/05 concern incomplete replay and lost lag recovery, not shutdown starvation.

### CTX-09 — Retrying a partially published join never repairs its missing lineage

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium append-failure recovery concern.
- **Source:** `cortex/workflow/shard.rs:301-310`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Reduce submission succeeds but the following parent-link append fails. After submission folds, retry sees the reducer and returns AlreadySubmitted without retrying the missing link. Parent deletion permanently misses that reducer.
- **Repair / boundary:** Repair ownership before reporting AlreadySubmitted or encode submit/ownership together. This does not assume cross-task transaction guarantees: the issue is retry bypassing its own unfinished work. No exact focused historical match found.

### CTX-10 — AtTick at the maximum logical time never drains

- **Severity / status:** Low boundary concern; practical priority low, source-confirmed inclusive-bound error.
- **Source:** `cortex/workflow/trigger.rs:265`; full-u64 native input `net/crates/net/bindings/node/src/cortex.rs:2901-2925`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Arm AtTick(u64::MAX), then advance world.tick to u64::MAX. saturating_add(1) stays MAX and split_off leaves that bucket in the future set, although its predicate is satisfied.
- **Repair:** Drain inclusively without overflowing the successor bound, handling maximum tick explicitly.

CortEX known overlaps: TaskLease's default metadata also selects ReservationFold's 30-second retention (`cortex/workflow/lease.rs:154-165`; `behavior/fold/reservation.rs:188-193`), expiring longer advertised leases under sweeping. This is GANG-01's same underlying TTL mismatch, now traced to another helper, not another numbered bug. GANG-04/05/06 capability-step publication/authorization/stale-release and PERSIST-04/05 replay/tail concerns remain applicable. Suffix double-application after a skipped event is explicitly documented (`cortex/adapter.rs:1000-1021`) and historically tracked May 3 #6/#7, not a new guarantee.

CortEX exclusions: Task/memory queries scan maps rather than maintaining secondary indexes. Copy-on-write preserves held reader snapshots; memory upsert preserves pin/creation time. Delete/rename/complete/retag/pin have fold consumers. Snapshot+watch subscribes before reading and documented conflation is not lost guaranteed delivery. Timestamp inclusivity/empty tag behavior are deliberate fixes. IfResult requires record-first in supported bindings; retry cancellation persists by explicit contract until delete/fresh submit. No CP resource fencing, autonomous external-worker cancellation, deferred adapter execution or automatic trigger action was inferred. No tests/builds/runtime reproductions were run.

### GOFFI-01 — Safe Go compute callback reentry can abort the process

- **Severity / status:** High availability; source-confirmed execution-context violation, application callback reentry required.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/bindings/go/compute-ffi/src/lib.rs:182-188,1213-1230,2030-2031`; `net/crates/net/sdk/src/compute.rs:1122-1133`; `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/compute/registry.rs:292-302`; `go/compute_dispatch.go:46-55,280-311`; `go/compute.go:605-620`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Daemon A's Process synchronously calls Snapshot(B), for a different daemon, or another runtime's blocking operation. Delivery entered Tokio block_on and invokes the application's Go callback on that thread. The reentered FFI operation detects an entered runtime and calls process::abort. Ordinary safe Go APIs terminate the whole process; selecting another daemon avoids confusing this with recursive host-mutex locking.
- **Guard check / repair:** Go panic recovery cannot catch abort. Runtime pins protect lifetime, not execution context; Close's deferred path is not the demonstrated failure. The synchronous callback contract does not forbid these calls. Execute callbacks on non-entered threads while retaining synchronous completion and owned arguments, as RPC already does (`rpc-ffi/src/handles.rs:237-273`).
- **Provenance:** Residual of June 18 binding hardening (`BUG_AUDIT_2026_06_18_BINDINGS.md:587-613`): abort containment is present, but supported Go callback reentry still reaches it. Not a peer-only exploit.

### GOFFI-02 — Idle Deck summary pull prevents stream Free from completing

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium shutdown-liveness concern; Free does not explicitly promise cancellation.
- **Source:** `go/deck.go:663-699`; `net/crates/net/bindings/go/deck-ffi/src/lib.rs:871-896`; unchanged-summary suppression `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/deck.rs:2476-2509`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Consume the initial summary on an idle client, then run Next(0) and Free in different goroutines. Next holds the stream read lock throughout an unbounded native poll; Free needs its write lock. Unchanged summaries are deduplicated, so periodic ceiling ticks do not end that wait. The goroutine/native call/OS thread can remain retained indefinitely.
- **Guard check / repair:** nextMu correctly prevents concurrent mutable Rust aliases but does not cancel a poll. Calling raw native free concurrently would deallocate the polled stream and is not a workaround. Signal independent close before waiting for operation quiescence, then free.
- **Provenance:** Analogous to October workflow wait/free liveness, not the repaired Deck concurrent-Next aliasing issue (`SDK_AUDIT_2026_10_11.md:963,1003`).

### GOFFI-03 — Deck watch-channel closure becomes permanent stream readiness

- **Severity / status:** Medium availability; source-confirmed closed-channel loop, exact CPU behavior scheduling-dependent.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/deck.rs:2354-2362,2409-2427,2476-2509`; sender ownership `behavior/meshos/event_loop.rs:86,593-606` under the same adapter/net directory; parent teardown `net/crates/net/bindings/go/deck-ffi/src/lib.rs:458-469`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Keep a snapshot/summary stream, free its parent Deck client, then keep polling. The stream owns its reader, so this is not a freed-client dereference. Once the supervisor sender disappears, changed() completes with error forever, but the helper erases the error and immediately rearms. Snapshot pulls repeatedly expose stale state without cadence; summary polls deduplicate that state and loop on a permanently ready source instead of EOF.
- **Guard check / repair:** Tokio cooperative yields may bound individual bursts, not remove permanent readiness. Native EOF/timeout handling exists, but core never returns None here. Terminate on closure or permanently disable the change arm if stale ceiling polling is intended; the current fallback comment does neither.
- **Provenance:** Distinct from GOFFI-02 and affects sequential C callers after teardown too. No exact focused Deck/MeshOS historical match found.

### GOFFI-04 — Failed callback-thread creation leaks allocated streaming handles

- **Severity / status:** Low/medium; source-confirmed resource-failure branch, conditional on OS thread creation failure.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/bindings/go/rpc-ffi/src/handles.rs:255-273`; `rpc-ffi/src/lib.rs:3077-3104,3190-3210,3367-3387`; equivalent `org-ffi/src/lib.rs:1582-1604,1641-1663,1714-1735`, with those latter paths under the same bindings/go directory.
- **Trigger / impact:** An admitted streaming request dispatches while OS thread/process limits or resource pressure make Builder::spawn fail. Boxes are allocated before spawn, transferred only as integer addresses, and reconstructed/freed only inside the worker. Dropping the never-run closure drops integers, not those boxes, retaining request/response channel resources on each failed dispatch.
- **Guard check / repair:** Successful worker cleanup and deliberately retained timed-out running callbacks do not cover a worker that never started. Capture RAII ownership and derive callback pointers inside the worker, or explicitly reclaim on spawn failure. Remote amplification requires admitted work plus an already-reached resource limit; no standalone exhaustion exploit is asserted.

Go/FFI exclusions: Current MeshOS v2 destruction transfers context ownership only after registration succeeds; historical UAF/double-delete was not repeated. Legacy v1 explicitly requires context lifetime until callbacks cease (`net/crates/net/include/net_meshos.h:326-385`). RPC/Org server-stream gates retire/wait before freeing; reviewed MeshDB/MCP wrappers serialize free, and iterators own their drained rows. Module-local callback deallocation is repaired. Oversized MeshDB TTL is BIND-04. No additional MeshOS pump failure was established. All eight shim crates and necessary callers were inspected; no executions/tests/builds were run.

A2A paths below are relative to `net/crates/net/`.

### A2A-01 — Acknowledged cancellation races terminal-result publication

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed non-atomic cancellation precedence.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/a2a.rs:1091-1099,1345-1354,1487-1500`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Executor completion samples cancel=false, another thread then cancels the still-in-flight entry and receives true, and completion subsequently commits Completed/Failed. The successful cancel acknowledgement is followed by an uncancelled terminal result, contrary to the implementation's rule that cancelled partial results are not promoted.
- **Guard check / repair:** Map operations individually lock, but the terminal choice uses an earlier unlocked cancellation sample. Decide cancellation precedence under the same lock that commits the terminal state. July H-5 executor cancellation selection does not make publication atomic.

### A2A-02 — Configured admission does not observe an accepted cancellation

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed pre-launch cancellation gap.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/mesh_a2a.rs:1791-1804,1932-1934,2055-2059`; `sdk/src/a2a.rs:1085-1092,1345-1354`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A configured submission waits in application preflight/admission with a Requested registry entry. cancel_task returns true, but neither wait checks that token. It can remain Requested indefinitely, or proceed to redemption/launch. On eventual launch the executor is polled before the already-ready cancellation branch, allowing first-poll effects before Cancelled.
- **Guard check / repair:** Registry executor cancellation starts only after admission; RPC cancellation is separate and unary supervision does not forcibly stop the handler. Make the admission window cancellation-aware, define post-redemption reconciliation, and never launch a cancelled reservation. Distinct from the older Python executor coroutine guard.

### A2A-03 — Failed decision-hold cleanup strands durable admission capacity

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed error/unwind cleanup gap.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/mesh_a2a.rs:1791-1795`; `sdk/src/a2a_journal.rs:621-626,1425-1433,1876-1881`; RPC panic containment `src/adapter/net/cortex/rpc.rs:2202-2205`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A normal refusal follows open_decision, but close_decision's write fails (e.g. transient disk exhaustion), or application preflight/gate panics after opening the hold. Cleanup errors are discarded and no owner clears deciding=true. The reservation remains capacity-holding beyond expiry and exempt from pruning; unrelated task IDs can receive Busy until same-ID retry or journal reopen.
- **Guard check / repair:** Ticket Drop releases the registry reservation, not the durable hold; contained RPC panics need not restart the process. Own cleanup across cancellation/unwind and retry/surface release failures. October terminal-write recovery concerns another state/path and does not repair this hold.

### A2A-04 — Stale preflight revocation can reconcile a replacement reservation

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium; supported concurrent operator-maintenance interleaving.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/mesh_a2a.rs:2104-2117`; key-only transition `sdk/src/a2a_journal.rs:1508-1558`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A's preflight waits. Operator operations advance A to unresolved, resolve/forget it, and prepare replacement B under the same owner/task ID. A later refuses. revoked() transitions by key plus old state tag; if B shares that tag it revokes B, attaches A's claimed payment information and clears B's hold.
- **Guard check / repair:** Decision holds block pruning, not explicit operator resolution. Identity safeguards on Paid-target writes do not protect this Reconcile write. Re-present A's admission identity in a generation-bound reconciliation transition. Ordinary duplicate submissions remain registry-serialized.

### A2A-05 — Durable status-read errors are reported as successful absence

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed error-to-null conversion.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/mesh_a2a.rs:2292-2307`.
- **Trigger / impact:** After restart or registry eviction, journal lookup encounters I/O failure/corruption. The fallback accepts only Ok(Some(...)) and silently ignores errors, returning successful null even if paid/launched work exists. Rust/Python/Node status callers cannot distinguish absence from unreadable durable evidence.
- **Guard check / repair:** Store exposes distinct Io/Corrupt failures and owner isolation is correct. Propagate provider/read failure; reserve null for a successful absent lookup.

### A2A-06 — Free-service re-serving resets admission accounting while tasks remain alive

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium lifecycle/API composition; production reconfiguration caller not established.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/mesh_a2a.rs:2361-2379,2552,2588-2598`; capacity accounting `sdk/src/a2a_journal.rs:1293-1300`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Drop an all-free serving handle while tasks continue, then serve again with the same registry and another journal-free configuration. New serving creates an empty admission store; old running tasks remain in the registry/old hook store and do not count against the new ceiling. More work than max_in_flight can be admitted.
- **Repair / boundary:** Preserve the store across serving generations or reconcile/reject outstanding supplied-registry work. Service-handle Drop unregisters services, not launched tasks.

### A2A-07 — Successful redemption evidence is omitted after payer mismatch

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium reconciliation concern; no authorization bypass claimed.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/mesh_a2a.rs:2159-2203`; unresolved filtering `sdk/src/a2a_journal.rs:1930-1936`.
- **Trigger / impact:** An organization-admitted task presents reservation-bound payment whose verified payer differs from the admitted caller. Gate successfully grants redemption, then the server correctly rejects the principal mismatch but records only an attempt note. Returned financial evidence is never recorded as Paid/Reconcile; the reservation is absent from the operator unresolved queue and remains prunable.
- **Guard check / repair:** Rejecting the mismatched principal is correct, and external payment evidence can still exist. Enforce expected payer before consuming redemption or durably retain evidence in identity-bound reconciliation.

### A2A-08 — Post-payment journal failure advertises safe re-quoting

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium recovery-advice defect; built-in paid caller is protected.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/mesh_a2a.rs:1242-1255,2200-2219`; guarded consumer `payments/src/flow/a2a.rs:3164-3183`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Redemption succeeds, then Paid persistence or launch claim fails. The refusal advertises safe_to_requote=true despite the existing payment and same-proof retry requirement. A raw consumer following that flag can initiate another purchase.
- **Guard check / repair:** A2aCallerFlow conservatively retains the paid proof rather than re-quoting; no production consumer automatically following the flag was established. Advertise same-proof retry, distinguishing genuinely pre-payment failures if re-quoting is supported.

### A2A-09 — Terminal journal APIs accept a nonterminal task disposition

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium public/admin argument-validation concern.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/a2a_journal.rs:1710-1731,1799,1840,1846-1854`.
- **Trigger / impact:** resolve, resolve_exact or record_terminal receives Requested/Accepted/Running. The shared machine stores AdmissionState::Terminal containing that nonterminal TaskState. Capacity/unresolved accounting considers it finished while status says in progress; retention may delete it.
- **Guard check / repair:** Source-state checks exist, but destination is_terminal validation does not. Ordinary terminal hooks supply valid states; no existing bad production caller was found. Reject nonterminal dispositions at the shared boundary before changing accounting.

A2A known residual: Terminal-write retries stop after about 20.5 seconds (`sdk/src/mesh_a2a.rs:2581-2585,2915-2949`); a longer outage still leaves Launched capacity until operator resolution, related to the October report. Crash-time Interrupted/outcome_unknown and no automatic relaunch are deliberate.

A2A exclusions: Free/configured status/cancel use authenticated owner keys; organization mode uses verified admitted identity. No supported ordinary stale AdmissionTicket replacement schedule was established. Ambiguous launch claims deliberately prevent replay; same-proof Paid resubmission before launch is intended. Free task-ID reuse after forgetting is allowed, not permanent exactly-once storage. This API polls status, not a promised task watch/reconnect stream. No tests/builds/runtime reproductions were run.

Wire locations below use full paths for the core mesh and wire modules; references to leaf paths are relative to `net/crates/net/`.

### WIRE-01 — Disabling byte backpressure also suppresses reliable acknowledgements

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed supported zero-window configuration failure.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/wire/src/session.rs:663-684,2417-2420,2498-2540`; `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:33030-33046`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Explicitly open the same reliable stream with window_bytes=0 on both native peers, then deliver a packet without loss. Receive byte accounting returns None and the duplicate path also requires nonzero window, so no cumulative ACK is emitted. Periodic reports only cover gaps. Sender retransmits already delivered data until retry exhaustion/reset.
- **Guard check / repair:** Implicit receive streams default to 65,536 bytes, so sender-only window=0 does not establish the failure. Separate required reliable acknowledgements from optional byte-credit accounting.

### WIRE-02 — Reliable batch producers never register recovery descriptors

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed unrecoverable loss in native batch paths.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:34646-34693,34730-34798,53161-53179`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Set default_reliable=true and send through send_to_peer_node, send_routed or Adapter::on_batch. Lose one datagram. These producers consume sequences and flag packets RELIABLE without retaining retransmit descriptors; neither NACK nor RTO can reconstruct loss. Later packets remain held behind the gap, and the untracked packet cannot itself exhaust retries to signal failure.
- **Repair / provenance:** Route these producers through reliable admission/descriptor registration, including credit/boundary ownership. Both batch flush sites and the real adapter caller were traced. Historical publish-flag/stale-ciphertext fixes do not cover these batch producers.

### WIRE-03 — Channel publish never states its fire-and-forget/reliable promotion boundary

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed reliable-delivery stall.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:45122-45130,45153-45164,45241-45247,45280-45293`; receiver `32952-32984`; `net/crates/net/wire/src/session.rs:2635-2688`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Lose fire-and-forget sequence zero on a channel, then publish reliable sequence one on that channel/session. Publish allocates through byte-credit admission rather than try_admit_stream_send, and emits RELIABLE without MODE_BOUNDARY. Receiver conservatively retains gap zero, for which no descriptor exists, blocking later reliable delivery.
- **Guard check / repair:** Reliable publication now registers descriptors and routing stamps; neither states the promotion split. Allocate/stamp promotion through shared authoritative reliable admission. Distinct from RPC-05's stream-producer claim race.

### WIRE-04 — Channel publish can weaken an already reliable stream

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed unretransmittable gap inside the reliable region.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:44911,45126,45241-45247,50490-50492`; receiver `32998-33025`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Publish reliable sequence zero, fire-and-forget sequence one, then reliable sequence two on the same channel stream. Lose sequence one. Publish flags/descriptors depend only on the current call's reliable argument, while the receiver's stream contract is already reliable and cannot skip that missing position.
- **Guard check / repair:** Serialized send_on_stream inherits established reliability, but this separate publish path does not. Resolve effective reliability at shared admission.
- **Earlier-scope correction:** Any earlier broad “current sends inherit promoted reliability” rejection applies to serialized stream sends, not this publication implementation; WIRE-05 additionally qualifies its concurrent case.

### WIRE-05 — Concurrent stream promotion can race a stale weaker send decision

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed concurrent inheritance gap.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:50031-50034,50130-50139,50361-50368`; `net/crates/net/wire/src/session.rs:970-981`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Fire-and-forget A samples tx_promoted=false and pauses. Reliable B sends the boundary. A then allocates a later sequence using its previously computed weaker flags; lose A's packet. It has no descriptor but lies inside the reliable region, stranding later delivery.
- **Guard check / repair:** Sequence/session/epoch admission does not upgrade the stale reliable=false argument. Return effective mode/flags from the same synchronized admission that allocates the sequence and establishes promotion. Unlike RPC-05, this introduces a later untracked weaker packet rather than losing an earlier genuinely reliable boundary.

### WIRE-06 — Refused first reliable send consumes its persistent boundary claim

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed returned-backpressure promotion failure.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:50349,50389-50404,50430-50445`; `net/crates/net/wire/src/session.rs:2735-2739,3080-3089`.
- **Trigger / impact:** After a lost fire-and-forget prefix, first reliable admission claims its boundary, but RTC/scheduler refuses submission. Sequence rollback succeeds and the call returns Backpressure. A new retry reuses that sequence without MODE_BOUNDARY: persistent one-shot claim was consumed, while claimed_boundary belonged only to the failed invocation. Receiver still waits for the unrecoverable prefix.
- **Guard check / repair:** Internal retries retain the local claim; returning before the first commit loses it. Derive restamping from persistent sequence/boundary ownership rather than which invocation won the initial claim. Distinct from RPC-05's concurrent ordering.

### WIRE-07 — Cancellation or failed rollback leaves reserved reliable sequences ownerless

- **Severity / status:** High; known source-confirmed recovery-ownership residual.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/wire/src/session.rs:1160-1174,3080-3089`; `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:50411-50427,50438-50451`; pending submission `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/transport.rs:525-537`.
- **Trigger:** Cancel an unscheduled send after sequence reservation while transport submission is pending; or let A's send fail after concurrent B has reserved the next sequence, making A's rollback CAS fail.
- **Impact:** No retransmit descriptor owns A's sequence. Cancellation refunds bytes only, and error paths ignore rollback failure. Later reliable delivery blocks behind a gap neither NACK nor timeout can recover.
- **Guard check / repair:** Epoch fencing and failed CAS correctly avoid corrupting B's lifetime/sequence, but do not settle A's abandoned position. Give reliable allocations cancellation-safe ownership and a terminal disposition if rollback is impossible.
- **Provenance:** Residual of June 18 finding 19. The serialized returned-backpressure rollback case is repaired, not these cancellation/concurrency cases.

### WIRE-08 — Fragment-group cancellation refunds already accepted prefix credit

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed partial-send accounting and sequence-ownership gap.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:50592-50624,50658-50717`; `net/crates/net/wire/src/session.rs:1160-1174`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Admit a two-piece group, accept the head, then hit tail pressure/backoff. Cancel the future or let the stall helper return via ?. The active group guard refunds the whole group, including accepted head bytes; if its grant already arrived, credit can exceed the intended window. Unsent reserved positions also remain without descriptors and can block later reliable delivery.
- **Guard check / repair:** Explicit Err handling settles partial bytes/reclaims tail sequences; cancellation and the helper's ? bypass it. Track accepted prefix and unsent tail in cancellation-safe group ownership on every exit. Contiguous sequence reservation fixes interleaving, not partial-group lifetime.

### WIRE-09 — Reused packet builders retain a previous channel's routing hash

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed unrelated-send misrouting.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/wire/src/pool.rs:134-135,232,331,840-903`; stamping `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:45219`; ordinary send `50411-50414`; receive routing `32537-32538,32634-32710`.
- **Trigger / impact:** On one worker, publish channel C, return its builder, then reuse it for ordinary send_on_stream to the same session. Pool acquire/release resets origin but not channel_hash. If C has a registered dispatcher, it consumes/drops the ordinary packet instead of the intended stream/shard sink.
- **Guard check / repair:** Fragment metadata is one-shot; retransmit rebuild with no routing stamp explicitly clears channel hash, making first-send/retransmit routing inconsistent. Reset per-packet routing at pool boundaries or require every producer to stamp it. No cross-peer authorization bypass is claimed.

### WIRE-10 — Direct fragment delivery re-resolves the successor's stream epoch

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed receive-lifetime contamination with native reassembly enabled.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:32443-32455,41709-41728,49690-49713`; disposal `477-492`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A fragment is accepted on epoch E; its stream closes/reopens as E+1 before reassembly. Direct delivery discards the accepting StreamLifetime and re-reads the current epoch, tagging the old piece E+1. Its later expiry then passes live-epoch disposal checks and resets the successor's receive state, potentially discarding legitimate held data/groups.
- **Guard check / repair:** Session retirement is fenced; stream replacement is not carried through this path. Reorder-held packets already preserve the accepting epoch. Carry/validate the accepting lifetime into direct fragment admission. Residual adjacent to existing NR4/R4-7 ownership fixes, not a claim those guards are absent.

### WIRE-11 — Native reliable reassembly abandonment is neither surfaced nor latched terminal

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed native receive-half disposition gap with reassembly enabled.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/rtc/fragment.rs:170,1134-1138,1515-1575`; `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:441-504`; `net/crates/net/wire/src/session.rs:1526-1547`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Receive/ACK a reliable fragment head, then delay the tail past the two-second group TTL but within sender retry horizon. Expiry disposal resets receive cursors/logs without notifying the application or latching that half closed. The sender may have discarded the acknowledged head; later high sequences are held behind a zero frontier nobody can refill.
- **Guard check / repair:** Reliable-only/exact-epoch checks exist. Avoiding the reverse-direction StreamReset is deliberate, so blindly adding that message would not safely repair it. Add a receive-half terminal latch and consumer failure notification without destroying reverse progress. Leaf already emits/latches StreamFailed (`leaf/src/node.rs:3656-3674`).
- **Provenance:** Abandonment-record/fence repairs are present; this is the remaining consumer disposition.

### WIRE-12 — Native send admission remains open after retransmit exhaustion

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed missing failed-send lifetime latch.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/wire/src/reliability.rs:1378-1398`; `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:33491-33529,50062-50116`; admission `net/crates/net/wire/src/session.rs:970-985`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Exhaust a packet's retries; the retransmit loop takes the failure flag and emits StreamReset. Continue sending through the same native handle.
