Taking the flag does not retire/latch the lifetime; new sends can succeed with continuing sequence numbers while receiver reset its frontier to zero and cannot recover acknowledged predecessors.
- **Guard check / repair:** abandoned_seq persists but admission does not consult it. Persist failed-send state and reject every producer until explicit synchronized recovery. Leaf's send_failed already rejects this continuation (`leaf/src/node.rs:1815-1875`).

### WIRE-13 — Empty-queue cleanup erases configured scheduler weights

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed scheduling configuration loss.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:49636-49638`; `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/router.rs:220-228,404-423,440-446,602-610`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Open a weight-four scheduled stream; other traffic triggers cleanup while it is idle, even before its first send. Cleanup deletes the sole queue-held weight. The stream remains open, but its next enqueue recreates a weight-one queue and silently loses its configured service share.
- **Guard check / repair:** Arc-count protection prevents deleting an enqueue's live queue, not loss after legitimate idle deletion. Retain authoritative weight separately or reapply it on queue recreation. Historical RR-index/active-snapshot race fixes are present.

### WIRE-14 — Multi-packet sends check congestion only once per call

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium congestion-control concern; control-flow bypass confirmed, throughput/fairness impact not measured.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:50083-50106`; per-packet admission `net/crates/net/wire/src/session.rs:970-981`.
- **Trigger / impact:** After loss reduces cwnd to two, a multi-packet call begins with one pending packet and passes can_send once. It can then emit many packets while byte/descriptor headroom permits, exceeding the stated in-flight congestion bound.
- **Repair / boundary:** Reserve/check per-packet congestion admission without enabling unsafe replay of already committed prefixes. Descriptor capacity and byte windows are distinct guards, not substitutes for cwnd.

Wire known overlap: Serialized channel publish reaches RPC-06's descriptor eviction (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:45122-45295`; `net/crates/net/wire/src/reliability.rs:1156-1158`). It performs byte admission without descriptor/congestion headroom: 129 tiny reliable publications before feedback can evict the first unacknowledged descriptor from the default 128-slot state while using far less than 64 KiB. This is additional serialized caller reachability for an existing root cause, not another numbered finding.

Wire exclusions: RPC-04/05 remain close/reopen and first-boundary concerns and were not recounted. Fresh-counter retransmission and fragment/routing stamp restoration are present. Repeated NACK budget collapse is guarded by recover-point/backed-off RTO behavior. Look-ahead/capacity rejection was not falsely acknowledged. Scheduler cloned-queue deletion is Arc-count guarded. Scheduler-enqueue retransmit-clock timing (T-4) and acknowledged stragglers beyond bounded look-ahead are documented residuals. Leaf stamp tables restore inspected subprotocol metadata; no supported extra silent route failure was established. No compatible native non-event-plane fragmented control producer was established from the leaf's different signed signalling codec. No tests/builds/runtime reproductions were run.

## Sixth audit pass

Sixth pass complete: four subagents audited delegation/consent, organization routing, browser-leaf lifecycle and the TypeScript SDK; the parent traced migration ownership. All returned concerns are recorded below: 27 added numbered entries (MIG 4, DELEGATION 1, CONSENT 4, TSDK 7, OLB 2, LEAF 9), plus cross-referenced historical/duplicate residuals without additional numbering. Source-confirmed behavior, inferred impact, conditional configurations and latent/unproven consumer paths are distinguished. No production edits, builds/tests, browser/network executions or runtime reproductions.

### MIG-01 — Retained migration handles can act on a later operation for the same daemon

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium operation-identity concern; source-confirmed origin-only lookup, not a remote authorization bypass.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk/src/compute.rs:1460-1465,1522-1528,2023-2046,2192-2196,2323-2345`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Keep a clone of migration A's handle after A finishes, then start migration B for that daemon. A's phase/wait/cancel methods resolve only origin_hash, with no operation generation. Calling A.cancel can abort B and notify A's old source/target; A's timed wait can likewise abort B. Observation can report B's state under A's original endpoints.
- **Boundary / repair:** Both handles belong to a trusted local runtime; this is not gaining authority through arbitrary origin_hash editing. Bind handles and all status/failure/abort mutations to a stable operation identity, rejecting stale owners rather than acting on a successor. The handle is documented as belonging to an in-flight migration, but no explicit stale-handle contract was established, so API-contract qualification is retained.

### MIG-02 — One waiter consumes a retryable failure and another reports success

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed false completion for supported cloned handles.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk/src/compute.rs:2094-2123,2153-2176,2284-2298`.
- **Trigger:** Two clones wait when a NotReady failure removes the orchestrator record. Waiter A removes the retryable failure and enters its 500-ms-or-longer retry backoff. B polls before A reinitiates.
- **Impact:** B sees no record and no failure and returns Ok although the migration failed and retry has not started. With retries explicitly disabled, A instead returns the real error while B still returns success.
- **Guard check / repair:** Terminal failures are retained for every waiter, but retryable failures are intentionally consumed to elect one retry owner. That election needs shared retry-in-progress/attempt outcome state so nonowners wait rather than interpret absence as success. Preserve outcomes per operation and coordinate retry ownership separately.
- **Provenance:** Related to the repaired October cancel/terminal-clone reporting issue; consuming retryable failure leaves a distinct false-success window.

### MIG-03 — Timed wait abort bypasses the failure evidence used by other waiters

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed timeout sibling of a repaired cancellation bug.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk/src/compute.rs:2153-2176,2189-2199`; compare the repaired atomic cancel path `2323-2334`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Clone A reaches wait_with_timeout and directly aborts the orchestrator, returning its timeout error. It never records recent_failures. Clone B's plain wait then sees no record/no reason and returns Ok for the same aborted operation.
- **Guard check / repair:** The comment exempting synchronous timeout because that caller returns Err does not cover another public clone. Publish timeout outcome atomically with abort, using the same per-operation outcome mechanism as cancellation, so every observer receives failure.
- **Provenance:** October's cancel fix is present; its general local-abort evidence requirement was not applied to this timeout branch.

### MIG-04 — Retry sleep can outlive both migration deadlines and still launch work

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed retry-budget enforcement gap.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk/src/compute.rs:1984-1994,2006-2014,2075-2077,2091-2122,2211-2269`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A NotReady outcome arrives shortly before retry_not_ready or wait_with_timeout's deadline. The loop checks budgets before sleeping the full 500-ms-to-16-second backoff, then reinitiates/sends without another deadline check. A retry begins after its budget expired; elapsed time can exceed the advertised retry cap by a backoff plus setup/send time.
- **Guard check / repair:** Attempt polling checks overall timeout only after the new attempt is initiated. Clamp sleep to the remaining budgets and recheck before beginning any retry. This is separate from the ordinary RetryPolicy API, which explicitly documents deadline-plus-backoff semantics.

Parent migration exclusions: Existing cancel failure preservation and retry send/seal cleanup are present. The local snapshot sealing helper itself aborts on missing prerequisites/rechunk failure (`net/crates/net/sdk/src/compute.rs:1651-1665`), so its caller's ? was not incorrectly reported as a leaked initial record. No runtime reproduction was run.

### DELEGATION-01 — Signed invokes have no provider audience binding

- **Severity / status:** Conditional high; missing signed binding source-confirmed, cross-provider exploitation is [INFERENCE], not reproduced.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/adapters/mcp/src/wrap/delegation.rs:157-165,255-266,293-340`; separate destination `serve/mesh_gateway.rs:224-241`; delegated acceptance `wrap/invoke.rs:326-355`; provider-independent service naming `wrap/descriptor.rs:199-201,270-271`, with shorter paths under the same adapters/mcp/src directory.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** A legitimate leaf invokes provider A. Compromised A forwards the unchanged signed body/headers to reachable B exposing the same service and trusting the same delegation root. The challenge signs domain/service/arguments/timestamp/nonce but not destination; B's independent replay cache has not seen that nonce. B can execute side effects attributed to the leaf even though it signed the request for A.
- **Guard check / repair:** Root/leaf/body/freshness/revocation and same-gate replay checks remain effective. Independent organization/network/payment/provider policy may deny B; this is not anonymous mesh-wide access. Bind a versioned intended provider/audience into the signature and verify locally.
- **Provenance:** Distinct from MCP-01 opt-in equivalence/failover; forwarding needs no equivalence opt-in. July Hermes same-gate replay analysis did not establish cross-provider audience binding.

### CONSENT-01 — Cancelled pin mutation can publish after a later rejection

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-backed cancellation-ownership gap, stale-approval schedule is [INFERENCE].
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk/src/pins.rs:279-320`; cancellable native consumer `net/crates/net/bindings/python/src/consent.rs:382-390`; locked Tokio versions `net/crates/net/Cargo.lock:4387-4388,5928-5929`.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** While X is approved, an async request for another pending pin Y loads that snapshot and reaches rename. Cancellation releases the advisory guard while the already-started blocking filesystem operation can continue. A separate CLI process rejects X and commits. The old rename can finish later and reinstate its older snapshot with X approved. Different process-specific temporary names preserve both rename sources.
- **Guard check / repair:** Requests do not self-approve; the stale complete snapshot is the admission path. Completed mutations serialize and replacement is atomic, but transaction ownership is not cancellation-safe. Retain the lock until all started filesystem commit work finishes, even if the original waiter stops. Tokio 1.54.1 rename delegates through asyncify/spawn_blocking; started blocking work is not aborted by dropping its await.
- **Provenance:** July F7 concerned blocking-pool starvation. This violates the pin store's explicit stale-approval guarantee; it is a distinct implementation/state boundary from PAY-04, though both need cancellation-safe filesystem ownership.

### CONSENT-02 — Successful pin rejection does not durably commit its directory entry

- **Severity / status:** Medium, POSIX/filesystem-dependent; missing barrier confirmed, crash recovery outcome is [INFERENCE].
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk/src/pins.rs:279-298`; success acknowledgement `net/crates/net/cli/src/commands/mcp.rs:342-356`; adjacent durable convention `net/crates/net/sdk/src/revocation.rs:306-324`.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** Reject an approved capability, receive success, then lose power before rename's directory update persists. On affected POSIX filesystems, recovery can retain the older approved store and later invocations are again admitted.
- **Guard check / repair:** Temporary-file fsync protects contents and rename protects atomic visibility; neither durably publishes the directory update. Sync the containing directory and define durability-failure acknowledgement. No cancellation/concurrent writer is needed, unlike CONSENT-01.
- **Provenance:** Historical/release documentation calls this durable (`net/crates/net/docs/releases/RELEASE_v0.31_HOLD_THE_LINE.md:84,132`); no exact prior pin-store finding was located. Same filesystem principle as PAY-05, separate store implementation.

### CONSENT-03 — Cold-start revocation-load failure uses an empty registry

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium availability-policy residual; behavior confirmed, admission outcome is [INFERENCE].
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/cli/src/commands/wrap.rs:322-341`; `net/crates/net/adapters/mcp/src/wrap/delegation.rs:280-290`.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** Restart with a configured revocation store unreadable/corrupt. CLI creates a fresh empty registry; gate refresh logs read/parse failures and keeps that empty state. A revoked but otherwise valid chain can pass until loading succeeds.
- **Guard check / repair:** Warm registries correctly retain learned floors. July 5 explicitly accepted last-known-floor behavior; this records its cold-start consequence, not a novel rollback mechanism. Require a successful initial load before delegated admission if fail-closed startup is intended.

### CONSENT-04 — Pin rejection does not fence an invocation using an earlier snapshot

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium in-flight revocation contract concern; not an established authorization defect.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/bindings/node/src/capability_gateway.rs:246-250`; `net/crates/net/bindings/python/src/capability_gateway.rs:301-304`; `net/crates/net/adapters/mcp/src/serve/gated.rs:117-150,204`.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** A binding loads approved pins, then awaits describe or payment. Approval is removed during that wait; invocation still uses the earlier snapshot and proceeds.
- **Qualification / repair:** Per-invocation loading avoids a persistent cached approval, not this ordering. Intended linearization/in-flight revocation semantics are unspecified. Clarify them before adding stronger generation fencing/rechecking; independent remote authorization remains authoritative.

Consent/delegation known compatibility residual: Legacy noncanonical pin keys are not migrated. Load preserves raw keys (`net/crates/net/sdk/src/pins.rs:211`), lookup/removal uses canonical display strings (`346-352`), and listing canonicalizes (`378-383`). An old 0x2a/echo record can display as 42/echo yet fail canonical lookup/removal [INFERENCE]. Current constructors canonicalize correctly. This is July bridge F11's existing root, recorded here without another numbered entry.

Consent/delegation exclusions: Core token verification enforces root/leaf binding, signer continuity, every link's action/channel/nested validity/depth/generation and revoked subjects; decoding rejects empty/oversized/trailing chains (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/identity/token.rs`). Wire credential “none” remains gated; pending pin requests do not self-approve, and ordinary persistence/read failures grant no new approval. Short-lived subagent leaves/no per-token CRL, standing capability approvals and attribution versus provider authorization are deliberate boundaries. No runtime reproduction was run.

TypeScript SDK locations below are relative to `net/crates/net/`; canonical Rust behavior is cited where cross-language parity matters.

### TSDK-01 — RedEX sync/close discard native asynchronous completion

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed lost durability/error ownership.
- **Source:** `sdk-ts/src/cortex.ts:461-475`; asynchronous native methods `bindings/node/src/cortex.rs:1052-1057,1069-1077`; persistence consumer `sdk-ts/src/store-persist.ts:127-130`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Flush a disk-backed snapshot using default sync=true, then shut down/reopen. SDK sync/close return void and discard native promises, so even await file.sync()/close() waits only for undefined. persistStore marks saved/increments count before fsync completes; rejected async errors bypass synchronous classification and onError, potentially becoming unhandled rejections. Native close may still be running when reopening begins.
- **Guard check / repair:** Heap files need no disk barrier; completed native operations remain correct. Return/await native promises end-to-end and advance saved bookkeeping only after requested durability succeeds.

### TSDK-02 — Axis predicates inspect only the first value under a key

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed SDK-local false negatives dependent on tag order.
- **Source:** `sdk-ts/src/capability-enhancements.ts:1182-1197`; canonical existential matching `src/adapter/net/behavior/predicate.rs:1246-1284,1970-1980`; permitted multi-values `src/adapter/net/behavior/capability.rs:1057-1105`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Advertise both software.runtime.python=3.10 and =3.12, then test equality to 3.12. axisTagValue returns only 3.10; equality/numeric/semver/prefix/substring leaves can reject a real match, propagating to evaluation traces/debug aggregation.
- **Guard check / repair:** Single-valued keys work; no native routing/auth bypass is claimed. Evaluate all matching value tags existentially. Distinct from historical presence-only-as-empty-value N-1.

### TSDK-03 — Numeric predicates accept JavaScript-only integer literal syntax

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed parser parity false positives.
- **Source:** `sdk-ts/src/capability-enhancements.ts:1220-1236,1271-1288,1341-1350`; canonical `src/adapter/net/behavior/predicate.rs:1247-1255,1294-1298`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Metadata capacity='0x100' is tested against numeric-at-least 100. Number() accepts it as 256 while Rust f64 parsing rejects it. Binary/octal forms likewise drift; tag numeric predicates share the parser.
- **Guard check / repair:** Whitespace/special-value checks do not exclude these literals. Enforce canonical decimal/exponent grammar, retaining supported infinity/NaN behavior. Related to historical N-2; rejecting 0x1p3 does not test accepted JavaScript 0x100. Historical Go N-14 is another implementation.

### TSDK-04 — Semver component conversion loses canonical u64 precision

- **Severity / status:** Medium edge case; source-confirmed lossless-integer mismatch.
- **Source:** `sdk-ts/src/capability-enhancements.ts:1126-1139,1289-1311`; canonical `src/adapter/net/behavior/predicate.rs:1989-2012`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Compare version 9007199254740992.0.0 against at-least 9007199254740993.0.0. JavaScript rounds both majors to the same number and falsely accepts. Finite components beyond u64 maximum are also accepted rather than rejected.
- **Repair / boundary:** Parse components losslessly with canonical u64 bounds. Ordinary small versions are unaffected; this is not intentional f64 numeric-predicate rounding.

### TSDK-05 — Empty typed-axis values escape schema errors as legacy warnings

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed validation parity defect.
- **Source:** `sdk-ts/src/capability-enhancements.ts:189-193`; `sdk-ts/src/capability-schema.ts:413-430,479-480`; canonical `src/adapter/net/behavior/tag.rs:426-438` and `schema.rs:574-589,720-735`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Validate hardware.cpu_cores= before announcement. TypeScript reclassifies the empty axis value as legacy, emits only a warning, and isReportValid returns true. Rust retains typed-axis classification and returns a numeric type error.
- **Guard check / repair:** isReportClean rejects the warning, but the public errors-only validity helper does not. Mirror canonical tag classification so malformed typed values reach schema validation. Separate from repaired UTF-8 sizing/indexed-key bounds.

### TSDK-06 — Metadata redaction still misses supported key/value shapes

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed known unrepaired TypeScript residual.
- **Source:** `sdk-ts/src/capability-enhancements.ts:1477-1478,1640-1653,1685-1723`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Generate a report for metadataEquals('credential=name','secret'), then request redaction of that key. The regex forbids '=' in the captured key and leaves the secret intact. Equality values containing line breaks also escape because '.' is not multiline, even with an ordinary key.
- **Guard check / repair:** Simple single-line inputs work. Match supplied keys literally against anchored label interiors instead of assuming delimiter-free key/value grammar.
- **Provenance:** Historical CR-19 (`docs/internal/misc/CODE_REVIEW_2026_05_10_CAPABILITY_SYSTEM_2.md:243-249`) records this root. C/Python repairs exist (`src/ffi/predicate_debug.rs:352-415`; `sdk-py/src/net_sdk/capability.py:1556-1604`); TypeScript remains affected. Not claimed as a wholly new root.

### TSDK-07 — Failed final persistence append irrevocably disables retries

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium close-contract concern.
- **Source:** `sdk-ts/src/store-persist.ts:146-154`; synchronous native append error `bindings/node/src/cortex.rs:962-968`.
- **Trigger / impact:** close marks closed and removes its timer before final flush. If append fails (e.g. disk full), subsequent close silently succeeds and flush returns false without retrying the unsaved state.
- **Qualification / repair:** This does not depend on TSDK-01's asynchronous fsync problem. Preserve retryability of an unsuccessful final save, or explicitly define irrevocable-close semantics; that policy is not currently specified.

TypeScript exclusions: Current cursor advancement, channel-filter intersection, handler isolation and externally closeable watches address inspected historical issues. Post-stop buffered delivery is SDK-02. Store stream retention avoids asymmetric epoch resets deliberately. Missing RTT fails closed; stale disabled-filter comments are documentation-only. Identity generation/projection concerns overlap existing consistency history. Restore validates identity/version and falls back after JSON/validator rejection. No additional aggregation/peer-ID/watch-forwarding failure was established. No runtime reproduction was run.

### OLB-01 — Provider selection treats a retained identity pin as live direct reachability

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed selection defect, concrete eviction schedule requires opt-in WebRTC.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk/src/org/call.rs:528-549,1266-1268,1579-1583`; pin accessor `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:15575-15579`; endpoint eviction `1333-1374`, RTC close `29156-29175`; final pin validation `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh_rpc.rs:9115-9138`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Authorized providers A and B advertise one capability, with A ordered first and both pinned. A's RTC endpoint closes; its live peer/session is evicted while unexpired discovery and identity pin remain. Unsensed selection marks A direct from the pin and calls it once rather than using still-connected B. Calls can fail/timeout repeatedly until cleanup/reconnect.
- **Guard check / repair:** Exact-provider proofs and admission remain intact. SDK requires default-enabled cortex; this specific cleanup window additionally needs webrtc, not every UDP failure path. Use coherent live session/directness information while retaining pin identity checks (`mesh.rs:10545-10623,15610-15615`). Sensing is unnecessary.
- **Provenance:** PART-01/03 concern cleanup fencing/detector retention; this is identity-pin/reachability conflation in selection.

### OLB-02 — A newer lock-free warm hit can be overtaken by an older demand mutation

- **Severity / status:** Qualified latent medium; no production OrgRoutingState consumer, no current send/auth failure established.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/org_routing_state.rs:654-660,690-699,723-725,790-816`; neighboring tests `org_routing_state_tests.rs:1825-2150`.
- **Trigger:** Owner-only route is cached at revision 3. Revision 6 adds a DISCOVER audience and revision 9 removes it. A carries revision 6, holds mutate and passes the high-water check, then pauses. B advances high-water to 9 and returns Warm from the still-correct Owner-only entry without acquiring mutate. A resumes and publishes Owner plus the removed audience from revision 6.
- **Impact / repair:** Retained demand regresses after the newer lookup returns, consuming obsolete handles/slots until another current lookup repairs it. Installed-grant checks prevent the removed audience yielding usable providers. Coordinate snapshot-order decisions with demand/index publication while preserving warmed-read goals.
- **Provenance:** Existing stalled-snapshot tests cover older callers arriving after newer publication, not a newer lock-free hit during older mutation. Adjacent to boundary-design §4.4, not a live privilege-escalation claim.

Organization-routing exclusions: No warmed production sender/load-counter/permit implementation exists here; their documented phase boundary was not called a missing cancellation or load-balancing defect. Current SDK calls use the cold planner independently of actor health. Successful snapshot comparison is the accepted proof-construction linearization point and remote admission remains authoritative. Cold planning under poison is intentionally witnessed. Expiry boundaries, actor restart fencing, wake arming, bounded quanta, slot incarnations, conditional invalidation and commit-pin protection yielded no other supported defect. No runtime reproduction was run.

Leaf lifecycle locations below are relative to `net/crates/net/leaf/src/`; TypeScript browser paths are relative to `net/crates/net/`. None of these findings requires the explicitly unsupported store-over-openSession composition.

### LEAF-01 — Identity-specific leader locks share one persisted generation counter

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed cross-identity false supersession.
- **Source:** `leader_session.rs:473-487,1387-1398`; identity election scope `leader.rs:94-95`; database-global key/read/write/fence `storage.rs:100,273-319`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Open two sessions on the same origin/default database with distinct supplied entity identities. Their identity-specific locks allow separate leaders, but both allocate from the same “generation” record. The second increment makes the first leader's next guard_lease mistake it for a successor, retire its healthy node and refuse work.
- **Guard check / repair:** Web Locks protect duplicate holders of one identity, not this shared counter. Separate dbName avoids it but is not default. Scope generation allocation/fencing to the same identity/election domain as locking.

### LEAF-02 — Ordinary direct-node close leaves peer-owned organization calls pending

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed whole-node-close retirement gap.
- **Source:** `wasm.rs:4018-4044`; terminal Direct settlement `1789-1794`; attempt retirement `1889-1925`; callOrg receiver `7072-7111`; closed pumping/ticker `697-698,1317-1319,5030-5043`; contrasting leadership retirement `4066`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Establish leaf-to-leaf connectivity, start callOrg to that peer, then close the node before response. close drops only the anchor session; already-Direct attempts decline further settlement and their bookkeeping is cleared without dropping peer sessions. Pending receivers keep state alive after reply delivery and deadline pumping stop, so calls can remain unresolved indefinitely.
- **Guard check / repair:** Anchor calls and leadership-loss all-call retirement are handled. Apply all-peer/all-call retirement, including org callers/serves, before ordinary close stops the pump. Related to historical MR#13 transport-loss cleanup, but explicit node close is another path.

### LEAF-03 — Incumbent dialog discards candidates for a pending replacement offer

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed verified signalling dispatch hole.
- **Source:** `wasm.rs:1503-1554`.
- **Trigger / impact:** B retains an old terminal Direct dialog. A sends a new offer and trickles valid candidates before B answers. The new offer is stored separately, but any peers[from] entry causes candidate routing to return even when its dialog differs. PendingOffer::early never receives the replacement candidates. If gathering finishes before answer, replacement can time out despite having received reachable candidates.
- **Guard check / repair:** First-time offers retain early candidates; signatures/replay/dialog authentication remain intact. Return only after routing a matching dialog, otherwise try the matching pending offer. Adjacent to MR#21/MR#52 repairs, not their exact old trigger.

### LEAF-04 — Last follower Detach never releases its declared memberships

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed subscription lifetime leak.
- **Source:** `leader.rs:363-364,1756-1758`; post-dispatch servicing `leader_session.rs:1648-1652`; release servicing `1551-1577`; add-only reconciliation `1415-1464`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A follower alone declares channel C, then explicitly closes. Detach removes its registry declaration, but only explicit Unsubscribe creates pending releases; reconcile only adds missing subscriptions. Leader retains membership/subscribed cache indefinitely, continuing traffic/resource use until unrelated teardown.
- **Guard check / repair:** Explicit unsubscribe has last-consumer arithmetic and capability withdrawal is separately reconciled. Reconcile membership removals or derive them from departing declarations against the remaining union.

### LEAF-05 — Follower session close suppresses its scheduled backend stream closes

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed close-order ownership leak.
- **Source:** `browser-ts/src/leader/session.ts:902-911`; `leader_session.rs:3414-3428,639-643`; duplicate ownership gate `stream_ownership.rs:334-349`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A follower opens an application stream then closes its session while the leader remains alive. Browser close ends local streams, whose ProxyStream closes only spawn future requests, then synchronously closes the wasm session. Those queued requests later refuse against closed lifecycle before sending StreamClose. Detach does not clean backend streams, so ownership/identity remains and a new follower cannot reopen it.
- **Guard check / repair:** Yielding after stream close, or closing the leader, can clean up but does not protect ordinary follower close. Synchronously enqueue departure cleanup or associate/retire backend streams with follower ownership on Detach.

### LEAF-06 — Proxied stream callbacks remain owned after close

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed known-class callback retention residual.
- **Source:** `leader_session.rs:914-920,1899-1923,3381-3395,3414-3428`; automatic browser registration `browser-ts/src/stream.ts:204`; application suppression `317-319`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Repeated proxied openStream/close cycles. on_message forgets a closure capturing lifecycle/consumer and registers its function in session listeners; close neither unregisters nor drops it. Every closed wrapper remains retained and later event dispatch keeps walking its listener.
- **Guard check / repair:** Browser wrapper suppresses application delivery after close and generation checks suppress stale generations; neither releases ownership. Use owned unregisterable registrations on close.
- **Provenance:** MR#54's direct-stream callback class is repaired with token removal (`wasm.rs:4660-4678`); the proxied counterpart still uses forget. No post-close application delivery or measured memory profile is asserted.

### LEAF-07 — Cancelled diagnostic probe skips RTC close and callback detachment

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed cancellation cleanup gap, browser retention duration unmeasured.
- **Source:** `bootstrap.rs:525-560,979-988`; servicing `wasm.rs:4377-4382`; cancelling lifecycle `leader_session.rs:1989-1995,2052-2065`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Leader probes an expired peer ICE attempt, then session close/retirement cancels that fenced operation during its two-second probe timer. Close/detach executes only after the timer, and StunProbe has no Drop cleanup. Separate probe RTC connections remain for browser reclamation and installed event properties can invoke wrappers whose Rust closures have dropped.
- **Guard check / repair:** Normal completion closes/detaches, and node transport teardown does not own these separate connections. Put closure detachment and connection close in a probe ownership guard. Cancellation residual adjacent to MR#107's normal-path closure fix; no native-memory-safety exploit is claimed.

### LEAF-08 — Cancelled IndexedDB awaits retain handlers for dropped wasm closures

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed installed-handler cancellation gap.
- **Source:** `storage.rs:516-527,579-595`; promoted-follower wait/cancellation `leader_session.rs:954,1029-1033,1229`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A promoted follower waits for generation storage, then closes; its Cancellable drops the takeover future. Request/transaction callbacks were installed before await and are cleared only afterward. Ongoing IndexedDB work can subsequently dispatch into dropped wasm closures, causing uncaught callback errors.
- **Guard check / repair:** Success/error paths detach correctly; atomic committed generation allocation remains protected. No duplicate generation or identity corruption is asserted. Guard installed handlers for detach-on-Drop and define whether abandoned write transactions should abort. Related dropped-callback class to MR#53, on another lifecycle primitive.

### LEAF-09 — Closed followers retain queued Web Lock acquisition tasks

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium resource-lifetime concern; operational magnitude unmeasured.
- **Source:** `leader_session.rs:169-235,540-543,940-999`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Repeatedly open/close follower sessions while an incumbent keeps its lock. await_promotion strongly owns lifecycle and waits in request_web_lock before checking closed. No AbortSignal/close cancellation removes the queued request, so options/vault/state remain retained until incumbent release.
- **Guard check / repair:** Once granted, closed followers immediately return/release; this is not permanent lock theft. Tab destruction also bounds retention. Cancel queued acquisition and safely release any concurrent grant.

Leaf exclusions: Expired game-anchor invites deliberately refuse reconnect outside validity (`net/crates/net/sdk/src/game_anchor.rs:35-46,121-124,968-971`). Bare carrier close versus session-loss replacement is intentional. Superseded-session refusal until a new promotion is adjudicated behavior, not a zombie regression. Frozen-leader rpc-indeterminate is an unknown-outcome/no-retry contract. Inspected signalling/proof role/pair/transcript bindings, fresh announcement lookup and bounded replay checks yielded no additional concrete failure. Historical comparison covered September WebRTC merge/repair and October SDK reviews. No browser/network/runtime executions were run.

## Seventh audit pass

Seventh pass complete: four subagents audited browser stores, netcode, world/region handoffs and native compute groups; the parent inspected lobby admission/teardown. All returned concerns are recorded below: 61 added numbered entries (LOBBY 3, CGRP 13, WORLD 12, NC 11, STORE 22). Existing roots are identified as residuals/cross-references, not claimed as novel bugs. Source-confirmed paths, inferred schedules, trusted-callback/API contracts and unproven runtime consequences are distinguished. No production edits, builds/tests or browser/network/runtime executions.

### LOBBY-01 — Native discovery descriptors omit the record required to list lobbies

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium documented integration gap; source-confirmed empty-list behavior for the native adapter.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk-ts/src/store-transport.ts:76-84,182-191`; `net/crates/net/browser-ts/src/lobby.ts:545-557`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Use the advertised native meshStoreTransport with listLobbies. query(listingTag) returns matching node IDs, but synthesizes each descriptor's capabilities as only that listing tag. listLobbies requires a separate net-lobby:<game>:rec: tag and skips every such descriptor, so discoverable native/browser lobbies yield an empty native listing.
- **Boundary / repair:** The adapter explicitly documents truncated descriptors even while describing world/lobby helper compatibility. Direct known-host/code matching does not require this record and is not claimed broken. Supply authenticated complete announcement descriptors for record-consuming helpers, or explicitly narrow helper support. This is one discovery-adapter root even if another helper also needs record tags.

### LOBBY-02 — Concurrent lobby close does not share the teardown completion barrier

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed wrapper completion-order gap.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/browser-ts/src/lobby.ts:529-540`; awaited host teardown `store/host.ts:764-818,887-901` in the same browser-ts/src directory.
- **Trigger / impact:** Two disposal paths call lobby.close concurrently. The first sets closed before awaiting the host-player/store shutdown and final announcement withdrawal; the second sees closed and returns a resolved promise immediately. Awaiting the second close can therefore report completion while the store/address/reply resources remain active or the old lobby announcement has not been withdrawn, interfering with a successor on that node.
- **Guard check / repair:** HostedStore.close correctly latches one closing promise, but the lobby wrapper hides it behind a boolean. Latch/return the complete lobby teardown promise, including withdrawal, for all callers. No claim that callers may run two live lobbies simultaneously is needed.

### LOBBY-03 — Failed join returns before its seek withdrawal can overwrite a successor's announcement

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium asynchronous-transport cleanup race; source-backed schedule, not reproduced.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/browser-ts/src/lobby.ts:633-678,692-694,701-720`; native async announcement `net/crates/net/sdk-ts/src/store-transport.ts:182-184` and `mesh.ts:985-986`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A seek announcement is still pending when reach/join setup fails. The failure paths call void stop() and reject without its barrier. The caller handles that completed failure and creates a lobby on the same node. When old seeking work settles, stop issues a new announce(keep), replacing the successor's whole tag set and erasing its listing.
- **Guard check / repair:** Successful joined-handle close waits for withdrawal; failure paths do not. This is not an old lower-version packet defeating version ordering: cleanup creates a later explicit baseline replacement. Await stop before rejecting, or fence cleanup against a newer announcement owner. The ordinary browser wasm announce body has no internal await (`net/crates/net/leaf/src/wasm.rs:2720-2732`); a long outstanding announcement is not asserted for that default fast path.

Lobby coverage/exclusions: Unlisted codes are deliberately guessable, listings are untrusted records bound to signed host identity, one lobby owns a node's announcement, and host capacity includes the host. Existing short announcement retry and decimal kick-ID fixes are present. Host-player close resolves normally; host-store teardown bounds goodbye waits and isolates individual stream-close errors. A missing host-close wait for an earlier announcement was not independently promoted: native baseline/version publication is serialized before broadcast (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:45885-45890`), and default browser announce completes its mutation synchronously. The join timeout's separate discovery/reach stages and awaited dial boundary need an explicit overall-deadline contract before claiming one. No runtime reproduction was run.

Compute-group locations below are relative to `net/crates/net/`. Rust SDK groups is default-enabled (`sdk/Cargo.toml:274-280`). These are local coordination overlays: placement metadata does not deploy remote members, and automatic failure detection/cross-node fencing is outside the current implementation.

### CGRP-01 — Group route lookup leaks load-balancer connection reservations

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed eventual routing exhaustion.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/compute/group_coord.rs:155-163`; `src/adapter/net/behavior/loadbalance.rs:350-365,687-702,1025-1026,1075-1079`; SDK consumers `sdk/src/groups/replica.rs:120-123`, `fork.rs:96-98`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Repeated successful route_event calls select members. Each selection reserves a connection, but coordinator returns only origin_hash and never records completion; later runtime.deliver does not release it. At the default 10,000 selections per member, healthy members hit the cap and routing returns NoHealthyMember until endpoint replacement/removal.
- **Guard check / repair:** Probe watchdog only clears half-open ownership, and SDK exposes no completion API. Give this API reservation ownership/completion or use a truly non-reserving lookup. Historical probe/underflow fixes are other roots.

### CGRP-02 — Standby replay compares input sequences against the active's output watermark

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed replay namespace mismatch, known X-19 repair residual.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/compute/standby_group.rs:260,420-423,736-739`; `compute/host.rs:218-249`; `state/snapshot.rs:158-171`, with shorter paths under src/adapter/net/.
- **Trigger / impact:** Snapshot an active at output sequence 10, then accept producer P's input sequence 1 and promote. The replay filter skips that accepted input because 1 <= 10, though those sequences belong to different chains. Zero/multiple outputs per input invalidate the comparison too; inputs above an output watermark can conversely be duplicated after partial sync.
- **Repair / provenance:** Track group-local accepted-input progress and snapshot replay boundaries atomically. Cross-reference X-19 (`docs/internal/misc/BUG_AUDIT_2026_05_18_CARRIED_FORWARD.md:707-711`); partial-sync double execution is not counted again as another root.

### CGRP-03 — First promotion deletes replay history still needed by another standby

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed consecutive-promotion state loss.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/compute/standby_group.rs:420-431,736-746`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A/B/C sync at S. A accepts E; promote B, which replays E and clears the shared buffer. Promote C before another sync. C still has S but E is gone, so successful promotion loses it even with serialized calls and sequences satisfying the filter.
- **Guard check / repair:** Only new active tracking is advanced; other standbys are neither updated nor made ineligible. Retain tail until every eligible standby covers it, sync them before truncation, or fence insufficiently caught-up candidates. Adjacent to X-19 but a separate premature-reclamation schedule.

### CGRP-04 — Promotion ignores failed replay and returns success

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed false successful failover.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/compute/standby_group.rs:383-400,420-433,717-746`; rejecting delivery `compute/registry.rs:292-303` under the same adapter/net directory.
- **Trigger / impact:** A buffered event passes the filter, but promoted daemon process returns an error. Promotion discards registry.deliver's error, clears the buffer and returns Ok(new_active_origin); snapshot failure is ignored too. Accepted state is incomplete yet exposed as successfully restored.
- **Repair / boundary:** Make catch-up success part of authority publication, retain tail/failure evidence and account for already-applied entries rather than blindly repeating side effects. Candidate health/no-candidate guards do not cover replay failure. Distinct from repaired April 30 CORE #103 no-candidate half-mutation.

### CGRP-05 — Standby restore combines its own identity with the active's chain origin

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed identity/chain inconsistency.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/compute/host.rs:290-320`; `state/causal.rs:177-182,189-201`; rejecting decoder `state/snapshot.rs:449-455`, with shorter paths under src/adapter/net/.
- **Trigger / impact:** Sync A's snapshot into standby B, then promote/deliver. B retains its own keypair/registry origin but from_head adopts A's chain origin. Routing names B while emitted events claim A; B's later snapshot carries entity B plus origin A and its serialized bytes fail StateSnapshot::from_bytes.
- **Guard check / repair:** from_snapshot validates identity, but in-place standby restore bypasses it without re-keying. Define one coherent logical identity/registry/chain model or explicit per-member chain continuity. Independent of STATE-03's missing head payload.

### CGRP-06 — Failed initial group construction leaves earlier members registered

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed partial-construction ownership leak.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/compute/replica_group.rs:133-151,374-400`; `standby_group.rs:149-164,570-592`; `fork_group.rs:129-146,422-445` in that compute directory.
- **Trigger / impact:** Request more members than distinct placement candidates. Earlier iterations register hosts, then later placement returns through ?. Caller receives no group object but retains those live registry entries. Deterministic replica/standby retries collide; repeated failed fork construction adds fresh random hosts.
- **Guard check / repair:** Collision checks work; constructor rollback/coordinator Drop does not clean this attempt. Track and safely unregister only entries it inserted on failure. Ordinary successful-overlay Drop is deliberately non-owning and is not the finding. Historical failed-replacement #7 is another consumer.

### CGRP-07 — Group mutations can register hosts after runtime shutdown

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed runtime lifecycle bypass.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/groups/replica.rs:130-158`; `fork.rs:100-120`; `standby.rs:227-243`; lifecycle `sdk/src/compute.rs:672-708,1717-1724`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Create a group, shut down its runtime, then scale up or replace a failed member. Retained factories remain available and group wrappers bypass readiness/registration fencing, repopulating the drained registry with hosts that runtime.deliver refuses to use. Initial construction also races shutdown after its first readiness check.
- **Guard check / repair:** Normal spawn includes post-insert shutdown rollback (equivalent guard at compute.rs:1050-1055); groups do not. Serialize lifecycle admission/registration commit and roll back only owned insertions. Distinct from aggregator drain/Spawn.

### CGRP-08 — Accepted input processing and standby replay capture are not one ordered operation

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed lost/reordered accepted inputs.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/compute.rs:1128-1154`; `sdk/src/groups/standby.rs:97-103,146-152,209-215`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Delivery A finishes registry processing, then promotion removes its old-origin observer before A looks it up; A returns success unbuffered. Alternatively host processing serializes A then B, but delayed A callback appends after B; failover replays B then A and diverges noncommutative state.
- **Guard check / repair:** New-active observer rebinding under group lock does not cover old-active processing/capture. Host mutex ends before callbacks; buffer mutex orders callbacks, not accepted inputs. Use a coherent group admission/input ordering/snapshot/promotion protocol without introducing callback lock-order deadlocks. October manual-double-buffer documentation issue is repaired, not recounted.

### CGRP-09 — Immediate replacement can co-locate members despite available spare nodes

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed placement metadata spread violation.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/compute/replica_group.rs:257-259,294-317,523-524`; `fork_group.rs:296-298,331-365,596-597`; `standby_group.rs:466-468,481-513,792-793`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Members occupy A/B/C, with spare D eligible. B fails; replacement excludes only failed B, so it may choose surviving A/C and mark the replacement healthy there. Later survivor failure affects multiple members despite the spare.
- **Guard check / repair:** Subsequent replacements exclude earlier replacements, not unaffected members. Recovery correctly excludes healthy placements but skips already healthy replacements. Seed exclusions with surviving and failed nodes in both placement variants. This is metadata/control spread, not proof of remote execution; adjacent to historical scale-up #6.

### CGRP-10 — Scale-down bypasses subscription-ledger teardown

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed uncovered consumer of the known subscription-ownership root.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/compute/replica_group.rs:224-235,478-489`; `fork_group.rs:256-266,546-556`; subscribe/normal stop/cleanup `sdk/src/compute.rs:1232-1278,1085-1102,1904-1953`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Subscribe a trailing member, then shrink the group. Core directly unregisters it and discards its ledger without last-holder unsubscribe. Node membership/traffic remain after the last interested member disappears; concurrent subscribe bypasses normal stop's membership serialization too.
- **Repair / provenance:** Route SDK group removal through serialized runtime-owned ledger teardown. Cross-reference October SDK audit subscription root (`docs/internal/misc/SDK_AUDIT_2026_10_11.md:405-407`), not a wholly new teardown mechanism.

### CGRP-11 — Failed standby synchronization has no replay-buffer admission bound

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium operational capacity concern.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/compute/standby_group.rs:215-217,307-310`; cadence contract `docs/COMPUTE.md:325-333`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Restore repeatedly fails while active deliveries continue succeeding. Accepted payloads are retained without count/byte cap, growing memory indefinitely.
- **Qualification / repair:** Snapshot cadence is application-owned; simply never calling sync is not independently a correctness bug. Define observable bounded admission/backpressure during prolonged sync failure. Related to migration-buffer X-11, but a different buffer.

### CGRP-12 — Recovery registrations have no unregisterable group lifetime

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium runtime-scoped ownership concern.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/compute/mod.rs:75-87,112-119,167-191`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Use documented recovery registration capturing the group strongly, then drop the application's group reference. Successful closures remain registered for runtime lifetime, retaining the group; registration returns a count, not an unregister token. Even empty weak-capture handlers remain registered.
- **Qualification / repair:** Permanent runtime-scoped registration may be intended, but conflicts with “dropped group” cleanup wording for the strong-capture example. Define lifetime or return unregister ownership. Historical M-12 addressed invocation-lock deadlock, not retention.

### CGRP-13 — Standby snapshot restoration omits subscription-ledger ownership

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium failover integration boundary.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/compute/host.rs:290-320`; contrasting full restoration `182-198`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Synced/promoted standby receives state/chain/horizon but not snapshot.bindings_bytes. It has no ledger for active subscriptions; stopping the old member can trigger last-holder unsubscribe although promoted workload expects those deliveries.
- **Qualification / repair:** The current standby contract does not explicitly promise ledger failover. Do not claim every channel-driven failover is supported; define and consistently transfer ownership if it is. Related to October subscription ownership, distinct restore consumer.

Compute-group known root, not another numbered entry: May 3 #15 remains reachable when all candidates are unsynced (`src/adapter/net/compute/standby_group.rs:365-378,511-512,676-685`; `docs/internal/misc/BUG_AUDIT_2026_05_03.md:49`). After sync discards pre-sync history, replace the only standby and promote before another sync. “No eligible synced candidate” falls back to a blank member although retained tail cannot reconstruct old state. Require proven coverage rather than conflating never-synced startup with discarded history.

Compute-group exclusions: Distributed split-brain fencing X-1 is explicitly deferred; local term scaffolding is not wire enforcement. Replica/fork fresh-state replacement is documented (`docs/COMPUTE.md:266-277,335-345`). Group factories are infallible Rust Fn factories, and fork identity plumbing satisfies from_fork guards. Stale pre-lock registry references are fenced; the documented already-locked-host residual is May 3 #13, not new. No-candidate mutation ordering, partial-sync bookkeeping, active-slot recovery exclusion, replacement lifecycle events and recovery-outside-lock fixes are present. Successful-overlay non-owning Drop, gang bridge and MIG-01..04 were not recounted. No runtime reproduction was run.

World locations below are relative to `net/crates/net/browser-ts/src/`. Supported world functionality is documented in browser-ts/CHANGELOG.md:386-431; missing runtime witnesses are called out where relevant.

### WORLD-01 — Handoff transport loses the destination region and fans out to siblings

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed cross-region duplicate execution/admission.
- **Source:** `world/link.ts:201-238,440-460`; `world/handoff.ts:193-215`; transport fan-out `local.ts:111-124`.
- **Trigger / impact:** X hosts A; Y hosts B and C under the documented shared handoff label. A targets B, but send uses to only to choose Y and serializes just {n,b}. Every matching Y driver receives the authenticated body; both B and C can admit the entity. Source settles B's reply and ignores C's unexpected reply, leaving C live too. Forwarded actions/ghosts have the same sibling dispatch hole.
- **Guard check / repair:** Sender authentication checks A's host, not destination; per-region dedup cannot prevent independent sibling effects. Carry/dispatch an explicit destination region. Multiple regions on a node are supported; this is separate from historical sibling-store routing.

### WORLD-02 — Colocated regions have no supplied local handoff carrier

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed supported local/browser integration gap.
- **Source:** `world/regions.ts:159-185`; `world/link.ts:229-238`; self-excluding discovery `local.ts:171-176`; peer-session requirement `net/crates/net/leaf/src/node.rs:2118-2135`.
- **Trigger / impact:** One node hosts A and B. Its directory excludes itself, so handoff A→B freezes the entity then repeatedly drops unknown-region until unresolved, although B is live. A custom self mapping still tries a self stream on a browser node with no self session.
- **Guard check / repair:** Same-region moves are rejected correctly; distinct colocated regions are permitted. Route registered local region destinations through the same durable step driver rather than discovery/self-streams. Keeping destination in WORLD-01 does not by itself add this local path; no native self-stream claim is made.

### WORLD-03 — Unrestricted late reoffer outlives destination decision retention

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed historical SHARD-3 residual, not a new root.
- **Source:** `world/handoff.ts:193-209,243-250,287-305`; historical `docs/internal/misc/CODE_REVIEW_2026_10_13_SHARDED_MATCH.md:205-213`.
- **Trigger / impact:** B durably accepts A's offered entity, but accepts never reach A. A retains an unresolved frozen copy. After default ten-minute decision retention, B prunes acceptance; A can reoffer that same ID at any later time. If B still owns it, B now refuses occupied and A restores its frozen copy, making both live. If B moved it onward, it can re-admit stale state instead.
- **Guard check / repair:** Retention greater than initial give-up does not cover recovery resetting since without an age limit; persistence cannot restore forgotten decisions. Retain decisions until settlement knowledge or define a safe bounded recovery protocol. The handoff simulation never prunes handled decisions. Separate actionTiming repairs combat coupling, not this recovery root.

### WORLD-04 — Duplicate responses bypass a failed durability obligation

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed durable-acknowledgement gap.
- **Source:** `world/link.ts:369-382`; unchanged duplicate offer `world/handoff.ts:193-195`; duplicate action `world/border.ts:73-75`; fallible real persistence `net/crates/net/sdk-ts/src/store-persist.ts:118-139`.
- **Trigger / impact:** B commits admission/effect plus decision in memory, then persist throws (e.g. append disk-full). Queue continues with that nondurable memory installed. A retries; duplicate step returns the same state object, so run skips persistence and sends accept/success. A can durably discard its frozen copy; B's crash before later successful flush loses the entity/effect evidence.
- **Guard check / repair:** Successful persistence is serialized, but failed persistence leaves no dirty/fenced condition. Fence the driver or retain the obligation and satisfy it before any response, including unchanged-state duplicates. This is not omitting required persistence, nor dependent on TSDK-01's separate fsync promise bug.

### WORLD-05 — Receiver prunes incoming action dedup using its unrelated outgoing timeout

- **Severity / status:** Conditional high; source-confirmed cross-host retry/retention mismatch.
- **Source:** `world/link.ts:435-438,493-507`; `world/border.ts:73-75,137-147`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A legitimately retries forwards for default 60 seconds. B uses short-action give-up 500 ms/retention 10 seconds. Both local settings satisfy retention>give-up, but B prunes A's incoming record from B's own timeout. Lost results let A's still-valid retries execute the same action again after ten seconds.
- **Guard check / repair:** Requests carry no sender retry horizon and duplicates do not extend record age. actionTiming lacks even handoff's local validation; validating only B's settings cannot fix heterogeneous hosts. Establish a world-wide retry/retention invariant or communicate/enforce an execution deadline retained by receivers. Distinct from WORLD-03's unlimited explicit recovery.

### WORLD-06 — Ready world subscriptions never react to later terminal replica status

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed same-host restart reacquisition failure.
- **Source:** `world/regions.ts:384-405,433-440`; available status subscription `store/join.ts:114-121,640-645`; owner loss `store/replica.ts:686-694`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Held region becomes ready, then its host store closes and restarts at the same connected node/address. Replica correctly becomes terminal/clears its view, but joinWorld observes document changes and ready only once. Its entry stays ready and is not replaced/retried; player actions target the dead replica until release/reacquire.
- **Guard check / repair:** Initial failures retry; ready-entry terminal loss does not. Subscribe to status, update region phase and reacquire under existing lookup/retry policy without retrying ambiguous actions. Native acceptance restarts east (`net/crates/net/sdk-ts/test/world_acceptance.test.ts:221-232`) but checks host ownership, not restored player subscription.

### WORLD-07 — Fresh unannounced browser world players lack discovery needed for peer establishment

- **Severity / status:** High under stated browser prerequisites; source-traced integration defect, not browser-executed.
- **Source:** `world/regions.ts:365-383`; peer connection `store/join.ts:271-284`; working prerequisite `lobby.ts:24-29,627-637`; receiving gate `net/crates/net/leaf/src/node.rs:4252-4258`; `node.ts:1330-1338`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A newly connected browser player has made no signed capability announcement and has no pre-established region peer sessions, then uses joinWorld. Hosts are discovered, but player discovery is never announced; joinStore's connection is refused by browser hosts without its signed identity. Retrying world acquisition repeats the missing prerequisite.
- **Guard check / repair:** Already announced players/preconnected sessions avoid the trigger. joinLobby explicitly publishes/refreshes player seek identity; WorldNode exposes announce but joinWorld does not own that lifecycle. Provide announcement/tag-preservation and discovery-before-connect, or clearly require that prerequisite. Native acceptance preconnects all participants (`world_acceptance.test.ts:103-105`), and the browser world witness is explicitly outstanding in the historical large-world plan. No unsupported openSession/store composition is assumed.

### WORLD-08 — Ghost receive expiry depends on the receiver's own send cadence

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed false disappearance with heterogeneous valid cadences.
- **Source:** `world/link.ts:403-406,462-466,484-492`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A sends ghosts each second; neighboring B uses default 100-ms cadence. B expires A's frames after 500 ms, so A disappears from collision/line-of-sight reads for roughly half each healthy interval. A receiver without outgoing ghosting still uses that default.
- **Guard check / repair:** New frames correctly replace old ones; the freshness bound comes from the wrong owner. Define compatible receive freshness separately or communicate/validate sender cadence. Matching-cadence tests do not cover this configuration.

### WORLD-09 — Closed region drivers still accept new handoff/reoffer work

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium lifecycle inconsistency.
- **Source:** `world/link.ts:369-382,513-518,534-553`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Retain a closed driver and call handoff/reoffer, or queue work just before close. Unlike forward, those methods and queued run execution do not check closed, so they can freeze/commit/send after receive handlers and retry timers are removed. Initiation resolves with no live driver to process answers/timeouts.
- **Qualification / repair:** Persisted frozen state remains recoverable by a replacement; no irreversible loss/duplication is asserted here. Fence new/not-started work and define completion of already durable steps without cancelling persistence after freeze.

### WORLD-10 — Failed sends to a stale known host never refresh its directory mapping

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium host-reassignment/cache concern.
- **Source:** `world/link.ts:142-170,183-197,229-238`; cached directory `world/regions.ts:157-185`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A caches B→P; external replacement/reassignment moves B to Q while P becomes unreachable. Refresh occurs for unknown destinations/incoming sender mismatch, not unanswered or failed outbound attempts. With no Q message, retries/reoffers keep selecting P despite current discovery pointing at Q.
- **Qualification / repair:** Stream retirement reopens the same stale peer. Refresh after controlled failed establishment/send/unanswered retries with stale-lookup fencing. Automatic placement/split/merge is deferred; this is not a proven shipped migration-protocol failure. Historical refresh repair covers initially empty caches.

### WORLD-11 — Out-of-order directory query completion can regress a current mapping

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium asynchronous API boundary; live scheduling not witnessed.
- **Source:** `world/regions.ts:159-180`; sender authentication consumer `world/link.ts:218-223`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Older lookup L1 is delayed, newer L2 caches a host/availability answer, then L1 overwrites it unconditionally. Sends and sender checks regress to old host or unhosted after successful refresh.
- **Guard check / repair:** Trusted-host and ambiguity checks validate each result, not ordering. Sequence/coalesce per-region lookup publication. The Promise query/shared-directory contract permits overlap; no claim that synchronous local mesh itself creates it.

### WORLD-12 — Ghost sequencing has no driver-instance generation

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium bounded stale-ghost interval.
- **Source:** `world/link.ts:400-403,429-431,462-465,484-488`.
- **Trigger / impact:** B keeps A's high ghost sequence; A rapidly recreates its driver at sequence one on the same peer/region. B rejects fresh lower sequences until old receive expiry while using stale entity states.
- **Guard check / repair:** Defaults bound suppression to the remainder of roughly 500 ms plus delivery cadence; longer restart avoids it. No permanent wedge or authoritative duplicate ownership is claimed. Identify driver generations and reset sequencing on an authenticated replacement.

World exclusions: Silence deliberately leaves handoffs frozen and unknown action outcomes are not retried under fresh IDs automatically. Caller newId must be fresh. Optional trustedHosts semantics and source-region (not individual player) attribution are explicit. announceRegions stop is not a withdrawal barrier; short failure backoff is repaired. Merged-view release/removal/generation checks work as designed. Native transports require preconnected peers/listening labels, and handoffLink has no stronger browser auto-connect promise. LOBBY-01's truncated native descriptor does not break regionDirectory, which consumes peerIdHex rather than companion record tags; no extra world root was counted. No runtime reproduction was run.

Netcode locations below are relative to `net/crates/net/browser-ts/src/netcode/`; browser/demo paths are qualified separately.

### NC-01 — Synchronous stream-open failure leaves a player permanently marked opening

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed per-player recovery wedge.
- **Source:** `host.ts:301-320`; synchronous browser transport `net/crates/net/browser-ts/src/node.ts:505-511`; missing-session refusal `net/crates/net/leaf/src/wasm.rs:2622-2648` and `node.rs:2118-2134`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A player's stream fails after session loss and is retired. Next send sets opening=true, then evaluates openStream before Promise.resolve. A synchronous throw bypasses catch/finally, leaving opening latched. Even after reconnection/pings, every reply/snapshot drops stream-not-open until that player record is removed; the triggering tick can skip later players.
- **Guard check / repair:** Async rejection and existing-stream send errors are handled. Invoke the opener inside a promise callback/try and clear opening on every path.

### NC-02 — Interest version state does not survive or renegotiate player lifetimes

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed interest recovery mismatch.
- **Source:** `client.ts:328-347,540`; `host.ts:361-365,509-514,561-567`; ownership rejoin `shards.ts:316-323`.
- **Trigger / impact:** After acknowledged interest v1, host player timeout deletes interest while preserving input boundary; the surviving client still considers v1 acknowledged and never resends, receiving all otherwise visible entities indefinitely. Conversely, a reconstructed client starts at v1 while host retains a higher version, rejects its changed interest and echoes an old acknowledgement that can stop retries.
- **Guard check / repair:** In-lifetime versioning works and codec fingerprints refresh independently. Interest is not permission; this is wrong filtering/traffic, not authorization bypass. Negotiate client/server incarnation and interest recovery across timeout/rejoin.

### NC-03 — Recreated clients do not advance their input sequence past retained host progress

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed identity-preserving reconnect controls outage.
- **Source:** `client.ts:200-202,297,689,739-742`; `host.ts:351-361,534,561-567`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Reload/recreate a standalone client under remembered identity while host/entity survive. Client sequences restart at zero; host retains lastApplied, including through timeout parking. New inputs are discarded until the user produces more than the entire old sequence boundary. Snapshot acknowledgements only trim pending inputs, not advance seq, causing repeated prediction correction.
- **Guard check / repair:** Shared sequencers protect in-process shard moves, not reconstruction. Establish an authoritative input floor before issuing inputs or negotiate an explicit incarnation. Different fresh identities avoid the trigger. Related to October NET-4 boundary retention, not its already-covered surviving-client case.

### NC-04 — Local prediction survives authoritative entity removal

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed persistent phantom local entity.
- **Source:** `client.ts:692-705,755-762`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Initialize local prediction, then host despawns/deaths the entity or omits it from a complete visibility/interest snapshot. Reconciliation skips absent authoritative state, while view always overlays retained prediction. The removed entity stays visible and locally responsive indefinitely.
- **Guard check / repair:** Remote interpolation removes absent entities. Clear local prediction/correction when absence is authoritative, distinguishing genuinely missing chunks from complete received state.

### NC-05 — JSON snapshots cannot fence a recreated host's reset tick counter

- **Severity / status:** High; source-confirmed chunked-JSON restart freeze.
- **Source:** `client.ts:541-555,578-589,684-685`; JSON frame `wire.ts:77-105`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A long-running JSON host recreates hostNetcode under the same node/label, resetting tick to zero; its snapshots remain chunked. Client rejects each new assembly with tick<=newestTick until host catches the predecessor's tick count, potentially freezing a long match for that entire interval.
- **Guard check / repair:** Binary instance replacement resets tick/assemblies/history. Default/mismatch/fallback JSON has no instance to activate it. Carry/consume the incarnation on JSON too. Residual of October NET-2's binary-only coverage, not a repeat of wrong-index corruption.

### NC-06 — Late players cannot materialize an already-path-driven NPC without baseline state

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed shipped resolver/recipe late-join gap.
- **Source:** `paths.ts:40-47,262-288`; producer `net/crates/net/browser-ts/demo/sharded-arena/shard.js:325-346`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A player joins after the NPC's initial path-announcement window. The recipe hides path NPC snapshots from players after that window; path records have waypoints but no baseline entity/factory. Resolver has no entry/last/live/drawn value and cannot place it, leaving the NPC invisible for its path mode despite a valid owner clock.
- **Guard check / repair:** Other hosts still receive baseline snapshots for rewind/recovery, not newly joining players. Send baseline with path records, seed each new player, or expose construction. Adjacent to October SHARD-2/9, whose repairs do not address cold player joins.

### NC-07 — Outstanding shard ready wait ignores newly added connections

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed false readiness.
- **Source:** `shards.ts:322-340`.
- **Trigger / impact:** ready captures pending A. A higher assignment adds B without replacing A; A receives a snapshot while B is silent. The wait checks only that captured entries still exist and resolves, though a current installed shard has never delivered.
- **Guard check / repair:** Replacement/removal makes an old captured entry noncurrent and correctly retries. Compare complete current/captured sets, including additions, before resolving. Distinct residual of October SHARD-5 readiness repair.

### NC-08 — Finite float32 overflow bypasses writer fallback and produces rejected snapshots

- **Severity / status:** Medium numeric edge; source-confirmed writer/decoder mismatch.
- **Source:** `codec.ts:224-236`; JSON fallback `host.ts:609-619`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A float32 field holds a finite JavaScript value outside finite f32 range, e.g. 1e39. finite accepts it and setFloat32 encodes infinity without throwing. Decoder rejects the entire snapshot; persistent values stop snapshots/input acknowledgements and can time out joins.
- **Guard check / repair:** Nonfinite decoder rejection works. Check f32 representability before writing so the existing JSON fallback runs. Related to October NET-11 decoder hardening, distinct writer boundary.

### NC-09 — Rewind history aliases caller-owned mutable snapshot objects

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium snapshot-ownership contract concern.
- **Source:** `host.ts:582-585,688-693`; public examples `net/crates/net/browser-ts/README.md:374-378` and host.ts:15.
- **Trigger / impact:** snapshot returns the same mutable ships map each tick, or mutates retained entity objects. History stores those references directly; later updates rewrite every historical sample and rewind returns current rather than historical positions.
- **Qualification / repair:** Public examples return ships/world.ships directly; immutable past-result ownership is not stated. The shipped shard recipe uses persistent replacements and is safe by that convention. Own stable history or explicitly establish/enforce retained immutability.

### NC-10 — Latest frame epoch is not bound to the rendered snapshot history

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium epoch/state association concern, not an established permission leak.
- **Source:** `client.ts:532-555,684`; `shards.ts:226-256`.
- **Trigger / impact:** First incomplete chunk advances snapshotEpoch before assembly. Installing its assignment can unfreeze rendering from older buffered snapshots because TimedSnapshot stores no epoch and joinShards fences the whole buffer with the latest-frame epoch.
- **Qualification / repair:** Carry-over/interpolation deliberately retain older positions. Associate epochs with accepted snapshots and define cross-epoch interpolation before treating every old position as erroneous.

### NC-11 — Frozen shard clients continue resending already-pending inputs

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium live/freeze contract concern.
- **Source:** `shards.ts:195,219,276-286`; `client.ts:718-730`.
- **Trigger / impact:** live() becomes false, blocking new input calls/freezing drawing, while subordinate timers retransmit shared pending backlog. A host still holding a valid lease can apply a formerly lost input after the player freezes.
- **Qualification / repair:** Previously issued inputs may reasonably remain committed/in flight; no new-input authorization bypass is claimed. Clarify the public “stop input sends” boundary; gate timer resends only if freeze is meant to stop all transmission.

Netcode known overlaps/exclusions: Assignment renewal still needs the caller's real delivery bound; default maxDeliveryMs=0 is explicitly unsound on networks (assignment.ts:238-245,281-299; historical SHARD-6), not newly counted. Retained replaced-host membership is the October open residual, not a new ongoing-authorization guarantee. Malformed null input entries can throw (wire.ts:167-170; host.ts:528,534), but browser/native dispatch isolation prevented establishing host-wide crash/persistent denial, so none is claimed. Input gaps/backlog truncation are intentional lossy-state policy. Missing-chunk carry, capped extrapolation and recorded-state rewind are deliberate approximations. NPC path keys are not permissions. Current angle/name request/finite decoding/exact-time sampling/shared-ack/late-adoption/throttle guards exist. Unsupported openSession lossy/store behavior, raw leaf reliability and world handoff were not counted here. No runtime reproduction was run.

Store locations below are relative to `net/crates/net/browser-ts/src/store/`. All scenarios are source-derived, not executed; none requires unsupported stores over openSession.

### STORE-01 — NUL-delimited visibility path keys can alias another entity's ancestors

- **Severity / status:** Conditional high confidentiality; source-established missing path separation.
- **Source:** `visibility.ts:268-307`; admitted entity keys `core.ts:306-316`; final emission `owner.ts:1925-1933,551-568`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Host-only rules hide entities.*.secret and entities.*.nested.secret.value. An admitted creation action accepts entity ID target\u0000nested with a secret field. Its hidden-path encoding aliases entities.target.nested.secret. Processing the deeper rule on target then incorrectly sees a hidden ancestor, skips redaction and emits the nested secret in snapshot/delta.
- **Guard check / repair:** JSON permits escaped NUL; entity IDs reject empty but not NUL; shallow-first rules and final value validation do not reapply redaction. Requires attacker-influenced IDs, not attacker-controlled rules/peer spoofing. Preserve path segments with a trie or unambiguous encoding instead of delimiter joining.

### STORE-02 — Interest/deferred projection paths emit without fresh read authorization

- **Severity / status:** High confidentiality under changed policy; source-established unguarded fresh emissions.
- **Source:** `owner.ts:1846-1894,1352-1375`; contrasting checks `523-534,789-800,831-839`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Join with empty interest; external read policy revokes the peer without a document commit. An interest expansion before lease expiry projects previously undelivered entities without permitsRead. Separately, defer an authorized audience/resume while projection is unavailable, revoke access, then enable projection: resumeDeferred checks handle binding but not current policy before installation.
- **Guard check / repair:** Ordinary join/audience/resume/resync/commit paths authorize; interest filtering is not permission but expanding it still performs a new read. Reauthorize immediately before both emissions using the original join key. Residual of September ongoing-feed revocation coverage on separate paths.

### STORE-03 — One dead recipient aborts fanout before healthy recipients

- **Severity / status:** Medium/high availability and synchronization integrity; source-established batch failure propagation.
- **Source:** `host.ts:505-557,579-586`; owner revision advancement `owner.ts:590-595`; direct/local failure `net/crates/net/browser-ts/src/local.ts:111-113,148-152`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A joins before B and disappears without farewell. Each commit's A send/open fails non-stale; emit throws before B's frames. Until A expires/removes, B can miss every update despite its owner-side revision advancing, then require resync.
- **Guard check / repair:** detached counts the failed batch, not continuation; shutdown separately isolates farewell failures. Isolate ordinary failures per recipient while preserving that recipient's frame order.

### STORE-04 — First admitted frame can pin the host to an attacker-selected stream label
