- **Severity / status:** Medium/high availability; source-established admitted-participant denial.
- **Source:** `host.ts:436-452,645-650,693-696`.
- **Trigger / impact:** An admitted peer first sends a valid store join on a differently labeled reliable stream. Host learns its numeric arrivesOn globally. Legitimate subsequent joins on the configured label are dropped foreign-stream across peers for that host lifetime.
- **Guard check / repair:** Denied outsiders cannot establish the pin, but valid acceptance does not prove the configured stream identity. Verify actual label derivation or remove learned global restriction while retaining store/address/handle attribution.

### STORE-05 — Handle-less join accepts a sibling store's terminal notice

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-established sibling replica misattribution.
- **Source:** `join.ts:491-532`; `replica.ts:409-423,686-725`; supported topology `net/crates/net/browser-ts/test/store/siblings.test.ts`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Client reads A and starts joining B on the same host. Before B learns its handle, A closes with handle-scoped owner-lost. All joiners consume that host's stream data and B's foreign-handle guard is disabled while handle=null; terminal notice closes B despite its store remaining live.
- **Guard check / repair:** Learned handles and manifest correlation work. Before installation accept only this join's correlated refusal, not another unknown handle's terminal notice. Distinct from repaired host-side sibling dispatch.

### STORE-06 — Remote input sends do not implement promised bounded coalescing

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-established remote/local input semantics mismatch.
- **Source:** `join.ts:712-736`; consuming inputs `owner.ts:1080-1117`; local coalescing `player.ts:189-194,240-254`; contract `docs/internal/plans/BROWSER_GAME_STORE_API_DESIGN.md:377`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Issue many same-name inputs in one turn. Remote input dispatches every increasing sequence with its own send continuation instead of replacing unsent pending value, so work grows with calls. Later lifetime calls report replaced even with no queued predecessor; host-player coalesces correctly.
- **Guard check / repair:** Rejecting older sequence arrivals does not coalesce increasing ones. Own bounded per-name pending slots/dispositions and flush their latest values.

### STORE-07 — Locally capacity-refused actions are still transmitted and can execute

- **Severity / status:** Medium integrity; source-established known September #46 residual.
- **Source:** `join.ts:624-639,701-710,795-798`.
- **Trigger / impact:** With 64 correlations outstanding, another act gets an already-rejected capacity promise, but still sends before awaiting it. Owner can execute an authorized side effect while caller receives capacity. setInterest has the same ordering.
- **Guard check / repair:** Attached rejection handling does not stop transmission; owner authorization/dedup cannot enforce rejected local admission. Reserve correlation capacity synchronously before send. September's explicit unresolved adjudication is preserved; not claimed new.

### STORE-08 — Reconnect reuses an expired transition clock/retry ladder

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-established unnecessary handle replacement/fencing.
- **Source:** `replica.ts:349-382,445-475`; `join.ts:800-835`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Stay ready beyond original assembly deadline, then reconnect. Unlike join/audience/resync, reconnect does not reset askedAt/retry state. A tick before resume manifest treats it overdue, sends a fresh join and discards old handle/ledger continuity; an exhausted ladder can fence immediately.
- **Guard check / repair:** Open-epoch fencing prevents stale stream opens, not stale replica timing. Initialize each reconnect transition's clock/ladder. Distinct from October's repaired in-flight-open race.

### STORE-09 — Handler wrappers hide thenables from the synchronous transaction guard

- **Severity / status:** Medium transaction integrity; source-established rejection-contract bypass.
- **Source:** `owner.ts:687,735-739,1037-1054,1102-1107,224-226`; guard `core.ts:201-226`.
- **Trigger / impact:** An async input handler writes before awaiting. Its wrapper discards the returned promise; transact sees undefined and commits the prefix instead of rejecting the forbidden thenable. Later continuation finds an invalid context. An action output parser can similarly convert the handler promise into a valid output and hide it.
- **Guard check / repair:** Escaped contexts cannot write later, but initial writes already committed. Inspect raw handler returns before discarding/validation. This enforces documented synchronous handlers, not a request to add async support.

### STORE-10 — Failed host initialization leaves its address permanently reserved

- **Severity / status:** Low/medium; source-established construction rollback gap.
- **Source:** `host.ts:381-434`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Host reserves its address before owner/visibility/initial-state validation throws. Corrected retry on that same transport/address is refused already-hosted; no handle was returned to release it.
- **Guard check / repair:** Normal shutdown releases addresses but is unreachable after construction failure. Publish ownership after successful initialization or unwind every exception.

### STORE-11 — Failed join encoding leaves acquired schedules/listener alive

- **Severity / status:** Low/medium; source-established construction resource leak.
- **Source:** `join.ts:491,536-615`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Initial audience/key/store request cannot encode. Event subscription and three schedules have already been installed before replica.join throws; typed wrapping returns an error but no cleanup handle, retaining listeners/timers across repeated corrected attempts.
- **Repair / provenance:** Validate/encode before acquiring resources or unwind them on failure. September #15 repaired the typed encoding error, not constructor cleanup.

### STORE-12 — Reply streams survive the last departed/refused peer handle

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-established known October resource-lifetime residual.
- **Source:** `host.ts:436-498,702-729,793-814`; direct stream retention `net/crates/net/browser-ts/src/node.ts:505-514`; `stream.ts:317-338` under browser-ts/src.
- **Trigger / impact:** Serve distinct peers over time and let them leave/expire; refused joins can also create response streams. Owner handles/ledgers disappear, but cached reply wrappers/native callbacks/buffered traffic remain until host shutdown. Historical peer count, not live-handle caps, bounds the leak.
- **Guard check / repair:** October's pre-consumer buffer cap limits each stream, not number/lifetime. Reclaim after last peer handle with in-flight-emission ownership. Do not recount the repaired unbounded-per-buffer root as new.

### STORE-13 — Host network shutdown never closes its authoritative core

- **Severity / status:** Medium lifecycle/retired-state integrity; source-established missing core retirement.
- **Source:** `host.ts:764-818,866-880`; `owner.ts:1433-1443`; core closure `core.ts:415-422`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Await host.close then call setState/setEntity. Network serving/emissions stop, but public setters still mutate the live owner core and authoritative subscriptions remain registered. farewell removes handles without activating core's closed-write fence.
- **Repair / boundary:** Close owner core/reject retired writes while retaining any promised last-snapshot reads. This is not network reopening.

### STORE-14 — Failed transport sends leave replica status ready/non-stale

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-established false state-health projection.
- **Source:** `join.ts:380-393,645-658,712-736`; ready tick `replica.ts:437-447`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Reach ready, then host vanishes without farewell (e.g. local node closes). Input send fails; fail rejects current waiters but does not alter replica state. With no new host frames, ready remains non-stale, ready() resolves and inputs remain admitted indefinitely.
- **Guard check / repair:** Terminal protocol notices transition state, send errors do not; recovery tick is inactive while ready. Mark transport failure stale/non-ready and fence input until explicit recovery, without replaying ambiguous actions.

### STORE-15 — Projection helpers discard valid own __proto__ entries

- **Severity / status:** Low/medium integrity; source-established unsafe map reconstruction.
- **Source:** `visibility.ts:247-264`; `owner.ts:1639-1653`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Store an own-data entity key __proto__, preserved by core writers/parser. Interest filtering or visibility rebuild assigns it into {}, invoking its prototype setter instead of defining an entry. Entity disappears from serialized projection and maps may become non-plain.
- **Guard check / repair:** Other parsers/writers preserve these keys correctly. Use null-prototype maps or own-property definitions here too. Arbitrary entity IDs are involved; no global prototype-pollution claim.

### STORE-16 — Public setters bypass read-only callback fences

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium trusted-callback contract concern.
- **Source:** `core.ts:234-266,285-286,405-411,428-440,546-549`; `owner.ts:1129-1150,1594-1605,1910-1933`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A trusted authorization/projection/read-only callback calls a public host setter. Only context setters check read-only depth; public setters and owner callback fencing are incomplete, permitting mutation during supposedly read-only or subsequently refused operations.
- **Boundary / repair:** This is not attacker-supplied callback execution. Centralize setter fencing and wrap every promised read-only callback; clarify allowed reentrancy.

### STORE-17 — Subscriber writes during transaction publication are silently lost

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium reentrant subscriber integrity concern.
- **Source:** `core.ts:220-226,259-264,474-486`.
- **Trigger / impact:** During action commit publication, an authoritative subscriber calls host.setState. The transaction is still active, so it changes staged state after that state already committed; finally ends the transaction without another commit. Accepted reentrant write disappears.
- **Repair / qualification:** Define/reject or safely queue publication-time writes rather than accepting lost state. Trusted subscriber behavior, not remote context escape.

### STORE-18 — Invalid audience encoding changes the replica before rejecting locally

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium known September #47 contract residual.
- **Source:** `replica.ts:304-338`; `join.ts:738-750`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Invalid audience names update desired audience, retire installation and clear view before encoding throws. Reconnect restates the invalid desired audience until another audience call repairs it.
- **Qualification / repair:** Immediate clearing for a valid audience transition is deliberate; mutation on encode refusal is the recorded unresolved boundary. Validate before modifying live transition state.

### STORE-19 — Last-waiter cancellation does not fence a lower-level joining replica

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium exported-helper residual; ordinary joinStore trigger not established.
- **Source:** `replica.ts:392-396`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Cancel the final waiter while the lower-level replica remains joining. The cancellation guard excludes that phase, so a later manifest may still install.
- **Qualification / provenance:** Known September #48. Default unanswered-join ladder normally terminates before the public transition deadline; no independent ordinary joinStore deadline failure was established. Define/fence joining cancellation at the supported helper boundary.

### STORE-20 — Host-player audience/refusal recovery differs from replica parity

- **Severity / status:** Qualified low/medium local-view lifecycle concern, not host-player confidentiality.
- **Source:** `player.ts:129-154,182-187,256-271`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Denied setAudience keeps prior audience/view ready rather than clearing/fencing like remote replicas. Conversely, read revocation during refresh permanently sets ended and prevents later permitted audience recovery on that local handle.
- **Boundary / repair:** Hosting page already owns the authoritative document. Clarify/align the advertised parity without treating projection as a security boundary against that page.

### STORE-21 — Multiple local host-player handles share non-reference-counted presence

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium local-handle support concern.
- **Source:** `owner.ts:1186,1197-1199,1223-1226`; `player.ts:168-179`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Create two host-player handles; close one. A boolean/set presence entry disappears and can emit leave while the other stays active; an application leave hook may remove that player's entity/inventory.
- **Qualification / repair:** No singular-handle restriction/guard was found, but intended multiple-local-handle support is unestablished. Reference-count presence or enforce/document one handle.

### STORE-22 — Join cleanup waits for courtesy leave delivery before local retirement

- **Severity / status:** Qualified resource-lifetime concern; indefinitely pending default direct/local witness not established.
- **Source:** `join.ts:837-872`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A structural transport's leave stream/send remains pending; listener removal, outstanding settlement and stream/core cleanup all await it and cannot complete. Reconnect can also place peer establishment ahead of cleanup.
- **Guard check / repair:** Default direct sends are synchronous under Promise wrapping and local sends settle promptly, so indefinite production-connect retention is not asserted. Perform local cleanup before bounded/best-effort courtesy delivery and define the remote completion barrier separately.

Store exclusions/coverage: Lost-manifest recovery, revision-gap resync, generation/foreign-incarnation fences, chunk identity/size/depth and reserved-byte accounting, replay binding and digest-path rebinding have current guards. Unknown action outcome/result-expired do not authorize automatic retry. Periodic tick still settles ready waiters after fencing; no permanent late-ready wedge was established. Authenticated event identity and per-handle peer binding prevent inspected spoofing. Read requests now retain original join keys, repairing historical loss. Direct stream close preserves cursor/arrival continuity; no sibling shared-label-close defect was established. Design-only/unimplemented types were not treated as missing shipped behavior. All 20 store files and necessary direct/local consumers were reviewed; no tests/builds/browser/network/runtime executions were run.

## Eighth audit pass

Four source audits completed: native cryptographic session establishment, public capability discovery/propagation, composed NetDB lifecycle and language consumers, and anchor credential/tenant ingress. The parent checked the Rust SDK tool attribute macro boundary and representative returned source evidence. This pass adds 24 numbered concerns plus explicitly cross-referenced known residuals; findings include exact sources, guard/impact qualifications and historical overlap. No production edits, builds, tests or runtime executions were performed.

### MACRO-01 — Tool macro's advertised arbitrary error type conflicts with generated registration

- **Severity / status:** Qualified low/medium documented compile-contract mismatch; no compilation reproduction run.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk-macros/src/lib.rs:162-167,247-256,278-324`; consuming bound `net/crates/net/sdk/src/tool.rs:323-332`.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** Use the documented async fn(Req)->Result<Resp,E> with an error type other than String. The macro extracts Resp and passes the original function directly to serve_tool; that API requires exactly Future<Output=Result<Resp,String>>. Generated registration does not adapt E, so an otherwise documented tool cannot compile even if only its descriptor is wanted.
- **Boundary / repair:** Canonical Result<Resp,String> examples work. Either implement a defined error conversion with appropriate bounds or narrow/enforce the macro's documented contract; do not claim arbitrary errors already work.

### MACRO-02 — Generated absolute SDK paths do not respect Cargo dependency renaming

- **Severity / status:** Qualified low compatibility concern; no compilation reproduction run.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk-macros/src/lib.rs:231-232,247-253`; public re-export `net/crates/net/sdk/src/lib.rs:106-118`.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** A consumer gives package net-mesh-sdk a valid Cargo alias and imports its tool macro through that alias. Generated descriptor/register functions still reference ::net_sdk, which is not the renamed dependency's external name, so expansion cannot resolve the SDK path.
- **Boundary / repair:** Default dependency naming is unaffected; renamed-dependency support is not expressly documented. Resolve the invoking dependency name or offer an explicit crate-path override, or state this limitation. No extra build/check was run to claim a compiler diagnostic.

Macro coverage/exclusions: Attribute parsing, generated metadata/registration, request/response extraction and serving bounds were inspected. Extra-argument/generic/unsafe signatures were not promoted as missing features without a supported contract. No runtime bug, implicit serialization fallback, or automatic error coercion was inferred.

### NETDB-01 — Python NetDB context helper calls an incompatible native factory

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-confirmed public signature mismatch, not runtime-executed.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk-py/src/net_sdk/netdb.py:51-55`; keyword-only native factory `net/crates/net/bindings/python/src/cortex.rs:2509-2524`; matching stub `python/net/_net.pyi:597-604` under that binding.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** Enter documented netdb_cm(redex, origin_hash=..., with_tasks=True). Helper passes redex positionally, while native NetDb.open accepts only keyword options and no Redex argument. Argument binding raises TypeError before constructing/yielding a database.
- **Guard check / repair:** Direct keyword-only opens work; cortex is default-enabled in Python. Tests replace the real factory with a stub accepting _redex (`net/crates/net/sdk-py/tests/test_wrapper_modules.py:114-125`), masking the mismatch. Reconcile helper/callers/docs with the actual constructor or deliberately add shared-manager support end-to-end.

### NETDB-02 — Closing a Go bundled adapter shuts the supposedly independent parent model

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium ownership-contract inconsistency.
- **Source:** `go/netdb.go:130-150`; cloned shared Arc `net/crates/net/src/ffi/cortex.rs:3355-3357,3386-3388`; native close `1700-1711,2335-2346`; Go calls `go/cortex.go:514-520,809-815`; shared closed flag `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/cortex/adapter.rs:394-402`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Get a=db.Tasks(), close a, get another db.Tasks() handle and create a task. Accessor succeeds, but it targets the same already-closed model and ingest rejects it. Memories is equivalent, despite docs saying child close/free does not affect NetDb.
- **Guard check / repair:** Freeing only the accessor handle is independent. Shared shutdown may be intended; distinguish release from model close or correct the independent-close contract rather than changing Arc ownership blindly. Existing test covers parent Free survival, not child Close isolation (`go/netdb_test.go:172-207`).

### NETDB-03 — Private native NetDB managers expose no explicit file durability barrier

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium opt-in persistence/API gap, not a promised adapter-close fsync.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/bindings/node/src/cortex.rs:2223-2226,2251,2286,2378-2388`; Python `net/crates/net/bindings/python/src/cortex.rs:2498-2502,2525,2571,2650-2657`; `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/redex/config.rs:7-28,205-210`; file Drop `redex/file.rs:73-95` under the same adapter/net directory.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** Open persistent Node/Python NetDb, append/wait for materialization, then close. Native objects retain only adapters from a privately constructed manager; close stops folding without syncing its file. Default Never requires explicit file sync/close, but that actual barrier is inaccessible through this NetDb API. A power/kernel crash before writeback can lose the unsynced tail.
- **Guard check / repair:** Ordinary reopen need not lose OS-page-cached writes. Core adapter close explicitly leaves files open and does not claim durability. Rust exposes redex(), Go owns a supplied Redex; those callers can sync. Expose enabled-file sync or retain/expose the manager without requiring unsafe competing managers. Distinct from TSDK-01 discarded promises.

NetDB existing-root consumer expansions (no additional numbering):

- **CTX-04:** Core NetDbBuilder prevalidates enabled model states (`net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/netdb/db.rs:305-313`), but native/FFI whole-bundle restore only decodes the outer bundle then calls typed restore: Node `net/crates/net/bindings/node/src/cortex.rs:2283-2332`, Python `net/crates/net/bindings/python/src/cortex.rs:2559-2607`, C `net/crates/net/src/ffi/cortex.rs:3173-3223` through `go/netdb.go:90-128`. These callers reach the existing checkpoint-before-embedded-validation root; “NetDB prevalidates” is limited to the core builder, not every language composition.
- **CTX-03:** Node second-model opens at `bindings/node/src/cortex.rs:2265-2271,2312-2329` and Python at `bindings/python/src/cortex.rs:2532-2538,2589-2605` (under net/crates/net/) propagate failure without explicitly closing a successfully opened tasks adapter. An inaccessible dropped wrapper retains its fold worker through the already recorded ownership root. Core rollback (`src/adapter/net/netdb/db.rs:270-274,361-365`) and C rollback (`src/ffi/cortex.rs:3225-3227,3238-3240`) are present.

NetDB exclusions: Cross-model atomic snapshot/restore is expressly not guaranteed. Disabled bundle entries are intentionally ignored; missing enabled entries use normal destination recovery. Persistent origin/version checks and foreign-snapshot replay-position reset exist. No-model native opens are deliberately valid, unlike the stricter Rust builder. Native close's early-return shape has no current error trigger because core adapter close always succeeds. RYW/error/origin/channel forwarding guards were traced. First-open Redex configuration ownership is documented. No tests/builds/runtime executions were run.

Public discovery locations below are relative to `net/crates/net/`; ordinary native/default reachability is distinguished from opt-in verified ownership.

### DISC-01 — Accepted capability announcements can exceed the actual packet limit

- **Severity / status:** Medium availability; source-established admission/propagation mismatch.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:46199-46223,46539-46574,46685-46704,45612-45648`; builder `wire/src/pool.rs:296-365`; receive bounds `wire/src/protocol.rs:32-55,576-582` and `parsed_packet.rs:29-39`; accepted tags `src/adapter/net/behavior/fold/tag_dictionary.rs:28-40`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Announce a valid set with a 9,000-byte nonreserved metadata value or enough short tags to exceed the roughly 8-KiB packet ceiling. Preflight checks tag/heat limits, not the fully augmented signed size. Baseline/self-fold commit, then the whole announcement is one event/packet. UDP may accept it while native receivers reject the over-cap header; API returns success but peers never discover it through refresh/late push either.
- **Guard check / repair:** Stream fragmentation is not used here. Reject complete signed wire shape before mutation or implement end-to-end announcement fragmentation. Native default flow; no RTC/org/sensing needed. The scale plan raises acceptance to 8,192 tags without resolving this wire mismatch.

### DISC-02 — Fold discovery silently ignores model context-length and modality filters

- **Severity / status:** Medium; known source-documented public false-positive gap.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/behavior/fold/capability_bridge.rs:62-75,126-153,165-225,1556-1573`; canonical matcher `behavior/capability.rs:3205-3228` under adapter/net; public callers `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:48159-48161`, `sdk/src/mesh.rs:1561-1563`; preserved tags `src/adapter/net/behavior/tag_codec.rs:559-575,621-626`.
- **Trigger / impact:** A text-only short-context node—or one without a model—matches with_min_context or require_modality(Image) on the fold-backed path although canonical matches rejects it. Scoped/selection consumers of the bridge inherit the omission.
- **Guard check / repair:** Populated filters disable the fast path but the remaining matcher still omits them. Canonical tags retain those axes. Evaluate them consistently; the comment's advice to keep a legacy index is obsolete because that index was removed. Not a TypeScript parser issue or remote authorization bypass.

### DISC-03 — Expired held announcements retain an obsolete version high-water

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-established late-join discovery loss and cache retention.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:42477-42502,42876-42898,27010-27086,15386,14820-14828`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Hub holds B's version 100; it expires. B restarts under retained identity with lower versions. When those are otherwise admitted (e.g. after dedup expiry), held replacement still requires a numerical increase and retains expired 100. New attachers get neither expired record nor fresh lower versions until another flood reaches them or B exceeds 100. Local fold recovery does not repair the replay cache.
- **Guard check / repair:** Replay filters expiry but never removes its payload/high-water; churned expired origins remain allocated/scanned. Remove expired records and separately define needed replay/incarnation tombstones. Default native; missing discovery needs a late attacher. DISC-04 can additionally mask low versions earlier in restart, but is another dedup owner.

### DISC-04 — Direct announcement dedup survives replacement sessions with reset publisher versions

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-established native extension of a historically observed restart root.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:42215-42230,42309-42388,14791-14828,15386,27047-27064,3787-3792`.
- **Trigger / impact:** R receives B versions 1..N, then failure removes B's pin/fold entries. B restarts with the same identity and a new session, emitting overlapping versions with fresh signatures/time/content. The origin/version/directness cache drops them before pin/discovery restoration, delaying recovery until an unseen version or default 600-second dedup retention/60-second sweep.
- **Guard check / repair:** Direct-versus-forwarded key separation fixes forwarded poisoning, not two direct incarnations. Scope direct dedup to session incarnation or define restart-safe publication identity; retain forwarded replay protection. Related known version-reset fragility in `docs/internal/spikes/S5_REPORT.md:2324-2334`, not May 18 F-3's different direct/forwarded collision.

### DISC-05 — Public verified owner projections discard certificate expiry

- **Severity / status:** Medium stale verified metadata; opt-in emission, no remote authorization bypass.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/behavior/fold/capability_bridge.rs:410-443,484-495,578-616,1500-1507`; projection `fold/capability.rs:225-252` in the same behavior directory; exported-provider consumer `src/adapter/net/mesh_rpc.rs:7721-7728,7756-7782`; certificate validity `behavior/org.rs:625-639` under adapter/net.
- **Trigger / impact:** Ingest an owner-certified public service shortly before certificate expiry with normal 300-second announcement TTL. Projection keeps member/org/generation but no deadline. After natural expiry, public owner/provider queries still report verified ownership until announcement expiry/change; no revocation-floor callback fires merely for time.
- **Guard check / repair:** Signature/member/node/pin/floor checks remain effective. Retain/check ownership expiry without necessarily removing public capabilities. emit_owner_cert defaults false (`mesh.rs:3816`). Analogous July private-record expiry fix is another projection consumer.

### DISC-06 — Chain baseline helpers can overwrite a concurrent explicit replacement

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium baseline replacement contract concern.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:47060-47066,47099-47104,45813-45821,46271-46300`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Helper captures X under baseline_rmw, explicit announce on another thread commits Y without that mutex, then helper submits the entire X-plus-chain snapshot and loses Y.
- **Qualification / repair:** Helper helpers serialize with each other; the repaired keep-alive/change loop uses current-baseline None and is not accused. If overlapping explicit replacement may intentionally discard helper work, define that contract. Otherwise mutate the current baseline under the shared announce critical section. Adjacent to historical RT-3 stale-snapshot findings.

Discovery exclusions: Entry/reverse membership/bitmap/dictionary/expiry-wheel updates are coordinated; no additional index-count divergence was established. Up-to-500-ms sweeper visibility is documented. TOFU pins require verified direct identity/node binding; unsigned discovery is opt-in and not verified ownership. Reserved metadata is stripped only after forwarding signature-covered bytes. Current coalesced publication/security correction and deferred-generation fences are present; obsolete bare-start comments do not establish a live dropped-flush bug. Receipt-relative direct TTL is explicitly retained/tested (`tests/capability_multihop.rs:649-683`); it was not relabeled as new authorization behavior. No runtime executions were run.

Native-session locations below are relative to `net/crates/net/`. Security findings remain in this local report; no exploit/network execution or public disclosure was performed.

### NHS-01 — Packet and route-hop keys derive from handshake hash rather than secret Noise Split keys

- **Severity / status:** High cryptographic concern; key-source defect source-established, observer consequences [INFERENCE], not reproduced.
- **Source:** `wire/src/crypto.rs:352-425,1048-1070`; native consumers `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:24760-24769,30505-30517,51862-51897,52377-52393`; pinned Snow version `Cargo.lock:5493-5507`.
- **Mechanism:** Export reads get_handshake_hash, constructs/discards transport state, then derives both packet and route-hop directions solely from that hash. These are not Noise's DH-derived Split outputs.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** A PSK-holding relay/observer sees the NKpsk0 exchange. In pinned Snow 0.10.0, PSK mixes into hash before DH; later DH changes chaining/cipher keys, while later hash inputs are public ephemeral keys and wire ciphertext. The observer can reconstruct the final hash without endpoint private keys and reproduce traffic/hop keys. Later PSK disclosure similarly exposes recorded exchanges, losing DH forward-secrecy protection.
- **Guard check / repair:** Handshake authentication still works; an observer without PSK is not shown to reconstruct the hash. Signed org/subnet authorization is independent and signature forgery is not claimed. Use secret Noise Split/exporter material for keys and keep transcript hash for channel binding; directional labels cannot repair the root.
- **Dependency evidence:** Locally cached pinned Snow `src/symmetricstate.rs:47-94,117-130` separates mix_key from hash and hashes ciphertext; `src/params/patterns.rs:331-335,534-543` defines NK and leading psk0. Parent checked those sources as well. No exact prior transcript-as-traffic-key finding was located.

### NHS-02 — Anonymous NK initiators appear identical at the routed same-static replacement gate

- **Severity / status:** High session-ownership/availability; known rotation-repair residual, protected-authorization bypass not established.
- **Source:** `wire/src/crypto.rs:17-22,206-233,367-379`; `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:2011-2050,30472-30503,30610-30720`.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** B has responder-created A session. Another PSK-holding participant makes a valid NK initiation claiming A's node ID/routing projection. NK authenticates B, not initiator static identity; both responder remote-static values are unavailable/zero. Equality selects “same peer.” A fresh ephemeral replaces a quiescent incumbent despite recent activity, or a busy incumbent after busy_liveness silence. A's negotiated keys/address are displaced.
- **Guard check / repair:** Prologue/routing-payload agreement authenticates the claimed bytes, not who chose A's ID. Fresh identity proof and session-bound subnet controls remain required (`mesh.rs:37377-37395`). Authenticate initiator/incumbent-authorized replacement; unavailable static keys must not prove equality.
- **Provenance:** Related May 3 mesh #3/#6, audit #26 and August 10 cross-subsystem rotation review, not a new introduction claim.

### NHS-03 — Invalid routed response consumes pending initiation before Noise verification

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-established unauthenticated establishment denial.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:29865-29885,30425-30435,50910-50938,50958-50973,51030-51047`; correct direct contrast `51966-51992`.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** While A connects via B, send a syntactically valid local routed HANDSHAKE naming B's routing source with invalid Noise bytes. Dispatch removes B's pending entry before reading/verifying, sends the error to its waiter and discards state. One datagram terminates the attempt; repeats deny the configured retry sequence and genuine response no longer finds its initiator.
- **Guard check / repair:** Noise rejects content after destructive lookup; no adjacent authenticated session/PSK is needed to reach that branch. Direct inbox loops retain state on rejected input. Remove only on owned advancing/terminal transitions. Simultaneous legitimate wrong-role messages can encounter the same branch.

### NHS-04 — Old routed attempt cleanup can remove a successor's pending state

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-established stale-cleanup race.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:4520-4530,30425-30435,50923-50938,50952-50970`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Dispatch removes A's pending entry and delivers its error; before A resumes, another connect_via for that peer inserts C into the vacant slot. A's error/channel-close branch unconditionally removes by routing key, deleting C's Noise state and closing C's result channel.
- **Guard check / repair:** Registration rejects simultaneous occupied entries, not this vacancy-to-successor schedule. PendingInitiator Drop already performs identity-conditional cleanup; explicit branches bypass it. Fence every removal by attempt identity/generation. NHS-03 can supply the error, but stale cleanup is separate ownership.

### NHS-05 — Handshake publication can repopulate sessions after node shutdown

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-established terminal lifecycle publication race.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:24760-24769,50958-50973,51030-51064,25444-25477,25502-25617,53186-53230,53270-53305`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Valid response has reached an externally owned connect/connect_via future, which pauses. Another task completes shutdown/retirement/task draining. The connection future resumes and shared install publishes a fresh unretired session/index entry and can return success after teardown.
- **Guard check / repair:** Native accept selects shutdown while waiting (`25896-25905`), but commit has no terminal check; a pre-wait check alone still races. Serialize session publication against shutdown's lifecycle boundary. No post-shutdown packet delivery is claimed; the demonstrated issue is live-looking state/success after teardown.

### NHS-06 — Default native builds omit displaced-session receive retirement

- **Severity / status:** Medium; source-established feature-boundary incarnation cutoff gap.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:530-538,25669-25675,30687-30695,34508-34516,31060-31075`; `wire/src/session.rs:1996-2016`; default feature sets `Cargo.toml:108-120,227-240`.
- **Trigger / impact:** In default non-webrtc native build, ingress holds the old session Arc while another task replaces it. The helper that marks receive lifetime retired/deactivates is compiled out. Old captured ingress later passes the unconditional retirement guard and can dispatch old-key authenticated application/control data after successor installation.
- **Guard check / repair:** The session flag/guard exists without WebRTC and shutdown retires unconditionally. Retire lifetimes unconditionally; gate only RTC reassembly disposal. Adjacent NR3/leaf fixes operate with WebRTC enabled, not this default-native branch.

### NHS-07 — Direct responder exits after a lost msg2, defeating initiator retransmissions

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium UDP robustness concern; one-shot responder contract acknowledged.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:52377-52393,51879-51905,30089-30102`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Responder sends msg2 once and reports completion, but UDP loses it. Initiator retries identical msg1; responder has exited and the started node discards unsolicited direct handshakes (or another accept expects a different prologue). One lost reply yields responder success versus initiator timeout despite configured three attempts.
- **Repair / qualification:** Existing same-msg1 retries handle delayed responders, not lost replies. Consider a bounded initiation-keyed reply cache without reinstalling sessions. No loss-specific historical match found; no stronger reliable-handshake guarantee or runtime reproduction is asserted.

Native-session known roots, not additional numbered entries:

- Latest-only routed replay memory (`mesh.rs:2022-2044,30697-30720`) still lets captured valid E1 replay after a later E2 establishment once quiescent. Replaying captured bytes needs no PSK; B can install keys A never negotiated. Busy/live protection is temporary. Historical May 3 #26 already covers the replay class; replacement needs real freshness/authorization, not remembering one ephemeral.
- Routed responder lacks direct HandshakePacer (`mesh.rs:30444-30477` versus `52313-52332`), allowing unauthenticated bounded packets to request DH/log work on the receive task. Historical #26 pacing residual; saturation was not measured.
- Failed data authentication still leads to session.touch (`mesh.rs:30149-30152`, source-independent session lookup `30106-30122`; eviction `34404-34406`; `wire/src/session.rs:1951-1972`). Observed session IDs can keep failed peers from eviction without keys. Authenticated detector health/route refresh remains guarded; this is the known `docs/internal/misc/NET_ISSUE_PEERS_EXPIRE.md:215-223` retention root, not new false Healthy status.

Native-session exclusions: Large replay-window jumps do not make accepted counters replayable; June INFO-37 remains the correct rejection. Supported builders share one atomic TX counter/session pool, replacements create fresh pools, and no cross-builder nonce reuse was established. Astronomical rollover/raw independent-cipher misuse was not promoted. Snow checkpoints failed direct reads; owned direct cleanup and exact PeerRegistrationGuard rollback are present. Credential freshness/session binding prevents treating transport substitution as a proven protected-call bypass. Immutable header authentication and reconstructed nonces hold. No builds/tests/runtime executions were run.

Anchor locations below are relative to `net/crates/net/`. All attack/concurrency scenarios are source-derived [INFERENCE], not executed; no public disclosure was performed.

### ANCHOR-01 — Public bootstrap PSK also authenticates unrestricted native UDP sessions

- **Severity / status:** Qualified high tenant-isolation concern; requires a reachable/addressable mesh UDP socket, not merely HTTPS/RTC exposure.
- **Source:** `cli/src/commands/anchor.rs:1248-1255,1495-1508`; `sdk/src/bootstrap_credential.rs:265-276,430-439`; `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:29417-29442,29865-29884,30444-30477,30631-30766,41626-41642,42009-42040`; `src/adapter/net/rtc/admission.rs:84-91`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Anonymous game credential recipients receive the shared transport PSK and anchor static public key. A custom native client can perform routed establishment directly against reachable UDP dispatch, without an operator waiting in direct accept. UDP-derived admission is admitted/tenant None, which “meets everyone”; held other-game discovery becomes eligible without game enrollment, HTTP-offer budgets or browser invite-deadline enforcement.
- **Guard check / repair:** Needs the genuine PSK and network path; credential does not itself promise/disclose a reachable UDP endpoint. Firewall/loopback binding mitigates; CLI's native default bind is 0.0.0.0:0. Org/subnet/channel authorization remains separate. Distinguish native privileged authentication from a publicly issued browser PSK, or require equivalent enrollment on that ingress. Not a claim that a private PSK was leaked.

### ANCHOR-02 — Enrolled RTC peer can introduce a routed session without its tenant restriction

- **Severity / status:** High tenant-isolation concern; custom admitted protocol client required, not normal stock-leaf behavior. Does not require UDP exposure.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:1170-1177,29865-29884,30631-30766,30900-30932,41626-41642,42862-42902`; `src/adapter/net/rtc/admission.rs:84-110`.
- **Trigger / impact:** After enrolling session A for game A, its client sends a locally addressed routed handshake for another identity X over its RTC DataChannel. Legacy route frames are admitted. derived_admission preserves provisional status only for denied/unenrolled RTC ingress; admitted ingress becomes default admitted/tenant None rather than game A. X may receive other-game replay and publish its own signed announcements across tenants through the attacker-controlled route.
- **Guard check / repair:** A's existing session tenant filter and X's signature/node-ID binding can all be correct; the newly installed logical session lost the restriction. Inherit confinement or independently enroll X, preserving new session-incarnation binding rather than assigning the upstream session ID.
- **Provenance:** Extends the historical provisional-routed-admission repair; that repair prevents unenrolled escalation, but not an already admitted tenant escaping through a new logical peer. Distinct from ANCHOR-01's public-PSK/native trust-domain ingress.

### ANCHOR-03 — Device-bound invitation does not bind the identity of the session it promotes

- **Severity / status:** High identity/issuance/Sybil concern; not a forged grant or another device's credential.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/game_anchor.rs:239-257,968-1004,1138-1176`; `sdk/src/enrollment.rs:908-926`; `sdk/src/mesh_rpc.rs:850-892,4313-4327`; `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:41154-41187,41263-41278,41445-41523,42260-42302`.
- **Trigger / impact:** One legitimate device D signs its reusable same-device join request. A custom client opens provisional sessions with participant identities E1/E2, sends D's identical valid body on each, and uses each session's own bound RPC origin/reply channel. Registry verifies D and binds nonce to D, while promotion binds the call/session/tenant but never compares admitted grant subject D with the participant identity being admitted. The E sessions can later present their own valid signed identities.
- **Guard check / repair:** Nonce reuse by a different device is refused; D's signature/grant remains genuine. Same-call/session-incarnation defenses are present, and global RTC/offer capacity plus configured player caps bound occupancy. Bind the granted device to the promoted participant and subsequent announcement identity. Not multi-instance nonce handling or deliberately expired-invite behavior.

### ANCHOR-04 — Per-game player ceiling is checked before admission without reserving a slot

- **Severity / status:** Medium configured-cap bypass; applies when open_max_players_per_game is enabled, not its default None.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/game_anchor.rs:978-1004,1157-1174`; `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:41215-41222`; `src/adapter/cortex/rpc.rs:2147-2172`; `sdk/src/mesh_rpc.rs:517-551,825-850,5268,4313-4327`.
- **Trigger / impact:** With --open-game-max-players 1 and zero admitted peers, two distinct provisional sessions' join handlers both observe tenant_peer_count 0 before either response causes promotion. Both return admitted outcomes and both promote, exceeding the cap.
- **Guard check / repair:** RPC call budgets are per session; global RTC capacity still bounds total peers and registered games intentionally use different cap policy. Reserve a tenant slot through response/promotion and release it on cancellation, failed/replaced calls and disconnect. A count-only preflight is not an admission transaction.

### ANCHOR-05 — Globally rate-limited requests still create and persist new open games

- **Severity / status:** Medium resource/quota-ordering concern, strongest with lowered aggregate issuance limits.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/game_anchor.rs:568-596,857-881,1100`; `sdk/src/rtc_bootstrap.rs:1438-1476`; `cli/src/commands/anchor.rs:977-985,1555-1566`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Configure --open-games-per-minute 1 and exhaust its allowance. Subsequent requests for distinct valid game IDs call open_game before testing the spent aggregate window: each allocates/persists a slot and initializes its idle timestamp, then returns 429. Refused requests can fill the bounded registry and cause OpenGamesFull for other visitors, with records surviving restart.
- **Guard check / repair:** Per-source HTTP rate limits and fixed registry capacity bound growth. Default aggregate ceiling exceeds capacity, so this is not asserted as a new default quota bypass/unbounded allocation. Make quota and new-game creation transactional, or roll back creations when issuance is refused. Existing state append/rewrite locking addresses a different persistence race.

### ANCHOR-06 — Idle-game reclamation can delete a game that just issued a valid credential

- **Severity / status:** Medium concurrent credential availability defect.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/game_anchor.rs:578-595,601-628,695-698,865-881,885-898,955-966`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Full-registry request snapshots G as idle. Another request gets G's Arc and successfully issues an invite, advancing last_issued. Reclamation later takes the write lock and removes snapshot-listed G and its root lookup without rechecking freshness/incarnation. The fresh unexpired credential is then refused as UNKNOWN_INVITE until the relevant registry state is recreated.
- **Guard check / repair:** Root derivation and state-file serialization can be correct; holding the Game Arc lets issuance race removal. Revalidate/reclaim the same game incarnation atomically with issuance, not just persistence. Distinct from intentionally dropping a truly idle game whose invites have expired.

Anchor known residual, not another numbered concern: Bootstrap shutdown joins the listener but accepted HTTP connection tasks remain detached (`sdk/src/rtc_bootstrap.rs:706-709,1596-1650,1771-1803`). A retained keep-alive connection can retain issuance state/node ownership beyond SDK service shutdown. Header/request deadlines bound inactivity, not task ownership; closing/draining accepted tasks is the relevant fix. This repeats the October 11 SDK audit's accepted-connection shutdown finding (lines 223-243), not a newly counted root. Current trickle handling has deadline/size/supersession bounds (`2405-2442`), so an indefinitely silent trickle worker is not reasserted. CLI process exit mitigates the SDK lifetime case.

Anchor exclusions: Invite MAC domains, signed credential framing/root separation, request device signatures, constant-time verification and same-instance nonce-to-device binding are present. Native Origin spoofability is not itself an anonymous issuance bug; CORS is not authentication. Socket-derived source IP, IPv6 /64 quotas, bounded rate tables, credential validation before HTTP offers, request/body deadlines and RTC capacity remain effective bounds. Cross-instance nonce binding is explicitly deferred, and expired-invite rejection is intentional. No independent exploit was established for origin parsing, nonce probability or conservatively restored idle state. No production changes or runtime executions were made.

## Ninth audit pass

Four source audits completed: continuity/contested reconciliation, CortEX workflow execution, Dataforts gravity/greedy placement, and subprotocol registry/negotiation/migration handling. The parent inspected standalone native proxy forwarding and checked representative returned evidence. This pass adds 26 numbered concerns plus explicitly qualified historical residuals. Findings include current sources, guard checks, supported-consumer boundaries and inference/contract qualifications. No production code changes, builds, tests or runtime/network executions were performed.

### PRX-01 — Standalone proxy ignores packet-size and latency configuration

- **Severity / status:** Qualified low public-configuration contract defect; source-established ignored options, no runtime reproduction.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/proxy.rs:21-39,181-220,254-339,348-409`; `net/crates/net/docs/TRANSPORT.md:188-200`.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** Construct ProxyConfig with max_packet_size below a routed packet's size or track_latency false. Constructor uses bind_addr/local_id and merely stores the rest; forward checks only the routing-header minimum and always measures/records elapsed latency. Valid oversized inputs are still delivered locally or prepared/sent, and disabling latency tracking does not disable its work or samples.
- **Guard check / repair:** UDP's hard datagram limit/caller-supplied receive buffers are not the advertised per-proxy bound. This public component is standalone, not MeshNode's RTC/native dispatch (`tests/rtc_proxy_boundary.rs:13-20,33-51`); no mesh admission bypass is claimed. Either honor these settings at the operation boundary or remove/document unsupported knobs. The packet-size concern is an inferred configuration contract, not a demonstrated exploitable memory limit.

Proxy known residuals, not additional numbered entries:

- The April 30 core audit #116 already included both route churn and zero-route packets growing hop_stats. remove_route now removes its matching stats (`proxy.rs:233-243`), but arbitrary unknown/expired destination IDs still reach record_hop_drop, which allocates permanent map entries (`280-305,455-456`). An in-flight lookup can also recreate stats after route removal. reset_stats only clears global atomics (`431-438`), not these entries. The historical “fixed” label does not close the no-route branch; bound/avoid per-destination accounting for nonexistent routes or give it eviction ownership.
- May 3 #119's send-before-success telemetry repair rolls back global counters on send failure (`proxy.rs:363-393`), but not per-hop counters already updated at `339,448-452`. Thus hop_stats can still report forwards the kernel rejected. Low telemetry-only residual of the same known root, not newly counted.

Proxy exclusions: Post-decrement TTL-zero rejection is explicitly historical policy, not relabeled here as an off-by-one defect. Local routing-envelope removal is intentionally payload-agnostic. Standalone proxy absence of authentication is not evidence that mesh RTC admission reaches it. No additional protected forwarding path or deadlock was established. No tests/network executions were run.

Workflow paths use crate root `net/crates/net/`; shorthand `workflow/` expands to `src/adapter/net/cortex/workflow/`, `cortex/` to `src/adapter/net/cortex/`, and native `node/`/`python/` to `bindings/node/`/`bindings/python/`. Findings are source-only; the parent checked restoration, advancement, empty fan-out and deletion contracts.

### WFLOW-01 — Workflow snapshot imported into a fresh log skips subsequent writes

- **Severity / status:** Medium persistence defect; source-established, supported import shape, no runtime reproduction.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/cortex/workflow/adapter.rs:255-292,552-573`; generic `src/adapter/net/cortex/adapter.rs:320-346,1061-1081`; allocator/notification `src/adapter/net/redex/file.rs:108-127,1693-1702`.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** Snapshot contains source last_seq K, then restore into fresh Redex, exactly as the current workflow regression does. Restore subscribes at K+1 although destination allocates from zero. Its first K+1 new events are excluded from folding, while wait_for_seq on those lower sequences immediately succeeds. For a running task, a newly appended completion can report a satisfied barrier but leave it Running.
- **Guard check / repair:** Same-log restore with matching history and snapshots without a source cutoff avoid this case. Existing regression only compares initial restored state, not writes afterward. Distinguish same-log resume from foreign import/rebase destination coverage; Tasks already has OwnCheckpoint/ForeignSnapshot handling (`cortex/tasks/adapter.rs:505-559`).
- **Provenance:** Workflow-specific missing import counterpart, not CTX-04 checkpoint publication-before-validation or historical skipped-event watermark handling.

### WFLOW-02 — Advancement mutates settled tasks and changes the step later retried

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium lifecycle contract concern; mutation source-established, stale-callback schedule inferred.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/cortex/workflow/fold.rs:68-105`; `workflow/adapter.rs:146-155`; public native forwarding `bindings/node/src/cortex.rs:2585-2591`; external failure observer `src/adapter/net/behavior/scheduler_bridge/driver.rs:108-123`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Running step N is marked Failed, then delayed completion appends advance(id). Advancement unconditionally changes Failed/N to Failed/N+1 and clears attempts; retry now starts N+1 instead of the failed step. Done tasks' cursors also change despite guarded terminal status transitions.
- **Guard check / repair:** Unknown IDs are no-ops. No autonomous in-tree executor producing this delayed callback was established; this is supported public operation ordering, not an observed executor failure. Define advancement's valid states and consider expected cursor/attempt fencing. Explicit retry is allowed to leave Failed, not Done.
- **Provenance:** Residual of terminal-state correction #2 in `docs/internal/plans/MESH_SCHEDULER_CODE_REVIEW.md`: transition/retry hardening did not cover advance. Distinct from CTX-01 dependency pruning.

### WFLOW-03 — Empty fan-out's zero sentinel can hang the normal sequence-barrier composition

- **Severity / status:** Qualified low API-composition concern; empty return behavior is documented.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/cortex/workflow/shard.rs:98-107,248-258,325-359,430-434`; `sdk/tests/workflow_surface.rs:68-72`; generic barrier `src/adapter/net/cortex/adapter.rs:320-346`.
- **Trigger / impact:** On a fresh workflow, a zero-partition map calls fan_out, then wait_for_seq on its return value, following normal SDK usage. fan_out appends nothing but returns zero; the barrier waits for a nonexistent first event until an unrelated append. Caller never reaches the already join-ready empty reducer.
- **Guard check / repair:** Documentation explicitly says zero for no shards, so this is not an unconditional undocumented guarantee violation; callers can check emptiness. No-op propagate_failure/parentless block_on_failure share the sentinel issue. Represent no append separately from valid sequence zero, e.g. Option<u64>. No exact historical match found.

Workflow native-consumer residual, not another numbered root: Rust deletion explicitly requires reading the subtree and calling TriggerEngine::on_delete for each ID (`workflow/adapter.rs:157-164`, `workflow/trigger.rs:272-280`). Node (`bindings/node/src/cortex.rs:2810-2933`), Python (`bindings/python/src/cortex.rs:4072-4161`) and C (`include/net_cortex.h:226-265`) expose no trigger deletion/disarm operation; native workflow delete only forwards the task event (`node/src/cortex.rs:2605-2612`, `python/src/cortex.rs:3881-3886`, `src/ffi/cortex.rs:3681-3688`). An AfterTask(A) dependency survives deletion and can fire if A is resubmitted/completed. Whole-engine disposal loses unrelated dependencies; absence during evaluation does not prune. Unclosed consumer boundary of historical scheduler correction #4's on_delete repair, not newly counted shallow deletion.

Workflow exclusions: CTX-01/02/05/06/09/10 and GANG-04/05/06 already cover dependency pruning, reducer admission, conflicting lineage, overlapping shard streams, partial reducer publication, max tick, publication/claim authorization and stale release. No repeat entries. Cancellation persistence until deletion/fresh submit is explicit. fan_out promises neither idempotence nor transactional replay. Trigger definitions/results/action delivery intentionally live outside workflow snapshots; no exactly-once guarantee inferred. Native callers must record results before evaluating result triggers. Conflicting caller-supplied topology was not promoted separately. All seven event tags have matching decoders/folds. Task metadata deletion does not itself cancel external execution or release claims. No builds/tests/runtime executions were run.

Placement paths use crate root `net/crates/net/`; shorthand `dataforts/` expands to `src/adapter/net/dataforts/`, `greedy/`/`gravity/` to that subsystem's corresponding directories, and `redex/` to `src/adapter/net/redex/`. Internal design-document paths are repository-relative. Seven concerns follow, with known ownership/heat limitations separated below. All scenarios are source-derived, not reproduced.

### PLMT-01 — Enabling greedy creates a strong manager/runtime ownership cycle

- **Severity / status:** Medium resource retention; high-confidence source mechanism.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/redex/manager.rs:125-150,203-207,397-427`; `dataforts/greedy/runtime.rs:148-155,338-377`; C release `src/ffi/cortex.rs:387-400`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Redex owns GreedyWiring → runtime → Arc<Redex>, while wiring also retains mesh. Dropping the application's enabled manager without explicitly disabling greedy cannot run wiring Drop, retaining files/mesh and potentially the periodic gravity task. The C free path drops its Arc without breaking this cycle.
- **Guard check / repair:** disable_greedy_dataforts breaks the cycle; Drop's observer removal/task abort cannot help until the cycle is broken. Use weak/non-owning manager access or independent file-opening ownership with deterministic teardown. Different from the historical replication dispatcher cycle and BLOB-05 eviction storage leak.

### PLMT-02 — Greedy's per-channel retention cap lacks a trim driver

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium integration/resource-limit concern; external retention driver mitigates.
- **Source:** `dataforts/greedy/runtime.rs:814-865`; `src/adapter/net/redex/file.rs:470-534,1329-1343`; `redex/manager.rs:397-427,1025-1031`; explicit-sweep test `dataforts/greedy/cache.rs:655-686`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Enable greedy with default 100-MiB per-channel / 10-GiB aggregate limits, then continuously cache more than the channel cap. Dispatch sets retention_max_bytes when opening, but append does not sweep; greedy installs no retention driver. Without application sweeps, the channel grows until aggregate eviction or another substrate bound rather than its per-channel setting.
- **Guard check / repair:** Manager documents sweep_retention as an owning-runtime heartbeat obligation, and applications can supply it. Therefore this is a missing integrated driver/prerequisite, not a claim that configured retention never works. Install/own the sweep and reconcile accounting, or clearly require an external driver. D-26 covers drift after trimming, not missing trimming; BLOB-05's backing-storage eviction leak is separate.

### PLMT-03 — Evicting one channel withdraws a shared origin still held by another

- **Severity / status:** Medium discovery availability; source-established ownership mismatch.
- **Source:** `dataforts/greedy/runtime.rs:838-842,906-915,963-984`; `greedy/cache.rs:128-138,250-256,392-402`; `redex/manager.rs:54-94,411-418`; `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:47097-47102,47175-47202,48104-48125`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Cache channels A and B from the same origin. Evict A while B remains. Cleanup directly withdraws that origin's causal advertisement, hiding retained B; B's subsequent writes do not reannounce because it is not newly admitted. Another ownership-aware mesh claimant can be affected too.
- **Guard check / repair:** Cache already maintains origin_counts, but cleanup ignores remaining claims. Replication uses ownership-aware ChannelChainSink; greedy directly uses MeshNode as sink. Use shared mesh-scoped origin ownership per cache incarnation and withdraw only the last claim. Another announcer may eventually restore the tag. Distinct from BLOB-06 concurrent replacement and D-1 missing withdrawal plumbing.

### PLMT-04 — Two colocation declarations share one resolved-target answer

- **Severity / status:** Medium placement correctness; source-established resolution gap.
- **Source:** `dataforts/greedy/runtime.rs:737-750`; `greedy/admission.rs:193-226`; independent standard checks `src/adapter/net/behavior/placement.rs:831-869`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Under StrictRequired, declare strict target A and soft target B; cache holds A but not B. Runtime chooses strict.or_else(soft), then admission uses that one true result for both declarations, admitting despite the unmet B requirement.
- **Guard check / repair:** Both keys are independent requirements under this policy in the standard placement implementation. Single-key resolution works. Resolve/evaluate each target separately. A residual in D-8's resolved-target representation, not its original always-unresolved implementation.

### PLMT-05 — Configured proximity RTT bound is never applied to greedy admission

- **Severity / status:** Medium placement/configuration concern; unused gate source-established.
- **Source:** `dataforts/greedy/config.rs:69-73,138-142,223-225`; `greedy/admission.rs:113-135`; `greedy/runtime.rs:338-377,720-800`; observer `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:32860-32870`; `docs/internal/plans/DATAFORTS_PLAN.md:205-230`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Set a small positive proximity_max_rtt and receive otherwise eligible traffic from a distant publisher. Admission receives no RTT measurement and never reads the configured bound, so it still caches data, potentially evicting intended nearby data.
- **Guard check / repair:** Scope, bandwidth, intent and colocation gates execute but do not enforce this option. Feed measured publisher/home proximity into the gate and define unknown-measurement policy. Parent's reference search found uses only in configuration/validation/tests. No exact historical proximity-bound finding located.

### PLMT-06 — Byte resynchronization double-counts an append awaiting cache accounting

- **Severity / status:** Medium premature eviction; source-established concurrency gap.
- **Source:** `dataforts/greedy/runtime.rs:540-542,847-865`; `greedy/cache.rs:215-224,375-388`; `src/adapter/net/redex/file.rs:361-364,470-534`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Dispatch appends N bytes, then pauses before acquiring cache lock. Resync reads file retained_bytes including N and replaces accounting. Dispatch resumes and adds N again. Phantom usage can cross aggregate cap and evict channels even though actual retained bytes remain below it.
- **Guard check / repair:** Individual file/cache operations are locked, but append→account is not atomic with resync. Later reconciliation cannot undo eviction. Use an append/account watermark or another atomic observation-to-delta relationship. New race in D-26's resync repair, not original retention drift or BLOB-05 storage ownership.

### PLMT-07 — Greedy disable discards blob-reference bookkeeping without balancing external counts

- **Severity / status:** Medium GC retention, conditional on supported operator-installed refcount wiring.
- **Source:** `redex/manager.rs:140-150,503-508`; `dataforts/greedy/runtime.rs:194-215,433-472,1001-1048,1083-1109`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Install shared blob refcount table, cache BlobRefs, finish dispatches, then disable/release greedy. Drop unregisters observer/stops gravity but neither evicts reference generations nor clears the table. Positive external counts remain even if former cache files are explicitly closed, preventing normal GC.
- **Guard check / repair:** Explicit clear_blob_refcount_table balances owned references before teardown; disable does not call it. Automatic table installation was not found in inspected production callers. Quiesce dispatches and balance every owned generation before discarding its table association. BLOB-07/table replacement and BLOB-05/backing files are different lifetimes.

Placement known/qualified residuals, not additional numbered entries:

- BLOB-06's generation class also permits **publication after eviction** (`greedy/runtime.rs:814-865,900,1001-1048,1083-1109`; `greedy/cache.rs:381-388`): an already-running dispatch retains X's file; another dispatch evicts X and finishes reference cleanup; the first appends, note_appended silently finds no cache entry, yet reference recording recreates X's shadow bucket/increments counts. No live entry remains to balance it later. The lazy-open recheck does not fence later eviction. Old-bucket detachment alone cannot fix this reverse schedule; validate/retire the live incarnation through reference publication.
- The bounded shadow's 8,192-hash overflow drops/decrements the oldest even if its Small/Manifest event remains retained (`greedy/runtime.rs:228-236,1029-1047`). With sufficient capacity and elapsed retention floor, content can become GC-eligible while still referenced. This is explicitly B-10's chosen bounded-bookkeeping lifetime tradeoff, not newly counted accidental behavior; related to BLOB-13.
- Retention resync trims only byte accounting (`greedy/runtime.rs:540-542,1001-1048`; `greedy/cache.rs:215-224`), not shadow reference ownership. A small old set can remain counted after event trimming until eviction/clear/overflow. Qualified by channel-lifetime bookkeeping design.
- Gravity counter eviction/reset forgets counters without withdrawing already published heat (`gravity/counter.rs:252-266,496-513`; `greedy/runtime.rs:390-408`). Blob migration consumes heat candidates (`dataforts/blob/migration.rs:403-482`), but ignores numeric rate for admission; stale candidate presence is established, a distinct additional placement/resource failure was not. Not promoted.

Placement exclusions: Existing BLOB-05/06/07/08/13 and historical wire-hash collision D-24 are not recounted. Bandwidth drops/out-of-order speculative caching are explicit behavior. Blob heat normalization differs from chain heat, but inspected migration does not consume rate for its decision. Prefetch tasks escape observer semaphore, yet native prefetch currently opens channels synchronously; unbounded slow custom-adapter accumulation remains an extension-point risk, not an established native failure. No builds/tests/runtime/network executions were run.

Continuity paths use crate root `net/crates/net/`; shorthand core subsystem paths (`continuity/`, `contested/`, `compute/`, `state/`, `subnet/`) expand under `src/adapter/net/`. Ordinary daemon delivery and SDK fork production have supported consumers. No production consumers were established for cone queries/merge, observation windows, propagation models, continuity assessment/proofs or contested classification/reconciliation. Their standalone defects must not be read as demonstrated live partition data loss.

### CONT-01 — Ordinary daemon delivery drops inherited transitive causal dependencies

- **Severity / status:** Medium correctness; high-confidence implementation evidence, qualified causal interpretation.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/compute/host.rs:214-245`; `compute/registry.rs:292-302`; `sdk/src/compute.rs:1115-1132`; `continuity/cone.rs:66-90`; `state/horizon.rs:208-225`.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** B processes A and emits B1 with A's bloom bits. C processes B1 but observes only B's origin/sequence; its outgoing C1 bloom is regenerated from direct observations, not B1's inherited horizon_encoded. For noncolliding masks, C1's cone says No to A despite transitive influence.
- **Guard check / repair:** Per-origin maxima/delivery ordering do not merge inherited bloom; false positives can hide but not guarantee preservation. Under documented causal “could have influenced” semantics, preserve approximate inherited dependencies; do not invent exact inherited sequence knowledge. If direct reception alone is intended, narrow the negative query claim. Unlike STATE-04, no snapshot, serialization or migration is involved.

### CONT-02 — Cone merge loses inputs' implicit same-origin history

- **Severity / status:** Medium standalone API correctness; source-established, no production query caller found.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/continuity/cone.rs:66-82,141-170,275-299`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Exact A2 and A10 cones have empty explicit horizons. merge([A2,A10]) retains first origin/sequence; querying A9 returns No although the A10 input says Definite. Reversing inputs changes the result. Different-origin inputs likewise lose the nonfirst cone's implicit same-origin predecessors.
- **Guard check / repair:** Mixed approximate input correctly drops exactness; it does not repair lost input boundaries. Existing test checks external horizon entries, not each input's own chain. Represent the union's per-origin bounds. Distinct from STATE-02 concurrency and the mixed-exactness repair.

### CONT-03 — Continuity assessment rejects valid fork and explicit-genesis representations

- **Severity / status:** Medium standalone assessment correctness; supported fork producer, no production assessor caller found.
- **Source:** `continuity/chain.rs:325-395`; `continuity/discontinuity.rs:115-143`; `state/log.rs:45-59,107-117`; `compute/fork_group.rs:123-137`; `sdk/src/groups/fork.rs:60-83`.
- **Trigger / impact:** fork_entity supplies a sentinel-parent sequence-zero genesis and valid sequence-one successor. Assess a log containing the successor with its matching fork-genesis snapshot: first_seq==1 unconditionally chooses canonical zero-parent genesis and ignores the valid supplied anchor, reporting Forked. Separately, an accepted explicit empty-payload sequence-zero genesis has no assessment branch and is Unverifiable.
- **Guard check / repair:** Fork record sentinel validation works, but assessor takes no ForkRecord/actual base anchor. Define consistent canonical, explicit and fork genesis anchoring. Adjacent to historical #114, not its missing-history acceptance root or STATE-03 snapshot payload omission.

### CONT-04 — Partial subnet metadata overstates failure concentration

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium standalone classification concern; behavior source-established, interpretation depends on evidence contract.
- **Source:** `contested/correlation.rs:20-23,238-276,304-313`; `contested/partition.rs:133-150,174-185`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Track ten nodes; only failed node 0 has registered subnet metadata; report failures 0–3. Default mass-failure threshold is met, but concentration denominator is with_subnet==1, producing affected_ratio 1.0. Detector accepts SubnetFailure and includes all four failures on its other side despite membership established for only one.
- **Guard check / repair:** Zero metadata yields Unknown; partial coverage does not. Unknown nodes could share the subnet, so this overstates evidence rather than proves their locations differ. Use total failures, minimum coverage or explicit coverage/uncertainty before definitive partition classification. Distinct from PART-06 and deterministic-ordering fixes.

### CONT-05 — GLOBAL subnet registrations become supposedly localized subnet failures

- **Severity / status:** Low/medium standalone classification correctness; complete metadata coverage still affected.
- **Source:** `contested/correlation.rs:123-125,242-264,304-313`; `contested/partition.rs:148-150`; GLOBAL semantics `subnet/id.rs:69-71`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Register nodes as SubnetId::GLOBAL and report enough failures. Ancestry walk counts GLOBAL before checking its parent, yielding SubnetFailure(GLOBAL,1.0); detector treats it as a localized partition despite no locality information.
- **Guard check / repair:** GLOBAL is excluded when reached as parent, not as registered starting value; registration accepts it. Exclude global/no-subnet values as locality evidence. Different from CONT-04's partial coverage; no exact historical root found.

### CONT-06 — Propagation inverse omits valid crossing depth four

- **Severity / status:** Low standalone API consistency; no production model caller found.
- **Source:** `continuity/propagation.rs:53-63,137-149,173-189`; four-level domains `subnet/id.rs:73-79,138-145`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Two depth-four subnets differ at level zero, giving crossing depth four. Default one-hop estimate clamps that valid depth to its final multiplier and gives 50 ms. max_depth_within(50 ms,1) only examines 3..0 and returns three, excluding a depth its own model puts in budget.
- **Guard check / repair:** Clamping prevents bounds failure but forward/inverse domains differ. Use the complete supported depth range and same multiplier rule. Different from historical #60 EWMA calibration.

### CONT-07 — Proof generator emits spans rejected by its own verifier

- **Severity / status:** Low standalone interoperability; source-established producer/verifier mismatch.
- **Source:** `continuity/chain.rs:92,102-124,150-170`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Retain 100,001 consecutive valid events. from_log returns Some spanning them; verify_against on the unchanged log rejects SpanTooLarge because sequence difference is 100,000.
- **Guard check / repair:** Generator documents/enforces no span precondition; EntityLog need not prune below it. Generate bounded/chunked proofs or report explicit generation failure. Keep verifier's deliberate cap. Residual producer mismatch adjacent to historical #98 / CR-13, not an argument to weaken verification bounds; no production verifier consumer found.

Continuity known/qualified residuals, not additional numbered entries:

- Observation temporal maps cap at 65,536, but underlying horizon retains every novel origin (`continuity/observation.rs:64-73,86-101,181-182`; `state/horizon.rs:33-45,62-68`), growing entity_count/encoding/divergence work. Horizon explicitly documents unbounded cardinality. Qualifies May 3 #63's map-bound repair; not a whole-window memory bound or demonstrated live ingress exploit.
- Fully pruned log with nonzero retained base head takes the empty Continuous branch with zero hashes (`continuity/chain.rs:311-320`; `state/log.rs:236-240`); partial pruning instead requires an anchor. Distinguish genuinely fresh empty logs from restored/pruned nonzero anchors. Residual of historical #114, not STATE-05 beyond-head pruning.
- Proof span cap bounds hashing but not EntityLog::range's full-vector filter (`continuity/chain.rs:164-172`; `state/log.rs:125-130`). Tiny proofs still scan total retained history. Sequence-indexed slicing would fix it; caller rate limiting remains explicit and no inbound verifier was found. Qualified #98 / CR-13 cost-bound residual.

Continuity exclusions: STATE-01–05 and PART-04/05/06/09/10/11 already cover snapshot/causal, horizon equality, prune alignment, zero-tracked throttling, losing-fork material, first-parent and overlapping-partition roots. Superposition readiness can become stale on source_advanced/advance, but no production source-advance caller was found and inspected replay immediately collapses; not promoted. Genesis/sequence-one superposition proof limits are explicit/tested historical CR-33. No expiry promise for active unhealed partitions was established. Invalid manually supplied numeric configurations and fork structural lineage were not promoted as new remote security bugs. No tests/runtime executions were run.

Subprotocol/migration notation: `core/` means `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/`; `sdk/` means `net/crates/net/sdk/`. Findings and schedules are source-only, not reproduced. Historical authorization concerns remain local/private.

### SUBP-01 — Migration-handler installation/removal has no registration owner

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium ownership concern; high-confidence source mechanism, multiple runtimes per mesh not explicitly promised.
- **Source:** `core/mesh.rs:21621-21652`; `sdk/src/compute.rs:307-355,668-705`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Start runtime A; construct B on the same mesh without starting B; shut B down. Its unconditional clear removes A's live handler while A stays Ready. Starting both similarly makes the last store win, and either shutdown disables the survivor.
- **Guard check / repair:** Atomic pointer swaps/runtime-local lifecycle prevent torn state, not registration ownership. Enforce a singleton with rejection or return an owner/generation-bearing registration. Analogous to REPL-04's separate slot, not existing MIG-01–04.

### SUBP-02 — Concurrent idempotent startup replaces a handler's partial snapshot state

- **Severity / status:** Medium supported startup race; source-established mechanism, thread schedule inferred.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/compute.rs:499-565`; `core/subprotocol/migration_handler.rs:298-304,365-378,1034-1061`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Two threads observe Registering. A installs a handler and publishes Ready; a chunk arrives into A's reassembler. B, already inside the same branch, later installs its separately built handler before losing the CAS. Subsequent chunks reach B's empty map and lose A's partial transfer.
- **Guard check / repair:** Shared source/orchestrator/target Arcs do not share handler-local reassemblers; the “functionally equivalent” comment overlooks them. Does not require test-only sleep or multiple runtimes. Serialize startup/publish one stable handler instance. No exact historical match found.

### SUBP-03 — Migration wait reports success before target activation acknowledgement

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium completion contract defect; source-established early-return branch.
- **Source:** `core/compute/orchestrator.rs:1578-1594,1607-1649`; `core/compute/migration.rs:237-244`; `sdk/src/compute.rs:2143-2151`; `core/subprotocol/migration_handler.rs:817-898`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Source cleanup sets Complete, but ActivateTarget or its execution is delayed more than 50 ms. SDK wait observes Complete, sleeps 50 ms and returns Ok without rechecking, although the target is unactivated and the record remains awaiting ActivateAck. Failure arriving during that sleep is also not reconsidered.
