- **Guard check / repair:** Orchestrator explicitly names target acknowledgement as the real lifecycle terminus; retained record alone does not fix wait. Separate source-cleanup state from acknowledged success. Different from MIG-02/03 missing-record failure evidence and historical missed phases() transitions.

### SUBP-04 — Final buffered migration events exceed packet limits without chunking

- **Severity / status:** High state-loss concern; source-established producer/transport mismatch.
- **Source:** `core/subprotocol/migration_handler.rs:751-766,809-815`; `core/compute/orchestrator.rs:331-346`; `core/compute/migration_source.rs:19-25`; `core/mesh.rs:31342-31368`; `net/crates/net/wire/src/pool.rs:296-359`; `wire/src/protocol.rs:32-55,577-581` under the same crate root.
- **Trigger / impact:** Two accepted 4096-byte buffered event payloads plus migration/event framing exceed the 8192-byte receiving packet ceiling. Cutover encodes the entire final vector as one event, queues an unfragmented send and unregisters the source before delivery outcome. Receiver cannot accept that final batch; those state changes lack the retired source's recovery copy.
- **Guard check / repair:** SnapshotReady chunking does not cover BufferedEvents. The 64-MiB buffer cap/integer-width encoder checks are not transport-size limits. Chunk by encoded wire size and retain recoverable source state until final replay is acknowledged. Historical #43 fixed snapshot chunking; MDB-02/REPL-01 are different producers at the same boundary class.

### SUBP-05 — Activation overtakes final replay because source retirement has no replay barrier

- **Severity / status:** High state-loss concern; source-established missing barrier, delivery schedule inferred.
- **Source:** `core/subprotocol/migration_handler.rs:751-815,817-889`; `core/mesh.rs:31342-31368`; `core/compute/migration_target.rs:302-330,390-414,449-481`.
- **Trigger / impact:** In supported three-node S→T migration orchestrated by O, S emits final events to T and CleanupComplete to O through independent sends. S→O→T activation wins over S→T final replay. T activates/completes, removing migration state; late replay is rejected because that state no longer exists, while S is already unregistered.
- **Guard check / repair:** One stream's ordering/reliability cannot order these different sessions. Activation drains arrived pending events but has no expected final frontier. Carry and acknowledge the complete final replay range before source retirement/activation completion. Different from repairs for already-arrived replay-phase regression and mid-batch processing errors.

### SUBP-06 — Remote target failure does not unwind the remote source's migration

- **Severity / status:** High operational correctness; source-established missing participant failure routing.
- **Source:** `core/subprotocol/migration_handler.rs:472-475,902-957,1091-1119,1357-1382`; `core/compute/migration_source.rs:144-146,226-293`; `sdk/src/compute.rs:2094-2123`.
- **Trigger / impact:** O orchestrates allowlisted S→T. T rejects restore with FactoryNotFound/NotReady and informs O. O records failure/aborts only local handlers; remote S retains migrating/buffering state. Retry sends TakeSnapshot to S, which returns AlreadyMigrating through an error-only handler path with no structured response, stalling the attempt. A daemon disappearing before remote snapshot can similarly leave a silent pending operation.
- **Guard check / repair:** Local source abort propagation exists, but cannot clear another node's handler. Propagate terminal/retry aborts to all correlated participants with loop prevention; turn request-side handler errors into protocol replies. Extends May 3 #14's local-only repair and related historical failure-direction omissions; not MIG-02 waiter election.

### SUBP-07 — Cancelling awaited migration setup leaves an operation without a returned owner

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium cancellation/lifecycle concern; requires cancellation during awaited submission.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/compute.rs:1443-1534,1670-1698`; `core/mesh.rs:45602-45644`; `core/compute/migration_source.rs:226-293`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Application timeout/task abort drops start_migration_with while initial sending awaits admission, after registration/snapshot setup but before MigrationHandle return. Explicit Err cleanup is bypassed; orchestration/local-source state can remain active and reject a later start as AlreadyMigrating. Partial chunk submission can leave remote pending state too.
- **Guard check / repair:** Returned send errors are cleaned up. Parent also checked that identity-sealing/rechunk errors explicitly abort (`sdk/src/compute.rs:1615-1666`), so those are not another missing-cleanup finding. Transport debit guards restore transport accounting, not migration ownership. Give setup cancellation-safe operation ownership and coordinate cancellation after any transmitted prefix. No permanent-retention duration is asserted. Existing MIG entries concern already returned handles; historical retry-send cleanup covers returned errors.

### SUBP-08 — Migration emissions discard routed node identity and transport-aware framing

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium routed/RTC transport coverage concern; direct UDP paths are not accused.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/compute.rs:1670-1688`; `core/mesh.rs:15622-15626,45545-45555,31336-31365,4389-4426`.
- **Trigger / impact:** SDK resolves destination node to UDP peer_addr and calls the address-based subprotocol primitive, which resolves direct address ownership again. Routed peers expose their relay's send address rather than their own direct ownership; initial emission can select the relay or fail. RTC-only destinations have no UDP address. Core handler responses likewise send a bare end-to-end packet to addr() without the transit wrapper a relay needs, so routed replies do not reach their intended endpoint.
- **Guard check / repair:** Core already has node-addressed submission and wire_route().frame for transit framing. Preserve node identity/transport through SDK and response emission instead of round-tripping via SocketAddr. No new authorization bypass is claimed. This is migration's real supported-consumer boundary, not PRX-01's separate standalone proxy/configuration root; no exact current MIG overlap found.

Subprotocol/migration historical residuals, not additional numbered entries:

- **High: incomplete participant binding (May 18 X-18).** RestoreComplete, ReplayComplete, CutoverNotify, ActivateAck and BufferedEvents lack checks present on adjacent arms (`core/subprotocol/migration_handler.rs:692-739,740-815,892-900,982-1013`; `core/compute/orchestrator.rs:1641-1649`; `core/compute/host.rs:214-225`). An unrelated admitted peer can act on a legitimate active migration: send CutoverNotify with a selected target to drain/retire its source, acknowledge activation to remove the record without peer/phase binding, or inject suitably sequenced replay input. LocalOnly/allowlist gates TakeSnapshot, not these arms; session authentication does not authorize migration participation. Complete the participant/phase matrix, including legitimate direct source→target final replay. These are known authorization-class residuals, not public disclosure or executed exploits.
- **Medium: no-record failure messages still reach an unbounded SDK outcome cache (X-18 residual).** `MigrationFailed` computes known but does not drop when false (`migration_handler.rs:913-945`), despite its comment/historical repair promise. Callback inserts distinct unused origins into recent_failures (`sdk/src/compute.rs:640-648`); matching start/shutdown clears them (`1460,695`). An admitted peer needs no matching operation. Drop uncorrelated failures before callback and scope outcomes to owned operations.
- **High: per-origin snapshot reassemblers defeat opportunistic expiry (#99/age-sweep residual).** One abandoned nonempty partial snapshot per novel origin allocates before factory/readiness validation (`migration_handler.rs:588-609,1034-1057,1091-1106`; `core/compute/orchestrator.rs:697-724,967,1116-1124`). Each origin's separate reassembler sweeps only when that origin receives more chunks; other traffic cannot reclaim it and no timed production sweeper was found. A per-entry 64-MiB cap is not an aggregate-origin bound. Correlate/authorize before allocation; own aggregate/per-peer budgets and expiry.
- **Medium: retained completed state authenticates against the old orchestrator.** After T receives under O1, migrates out and expects a later arrival under O2, its still-retained completed entry supplies O1 (`core/compute/migration_target.rs:289-297,449-489`). SnapshotReady rejects O2 before newly configured expectation can bind (`migration_handler.rs:588-609`; `sdk/src/compute.rs:1382-1408`). Active records take precedence only after installation. Scope/retire completed acknowledgements by operation identity. Current authentication manifestation of May 18's stale-completed-state shadowing, not a wholly new root.
- **Qualified medium: multi-chunk restore still trusts wire frontier independently of snapshot frontier (X-10).** Single-chunk forwarding checks through_seq (`core/compute/orchestrator.rs:1461-1507`); after full target assembly, snapshot frontier N drives restore while wire M is acknowledged without comparison (`migration_handler.rs:1064-1073,1138-1149,1181-1184`). Requires inconsistent participant metadata. Compare the fields after reassembly; this is the known still-open multi-chunk half.
- **Qualified medium: registry/negotiation remains standalone metadata despite live-feature docs.** Registry holds descriptors, not handlers (`core/subprotocol/registry.rs:20-22,68-69`); negotiate is pure (`negotiation.rs:176-202`). Core accounts negotiation but has no dispatcher/exchange/enforced negotiated set (`core/mesh.rs:33143-33147,32405-32424`). Custom registration documented in `net/crates/net/docs/SUBPROTOCOLS.md:99-130` and `web/src/content/docs/reference/subprotocol-ids.md:59-78,121-123` does not install a handler; unhandled nonzero IDs are dropped. Searches across supported implementations found no production exchange. Historical May 18 missing-dispatch-integration root, with current drop rather than application-queue fallback. Documentation/support mismatch, not evidence of a runtime downgrade attack.

Subprotocol exclusions: Manifest exact length/overflow/duplicate checks, wire version-floor validation, builder clamping, bilateral minimum compatibility, forwarding-only filtering and deterministic order are present. Explicitly permitted cross-major floors are deliberate. Exhaustive standalone registry limits/public-field invariant bypass were not promoted to supported-consumer remote defects. Bare-handler identity-hook omission is documented; SDK installs hooks. Current MIG-01–04 were compared, not recounted. No production changes, builds/tests or runtime/network executions were made.

## Tenth audit pass

Four source audits completed: Redis/JetStream delivery and deduplication, CLI schema/type generation, generic provider selection/proximity, and policy predicates/rules/safety. The parent inspected CLI channel-link ownership and checked representative returned evidence, including the pinned NATS dependency. This pass adds 45 numbered concerns plus qualified historical residuals/contract limitations, with current sources, guard checks and supported-consumer boundaries. No production changes, builds/tests or CLI/runtime/network executions were performed.

### CLINK-01 — Offline publisher prevents channel expiry/readiness status from refreshing

- **Severity / status:** Low CLI status correctness; source-established refresh dependency, scenario inferred.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/cli/src/commands/channel_link.rs:275-292,324-331`; `cli/src/commands/lifecycle.rs:594-611,1253-1295,1390-1404,3131-3155` under the same crate root; contract `docs/internal/plans/NET_CLI_PLAN_V3.md:2994-3000`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Start with an active, locally trusted publish credential, lose the enrolling publisher, then pass credential expiry while reattachment continues failing. The no-session supervisor branch only calls lost_session and continues; expiry/local publish-trust refresh happens solely in keep after a live session exists. channel/node status serializes the cached link unchanged, so state can remain active and publish_ready true after expiry, or after local trust changes while offline.
- **Guard check / repair:** Core publishing verifies current credential validity/revocation on every publish (`src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:35948-35960`), so this is misleading readiness/status, not expired authorization bypass. Refresh local time/config-derived fields independently of remote attachment, including status reads. No exact historical match found in inspected channel-link/CLI entries.

### CLINK-02 — In-flight supervisor subscribe can win after an acknowledged channel leave

- **Severity / status:** Medium leave/ownership concern; source-established missing quiescence, concurrent schedule inferred.
- **Source:** `cli/src/commands/channel_link.rs:266-313,337-409`; separate supervisor/control consumers `cli/src/commands/lifecycle.rs:617-691,1198-1215,1390-1404,3159-3160`; core `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:35930-35945,36490-36496,36624-36680`; crate root `net/crates/net/`.
- **Trigger / impact:** keep passes its initial left check, then awaits subscribe identity preparation. Concurrent leave records departure, sets left and obtains an unsubscribe ACK. The older keep resumes, emits a valid fresh Subscribe and can restore publisher membership. Its post-await left check suppresses local bookkeeping only; it does not undo the remote subscribe or prevent its transmission. The link stays reported left while the publisher can retain/serve the membership until another cleanup.
- **Guard check / repair:** Atomic left check is after the side effect, not a stop barrier. Ordinary ACK ownership and current credential authorization still hold; no privilege beyond the still-valid credential is claimed. Serialize/quiesce credential-use operations with leave or fence them at a common per-relation lifecycle owner.
- **Provenance:** Explicit CLI claim that a racing supervisor subscribe cannot win (`docs/internal/plans/NET_CLI_PLAN_V3.md:3003-3008`). Related to CHAN-04's wire ordering class, but this schedule emits a new request after leave rather than merely reorders already-sent datagrams; CLI quiescence is independently missing.

Channel-link qualified operational limitation, not another numbered entry: A timed-out/failed unsubscribe leaves a truthful unconfirmed result, but departure was already committed. Subsequent leave returns the prior record/left relation without retrying the unfinished remote stop (`channel_link.rs:349-377`; `lifecycle.rs:623-649`). No pending cleanup obligation/confirmed-result retention exists there. Session teardown remains a way to retire remote membership; a later response saying left is not itself a new unsubscribe ACK. Record this as cleanup/retry scope, not an invented reliable-delivery promise.

Channel-link exclusions: A join store rejects a different invitation token (`enrollment.rs:1968-1987`); the retained departure marker is therefore not demonstrated to poison an unrelated replacement join. Standalone approved rejoin is a distinct incarnation. Managed publish-chain removal is fingerprint-conditional, residual publish credentials are explicitly unconfirmed, and per-action expiry/revocation checks remain present. No tests, CLI/runtime or network executions were run.

Selection notation: `core/` means `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/`; `sdk/` means `net/crates/net/sdk/`. No scenarios were executed.

### SEL-01 — nRPC looks up proximity using entity IDs instead of graph IDs

- **Severity / status:** Medium supported SDK selection correctness; high source confidence.
- **Source:** `core/mesh.rs:1437-1441,14270-14281,24778-24780,35746-35749`; `core/mesh_rpc.rs:7821-7828,7966-7973,8061-8069`; `sdk/src/mesh_rpc.rs:353-359`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Graph nodes are keyed by zero-padded little-endian node u64, while entity_id_for_node returns actual Ed25519 identity bytes. call_service/streaming availability filters and LowestLatency query the latter, miss ordinary populated entries, keep unhealthy candidates and fall back to smallest candidate ID instead of measured latency.
- **Guard check / repair:** Authorization/candidate sorting work; permissive no-data fallback masks the wrong key. Existing discovery tests exercise missing-data fallback, while `tests/three_node_integration.rs:4562-4604` pins graph encoding. Use graph-ID encoding independently of identity pins. Historical claims that the entity-ID bridge works do not match current graph construction.

### SEL-02 — Lost better paths retain authoritative latency while worse paths refresh liveness

- **Severity / status:** Medium chain-holder selection; source-established stale-path lifecycle.
- **Source:** `core/behavior/proximity.rs:363-375,1031-1038`; `core/mesh.rs:14876-14882,48129-48148`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Learn B directly, lose the link, then receive fresh B pingwaves via C. Higher-hop updates never replace cached address/hops/latency, but refresh B's last_seen. Edge removal does not invalidate cached node path metrics; chain-holder ranking can indefinitely prefer B's former direct latency over a genuinely nearer holder.
- **Guard check / repair:** Stale-edge sweeping repairs topology edges, not node path cache; node expiry is prevented by fresh indirect observations. Track selected-path freshness separately from origin liveness and invalidate/reselect when its edge disappears. Converse of May 3 #21's worse-path-overwrite repair, not PART-01/08 callbacks/reroutes.

### SEL-03 — Dedup expiry cadence causes live topology loss below node capacity

- **Severity / status:** Medium scale-dependent production liveness; source-established capacity/cadence mismatch.
- **Source:** `core/behavior/proximity.rs:492-497,780-786,1234-1239`; GC `core/mesh.rs:27046-27075`; ping emission `mesh.rs:3760,33746-33751`.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** Under default 5-second pingwaves, 10-second dedup lifetime, 30-second node lifetime, 40,000 dedup slots and 60-second GC, about 8,000 live origins can fill slots with five sequences well before a sweep despite remaining below the 10,000-node cap. Capacity rejection precedes node/edge refresh. By the next sweep, their last admitted observations can exceed 30 seconds and still-live topology is removed; repeated saturation causes gaps.
- **Guard check / repair:** Reserved quarter helps known origins only until absolute ceiling; expired entries are not reclaimed at admission. Use independent timely expiration or a capacity/admission relationship consistent with actual retention. Related SEC-02 bounded-state repair, not its original unbounded memory root.

### SEL-04 — Endpoint completions lack reservation and probe incarnation ownership

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium direct substrate API concern; high source confidence, no completing group/SDK consumer established.
- **Source:** `core/behavior/loadbalance.rs:365-384,476-483,831-840,1041-1044`.
- **Trigger / impact:** With cap one, select A, remove/re-add its node, select B, then complete A. Completion resolves the current node state, decrements B's reservation and permits another selection while B remains active. Likewise, an older ordinary completion can consume a new half-open probe flag, or a watchdog-reclaimed probe can later resolve its successor.
- **Guard check / repair:** Saturating subtraction prevents underflow, not stale release; CAS protects probe claim, not completion identity. Watchdog does not cancel the real request. Carry endpoint generation and unique request/probe ownership through completion. One ownership root, not three entries. Adjacent historical CR-19/watchdog work; CGRP-01 is missing completion, RESIL-01 another breaker implementation.

Selection known/qualified residuals, not additional numbered entries:

- Weighted round robin still fails non-minimum-multiple ratios (`core/behavior/loadbalance.rs:1199-1210`). Weights 2 and 3 use ceil(5/2)==3 wheel positions, producing one-third/two-thirds depending on snapshot order rather than two-fifths/three-fifths even with matching completions. June 18 #33/exact-commensurate-ratio repair residual; normalize representable ratios correctly.
- **Private SEC-02 reserve-policy residual:** known_origin means only presence in nodes (`core/behavior/proximity.rs:780-782`). An adjacent malicious sender can introduce junk origins below the novel threshold, then use fresh sequences of those now-known origins to consume reserved slots and block legitimate refreshes. Absolute memory bound remains effective. Related to SEL-03 saturation but admission provenance differs from expiration cadence; not independently counted here.
- Generic options metrics_stale_after_ms, health_check_interval_ms, preferred_zones and preserved endpoint priority have no selection-side enforcement in inspected `core/behavior/loadbalance.rs`. Configuration/integration concerns retained without additional demonstrated supported SDK impact or extra numbered entries.
- An older unseen pingwave sequence may overwrite health/load (`core/behavior/proximity.rs:363-375`); current nRPC health consumption is already masked by SEL-01. Source mechanism retained, not independently promoted as another supported-consumer failure.

Selection exclusions: Registry overwrite is atomically refused, lookup clones ownership before callbacks, unknown IDs veto placement, and process-wide sharing is documented. Concurrent snapshot health/config changes alone lack a stronger selection linearization promise. Atomic cap reservation, removed-snapshot exclusion, zone fallback, explicit admission dispatch and zero-recovery clamping are present. CGRP-01, WIRE-13, PART-01/08 and PLMT-05 were not recounted. No files beyond this report were changed; no builds/tests/runtime executions were run.

Backend paths use crate root `net/crates/net/`; shorthand `adapter/`, `consumer/`, `shard/`, `bus.rs`, `config.rs`, `event.rs` and `error.rs` expand under `src/`. All consequences below are source-derived, not runtime reproductions.

### BACK-01 — JetStream enqueues a whole batch before releasing acknowledgement permits

- **Severity / status:** High incomplete-publication concern; high-confidence adapter/dependency mechanism.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/jetstream.rs:328,470-503`; `src/config.rs:315-316,368-371`; locked dependency `Cargo.lock:278-281`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Submit more than 5,000 events. Phase one awaits all enqueues before phase two consumes any PublishAckFuture. In pinned async-nats 0.52.0, each returned acknowledgement retains one of the default 5,000 permits, so later enqueues wait on permits the adapter itself holds. Timeout eventually breaks the cycle, leaving events unpublished; whole-batch retries can repeat it. Concurrent shard batches share the context and can collectively exhaust permits.
- **Guard check / repair:** Default batch maximum is 10,000, high-throughput 50,000; upstream retries do not submit only the unsent suffix. Interleave ordered publication and acknowledgement consumption with bounded in-flight work. Historical #32 parallelized enqueueing but did not close this dependency cycle.
- **Dependency check:** Parent checked cached `async-nats-0.52.0/src/jetstream/context.rs:199-201,557-563,594-603,1935-1973` for capacity, acquisition and permit ownership. No NATS service was contacted.

### BACK-02 — JetStream advances its cursor past the discarded lookahead event

- **Severity / status:** High event omission; source-established supported filtered-poll path.
- **Source:** `adapter/jetstream.rs:740-750`; `consumer/merge.rs:559-573,642-663,813-818,868-903`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Adapter fetches limit+1 good events, updates last_seen_seq for all, drops lookahead and returns its later sequence as cursor. Direct pagination skips that record. Supported bus example: one shard, ConsumeRequest limit one, filter matching only stored record four; merger requests three, adapter reads four/returns first three with cursor four, filter removes all returned rows, then preserves that cursor. Matching record four is never returned.
- **Guard check / repair:** Unfiltered merger reconstructs cursors from returned rows and often hides the defect. Filtered path deliberately preserves adapter progress unless matches were truncated. Advance only past actually consumed/skipped rows, not unreturned lookahead. Historical rejected has_more allegation did not cover this clean-data cursor schedule.

### BACK-03 — Valid JSON null events are classified as corrupt JetStream records

- **Severity / status:** High delivery correctness; source-established contract mismatch.
- **Source:** `adapter/jetstream.rs:152-160`; `event.rs:1-5,19-25`; `bus.rs:1016-1034`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Ingest Event(Value::Null), valid under the opaque-any-JSON event contract. Writer stores r:null, but reader returns Fatal rather than the event; ordinary pagination cannot deliver it or progress beyond it.
- **Guard check / repair:** No ingest non-null restriction exists; Redis preserves the same value. Distinguish a missing required field during decoding from legitimate literal null. Regression in historical #135 missing-r repair; BACK-04 independently concerns exposing actual decode failures.

### BACK-04 — Fatal decode failure is never surfaced when the good prefix is empty

- **Severity / status:** Medium persistent read stall/error masking; source-established.
- **Source:** `adapter/jetstream.rs:691-707`; `consumer/merge.rs:921-944`; `bindings/node/src/lib.rs:750-754`; `bindings/python/src/lib.rs:848-852`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Poll encounters Fatal initially or after returning its good prefix. Every retry returns Ok(empty, unchanged cursor, has_more=true), never the promised Fatal. Merger suppresses has_more on the stall; Rust exposes stalled_shards, but Node/Python discard that diagnostic and can appear drained.
- **Guard check / repair:** Good-prefix preservation works; empty-prefix retry must return the actual error. Named fatal-poll test only tests decode/classification, not poll_shard. Residual of historical #38, not merely null acceptance.

### BACK-05 — Temporary stream creation failures become non-retryable batch failures

- **Severity / status:** Medium event-loss/retry classification concern; source-established.
- **Source:** `adapter/jetstream.rs:212-246`; `error.rs:73-77`; `bus.rs:2233-2259`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Cold shard's first batch encounters temporary get_stream/create_stream timeout or unavailability. Fallback create maps every error to Connection, which bus does not retry, dropping the batch despite remaining retry allowance and an outage shorter than the outer budget.
- **Guard check / repair:** Initialization connects but does not provision all streams. OnceCell permits a future initialization attempt but does not recover this batch. Create only for actual missing-stream responses and preserve transient request classification.

### BACK-06 — Configured JetStream request timeout does not reach publish context

- **Severity / status:** Medium configuration/deadline correctness; source-established dependency gap.
- **Source:** `adapter/jetstream.rs:328`; pinned async-nats `src/jetstream/context.rs:115,376-377,594-603,1946-1949`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Configure request_timeout greater than five seconds for slow publication/acks. Adapter sets client/outer phase budgets but creates a default context, whose independent five-second publish/ack deadline still fails work early.
- **Guard check / repair:** Outer timeout cannot extend an inner one. JetStream API requests use client timeout; this concerns publish/ack context specifically. Apply Context::set_timeout before creating handles. Different from historical F-9's two-phase wall-time coupling.

### BACK-07 — Redis silently ignores advertised pipeline, pool and connection-timeout controls

- **Severity / status:** Medium supported configuration concern; source-established.
- **Source:** `adapter/redis.rs:379-383,450-452`; `config.rs:424-434`; `bindings/node/src/lib.rs:902-915`; `bindings/python/src/lib.rs:491-504`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Set pipeline_size, pool_size or connect_timeout. Adapter creates the default connection manager and puts the whole batch in one atomic pipeline; clones share the underlying connection rather than allocating the requested pool. Advertised command/deadline bounds are not applied.
- **Guard check / repair:** Validation and bus's outer initialization budget do not implement those controls. Honor them without changing atomicity or remove/document unsupported settings. No Redis connection was created for this audit.

### BACK-08 — Existing JetStream streams bypass requested configuration validation

- **Severity / status:** Medium durability/retention/dedup integration concern; high source confidence.
- **Source:** `adapter/jetstream.rs:212-246`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Reopen an existing prefix requesting stronger replication, changed retention or longer dedup window. Existing stream is accepted without compatibility check/update/warning; settings apply only at creation. A historical two-minute window remains two minutes despite configured one hour, allowing retry duplicates within the advertised window. A stream without allow_direct can accept writes but fail polling.
- **Guard check / repair:** Whether to mutate externally managed streams is policy; silently accepting contradictory configuration is the concern. Validate and reconcile supported settings or reject explicitly.

### BACK-09 — Removing an ingestion shard also hides its persisted unread history

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium read-catalogue contract concern; behavior source-established.
- **Source:** `bus.rs:967-977`; `consumer/merge.rs:343-344,546-551`; native poll surfaces `bindings/node/src/lib.rs`, `bindings/python/src/lib.rs`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Remove a shard containing successfully persisted unread Redis/JetStream events. PollMerger is replaced with current ingestion IDs and ignores retired cursor positions, so default polling omits that durable history without self-healing.
- **Guard check / repair:** Explicit Rust shard selection can recover it; Node/Python expose no equivalent selection. “None means all shards” leaves active-only semantics unclear. Separate read catalogue from ingestion membership or explicitly define that boundary. Different from BUS-04/05 data that had not yet persisted.

### BACK-10 — Nonce publication lacks a directory durability barrier

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium filesystem-dependent durability concern; no power-loss reproduction.
- **Source:** `adapter/dedup_state.rs:298-308`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Initialization writes/syncs temporary nonce file, renames and returns success without syncing the parent directory. On filesystems requiring directory fsync for durable rename, power loss may lose the final pathname; restart creates a different identity instead of preserving configured nonce continuity.
- **Guard check / repair:** File write/sync errors propagate. This is rename-metadata publication, not historical swallowed sync errors or Windows read-only-handle issues. Use platform-appropriate durable publication; arbitrary crash replay is not inferred from this concern.

Backend known roots/residuals, not additional numbered entries:

- **High: producer identity outlives allocation ownership (#39/deferred persistent-sequence root).** Persisted nonce survives restart while worker sequence resets (`bus.rs:379-388`; `event.rs:335-338`; `shard/batch.rs:270-277`; `adapter/jetstream.rs:433-447`, `redis.rs:460-465`). Distinct events can reuse dedup IDs and be suppressed. Even same-process buses share the static default nonce with independent counters; same-prefix buses collide without restart. Shared Redis helper across prefixes also lacks prefix in key. Distinct nonce paths/JetStream streams mitigate their respective scopes. Match identity and allocator lifetimes; #56/#57 does not prove new-event uniqueness after restart.
- Redis dedup_id is retained in core (`redis.rs:278-306`) but omitted by Node/Python and structured/JSON FFI conversions (`bindings/node/src/lib.rs:735-746`; `bindings/python/src/lib.rs:833-845`; `src/ffi/mod.rs:1204-1240,1800-1818,1988-2002`). Language helpers cannot identify retry-created records from ordinary poll results. Raw Redis clients can. Historical F-1 consumer-boundary residual, not another wire exactly-once claim.
- Acknowledgement errors bypass the classifier (`jetstream.rs:495-497` vs `802-826`; async-nats `context.rs:1953-1971`): StreamNotFound/wrong-last/structural rejection becomes Transient in phase two despite non-retryable classification in phase one. Finite retries still bound it. Historical #45 residual.
- Redis shutdown gates operations but retains Some(ConnectionManager) (`redis.rs:538-543`). Retaining an Arc<EventBus> after shutdown_via_ref keeps resources until drop; no write-after-shutdown bypass claimed. Historical #37 gate repair did not fulfill connection-release ownership.

Backend exclusions: Accepted Redis timeout duplicates, actual finite server dedup windows and lack of arbitrary durable crash replay are declared limitations. Helper FIFO/no-refresh/capacity behavior is intentional, not a processing ledger. Concurrent nonce creation intentionally uses last rename with unique temporaries; returned-identity divergence is acknowledged. OnceCell does not cache errors permanently. Upstream serializes ordinary per-shard batches; no independent enqueue ordering defect established. Redis excludes its cursor and does not consume lookahead. Polling uses neither XACK nor NATS pull-consumer ACK state. Missing native nonce/retry/dedup knobs remain integration limits, not another default exactly-once promise. BUS-01–05 were not recounted.

Typegen notation: `gen/` means `net/crates/net/cli/src/commands/typegen/`; other abbreviated crate paths use `net/crates/net/`. Examples are inferred renderer output, not executed/generated-package reproductions. Valid schema reachability comes from handwritten Python descriptors (`bindings/python/python/net/tool.py:134-173`), verbatim MCP lowering (`adapters/mcp/src/wrap/descriptor.rs:264-286`) and independently generated Rust input/output schemas (`sdk/src/tool.rs:204-215`). Offline and hydrated live generation share these renderers.

### TG-01 — TypeScript enum arrays and mixed compositions lose precedence

- **Severity / status:** Medium generated type correctness; source-established.
- **Source:** `gen/ts.rs:227-235,250,306-325`.
- **Input / impact:** An array with enum items ["a","b"] renders as `"a" | "b"[]`, accepting scalar "a" but rejecting valid ["a"]. Array parenthesization handles Union/Intersection, not Enum's rendered union. An allOf containing a union similarly loses needed intersection grouping.
- **Guard check / repair:** These supported constructs pass parsing. Track expression precedence or consistently parenthesize composite operands. No exact historical match found.

### TG-02 — Input and output definitions share one generated namespace

- **Severity / status:** Medium conflicting/rebound generated types; source-established.
- **Source:** `gen/ts.rs:75-79,144-149`; `gen/python.rs:287-291,350-354`.
- **Input / impact:** Independent input/output schemas each define Payload, one string-valued and one integer-valued, referencing their own definition. Both sets emit ToolPayload into the same scope. TypeScript interfaces conflict; Python later class definition replaces the earlier name before request/response lowering resolves it. Even identical shared enums duplicate TypeScript aliases.
- **Repair:** Allocate schema-slot namespaces and resolve refs within the originating input/output schema; deduplicate only semantically identical definitions deliberately.

### TG-03 — Basename collision checks miss generated symbol collisions

- **Severity / status:** Medium unusable/rebound generated declarations; source-established.
- **Source:** `gen/mod.rs:275-295,635-678`; `gen/ts.rs:144-149,393-399`; `gen/python.rs:209-215,350-354`.
- **Input / impact:** Tool IDs a_b and a__b have distinct module filenames but both become AB, conflicting in the TypeScript barrel's exported symbols. Definition names foo-bar/foo_bar likewise collapse; a definition named Request can collide with the generated request type. Python inline nested-class names share the unallocated namespace.
- **Guard check / repair:** Filesystem overwrite guard executes but is a different namespace. Allocate collision-aware symbols across definitions, generated reserved names, nested classes and exports.

### TG-04 — Python sanitized property names collide

- **Severity / status:** Medium request/model correctness; source-established.
- **Source:** `gen/python.rs:209-221,659-693`.
- **Input / impact:** Required wire properties a-b and a_b both emit attribute a_b; class and class_ similarly collide. Later annotation/default/alias overwrites earlier declaration, leaving one model field for distinct required wire properties.
- **Guard check / repair:** Ordinary alias support preserves a single renamed key but does not disambiguate attributes. Allocate unique per-class attributes while preserving each original alias.

### TG-05 — Python valid identifiers can be invalid Pydantic field names

- **Severity / status:** Medium generated validation/serialization concern; source-established lowering, Pydantic behavior inferred.
- **Source:** `gen/python.rs:659-668,536`.
- **Input / impact:** Required _id is emitted unchanged without alias; Pydantic treats underscore-prefixed attributes as private, losing the required wire field. model_config also passes identifier validation but collides with Pydantic configuration.
- **Repair:** Reserve model/private names, generate safe public attributes and preserve original wire aliases. Python identifier/keyword checks alone are insufficient.

### TG-06 — Python keyword tool IDs produce invalid package imports

- **Severity / status:** Medium generated package syntax; source-established.
- **Source:** `gen/mod.rs:659-678`; `gen/python.rs:584-589`.
- **Input / impact:** Valid tool ID from becomes module from and root initializer `from . import from as from`; class/async similarly break Python syntax, making the selected generated package unimportable.
- **Guard check / repair:** Module sanitizer handles digits/punctuation, not Python keywords. Apply language-specific reserved-name handling before filenames/imports, not only field attributes.

### TG-07 — Python alias declarations precede their dependencies

- **Severity / status:** Medium generated import correctness; source-established.
- **Source:** `gen/python.rs:350-354,445-452`.
- **Input / impact:** Nonrecursive definitions A=array(ref Z), Z=object produce ToolA=list[ToolZ] before defining ToolZ. Ordinary assignment evaluates immediately and raises NameError; future annotations do not defer aliases or class bases.
- **Guard check / repair:** Requires no unsupported recursive schema. Topologically order alias/base dependencies or use a correctly deferred representation.

### TG-08 — Python aliases and literals are not completely escaped

- **Severity / status:** Medium generated source safety/correctness; source-established escaping gaps.
- **Source:** `gen/python.rs:503-509,651-654`.
- **Input / impact:** Property names containing quotes are inserted raw into Field(alias="..."), breaking syntax; backslashes can change the effective key. Enum strings containing newlines are emitted with a literal newline inside a quoted string because py_str escapes only quotes/backslashes. Schema-controlled unescaped source also creates an injection risk when generated code is later executed; no executable payload was constructed or run.
- **Repair:** Use one complete Python string encoder for aliases, enum/const values and metadata literals.

### TG-09 — Python helpers discard explicitly supplied valid nulls

- **Severity / status:** Medium wire request correctness; source-established.
- **Source:** `gen/python.rs:535-538`.
- **Input / impact:** Required nullable string x is supplied as None, valid under its schema. model_dump(exclude_none=True,by_alias=True) sends {}, turning explicit null into a missing required field. Nested and optional explicitly supplied nulls are also lost.
- **Repair:** Omit genuinely unset fields while preserving explicit nulls, rather than dropping every None.

### TG-10 — Model-valued refs and allOf roots bypass Python model serialization/validation

- **Severity / status:** Medium generated helper contract; source-established.
- **Source:** `gen/python.rs:239-265,302-315,535-546`.
- **Input / impact:** Root ref to an object becomes an alias to a Pydantic class; supported allOf refs become a subclass. has_request_model/has_response_model recognize only literal Object roots, so helpers pass model instances to the dict-taking client without dumping and return raw response dicts despite model return annotations.
- **Repair:** Determine model behavior from resolved/lowered types, not the root enum variant alone.

### TG-11 — Python client protocol contradicts supported non-object root types

- **Severity / status:** Medium generated typing contract; source-established.
- **Source:** `gen/python.rs:540-565`.
- **Input / impact:** Root string input generates ToolRequest=str but helper passes it to _MeshLike.call's fixed dict parameter. Root string output declares str while protocol returns dict and helper returns that unchanged. Arrays/scalars therefore produce internally contradictory helpers under the advertised strict typing contract.
- **Guard check / repair:** Structural-client design does not imply dict-only schemas. Model JSON-valued inputs/results correctly and validate/convert declared outputs.

### TG-12 — Object/array enum and const values become invalid Python Literals

- **Severity / status:** Medium accepted-schema lowering concern; high source confidence.
- **Source:** `gen/python.rs:151-156,198-200,633-647`.
- **Input / impact:** Valid property const {"ok":true} becomes Literal[{"ok":true}]; JSON true is not a Python expression, and containers are not valid typing.Literal members regardless. Array/object enum members are likewise accepted by parsing without a generation warning/rejection.
- **Guard check / repair:** Float members have special handling, not containers. Represent these values appropriately or reject unsupported shapes before publishing unusable generated code.

### TG-13 — Local refs retain escaped JSON Pointer segments instead of resolving them

- **Severity / status:** Medium undefined generated references; source-established.
- **Source:** `gen/schema.rs:254-258`; `gen/ts.rs:251`; `gen/python.rs:176`.
- **Input / impact:** Definition a/b referenced by #/$defs/a~1b emits from a/b as AB, but ref derives A1b from its raw suffix. Both generators reference an undefined symbol from a valid local pointer; ~0 has the corresponding escape issue.
- **Guard check / repair:** Prefix validation is not pointer resolution. Decode/resolve pointer segments before symbol lookup.

### TG-14 — Composition parsing silently discards supported sibling constraints

- **Severity / status:** Medium accepted-schema weakening; source-established.
- **Source:** `gen/schema.rs:153-174`.
- **Input / impact:** Object requires sibling property a and has allOf requiring b. Parser returns only Intersection(allOf), losing a; generated TypeScript permits omitting required a, and Python's documented intersection fallback loses the sibling too. Draft 2020-12 ref siblings are similarly discarded.
- **Guard check / repair:** Individual keywords are supported and parsed result validates. Combine applicable constraints or reject unsupported combinations explicitly rather than first-keyword short-circuiting.

### TG-15 — Tuple lowering discards optional-prefix and trailing-item semantics

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium tuple-shape support concern; accepted schema exceeds retained representation.
- **Source:** `gen/schema.rs:240-245`; `gen/ts.rs:237-240`; `gen/python.rs:138-145`.
- **Input / impact:** prefixItems string/integer, items:false, minItems:1 permits ["x"] and ["x",1], but generators emit fixed two-element tuples. When trailing items are permitted, those are lost as well.
- **Repair / qualification:** This is valid accepted prefixItems input, not malformed internal AST. Preserve minimum length/trailing policy or explicitly reject/document shapes the tuple representation cannot express. No runtime validation or generated compiler check was run.

### TG-16 — Removing a tool does not trip the breaking-change exit gate

- **Severity / status:** Medium false-green compatibility gate; source-established.
- **Source:** `gen/diff.rs:91-96`; `gen/mod.rs:396-407`.
- **Input / impact:** Remove the only previously advertised tool. report.removed is populated but breaking_count counts only surviving tools' changes, so typegen diff --exit-code succeeds although old callers lose the tool.
- **Guard check / repair:** Removal test checks list membership, not exit gating. Include removals in breaking status/count.

### TG-17 — Object diffs ignore additional-property admission/type changes

- **Severity / status:** Medium false-green input compatibility; source-established.
- **Source:** `gen/diff.rs:206-298`.
- **Input / impact:** Change input additionalProperties true→false without changing named fields: no change reported, though existing extra-key requests now fail. A typed additional-property schema string→integer is equally invisible.
- **Guard check / repair:** Unlike documented shallow-ref/array limitations, this omission is not in the inspected caveat. Compare extra admission/value contracts with input/output direction.

### TG-18 — Enum widening hides simultaneous nullability narrowing

- **Severity / status:** Medium false-green input compatibility; source-established.
- **Source:** `gen/diff.rs:336-350`.
- **Input / impact:** Change nullable enum ["a"] to nonnullable enum ["a","b"]. strip_null exposes enum comparison, whose added-member nonbreaking return bypasses removed-null detection. Existing null callers break while exit gate succeeds.
- **Repair:** Accumulate membership/nullability changes or combine breaking flags before returning. Distinct from documented shallow-reference limitations.

Typegen qualified/shared-root concerns, not additional numbered entries:

- Output variance uses documented input-style widening heuristics (`gen/diff.rs:250-254,270-280,336-355`). Required→optional, nonnullable→nullable and enum widening may break existing output consumers but are marked nonbreaking. This is a disclosed heuristic/design limitation, not another undisclosed implementation/docs mismatch; propagate schema direction for meaningful output compatibility.
- Live hydration rejects lossy-normalized tags (`gen/live.rs:83-85,139-146`; `sdk/src/tool.rs:145-156`; `src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:46058-46061`; `src/adapter/net/cortex/tool.rs:179-187`). Free-form " research " or comma-containing tags are accepted unchanged, published via comma joining, discovered through splitting/trimming, then compared to original full descriptors. Hydration can fail metadata validation on an ordinary accepted tool, including when absent output metadata triggers hydration despite inline input. Concrete consumer extension of the shared lossy metadata-encoding root, not counted as wholly independent serialization. Preserve/compare one lossless representation.

Typegen exclusions: Behind-ref/array-of-object diff shallowness is disclosed (`gen/diff.rs:263-266,388-393`; `net/crates/net/docs/cli/TYPEGEN.md:190-196`). Python unsupported intersections→Any is historical review #22 and documented; typed extras becoming untyped allowed extras is explicit. Streaming helpers are deferred. Strict treatment of omitted additionalProperties and boolean-false→unknown parsing are explicit choices. Existing publication/stale-file/filesystem/security concerns were not recounted. Requested IDs, tag intersection, bounded discovery, conflicting-replica rejection and shared hydration deadlines have their inspected guards. No files were generated and no compilers/interpreters/tests/runtime scenarios were executed.

Policy notation: `behavior/` means `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/behavior/`; other crate paths use `net/crates/net/`. SafetyEnforcer/RuleEngine findings concern public standalone APIs: no production engine wiring was established. Predicate decoding and capability requirements have supported SDK/placement consumers. No resource-exhaustion/deadlock scenarios were executed.

### POL-01 — Small flat predicate DAG expands exponentially during decoding

- **Severity / status:** High conditional resource-exhaustion concern; source-established expansion, no exploit execution.
- **Source:** `behavior/predicate.rs:735-750,767-773,920-928`; `sdk/src/mesh_rpc.rs:248-256`; `src/ffi/predicate.rs:104-112`; `behavior/meshdb/executor.rs:579-590`.
- **Input / impact — [INFERENCE]:** Flat wire table contains a leaf followed by And nodes each naming its predecessor twice. child<parent validation passes, but recursive reconstruction independently expands shared subtrees into owned ASTs, producing exponential allocation/work before evaluation or short-circuiting. An RPC handler that invokes where_predicate reaches this decoder.
- **Guard check / repair:** Cycle/root validation and 4096-byte RPC-header cap do not bound expanded nodes; flat JSON avoids nesting-depth protection. Budget expanded work/nodes before construction or enforce a bounded tree representation, including depth. Historical N-6 byte-limit repair did not address expansion inside accepted bytes. This is not a claim that every RPC automatically decodes the header.

### POL-02 — Automatic kill reset deadlocks on its retained timestamp read guard

- **Severity / status:** Medium standalone API deadlock; high source confidence, no concurrent caller required.
- **Source:** `behavior/safety.rs:1550-1556,1501-1504`.
- **Input / impact:** Configure auto_reset_secs Some(0), call kill then check/acquire. if-let scrutinee retains kill_switch_at's read guard through its body; reset requests that same lock for writing, blocking itself. Positive auto-reset intervals reach the same branch after elapsed time.
- **Guard check / repair:** drop(envelope) releases a different guard. Copy timestamp out in a separate scope before reset. No exact historical match located.

### POL-03 — Active kill checks recursively read a writer-contended envelope lock

- **Severity / status:** Medium standalone concurrency deadlock; source-established lock schedule, not reproduced.
- **Source:** `behavior/safety.rs:1120-1135,1171-1183,1543-1550`.
- **Schedule / impact:** A holds envelope.read in check/acquire. B queues update_envelope's write. A's active-kill helper requests another ordinary read; parking_lot writer-aware locking can block it behind B, while B waits for A's first read.
- **Guard check / repair:** Disabled mode/inactive kill avoid the nested path, not the active concurrent schedule. Pass already borrowed configuration rather than recursively reacquiring. Different lock/root from POL-02.

### POL-04 — Kill-switch configuration and runtime state diverge

- **Severity / status:** Medium standalone configuration correctness; source-established.
- **Source:** `behavior/safety.rs:1102-1113,1120-1123,1543-1567`.
- **Input / impact:** update_envelope enabled false→true changes reported config but leaves runtime atomic false, continuing admission; reverse update leaves an active runtime kill. Constructing enabled with reason/auto-reset initializes atomic true but neither reason nor timestamp, losing configured reason and preventing timed expiry from starting.
- **Guard check / repair:** Explicit kill/reset manipulate state; config update does not. Define one authoritative state and coherently apply construction/transitions. Deliberate Disabled enforcement mode bypass is not the finding; no deployed kill-switch wiring was established.

### POL-05 — Updated audit settings do not update the active audit log

- **Severity / status:** Medium standalone logging/configuration concern; source-established.
- **Source:** `behavior/safety.rs:954-976,991-994,1103,1120-1123`.
- **Input / impact:** Construct audit disabled, then enable through update_envelope: AuditLog retains its old config clone and records nothing. Disabling, filter changes and retention reductions likewise do not affect actual logging despite updated envelope reporting them.
- **Guard check / repair:** Resource/rate/content settings borrow current envelope; audit uses its private construction snapshot. Update log state/retention atomically with configuration. Different from historical AuditOnly violation suppression.

### POL-06 — Declared request execution-time limit is never enforced

- **Severity / status:** Medium standalone API contract concern; source-established ignored declared limit.
- **Source:** `behavior/safety.rs:68-69,333-334,364-366,1185-1202,1619-1675`.
- **Input / impact:** Set max_time_ms 100 and acquire a with_time_ms(101) claim. Admission never compares time; ResourceType::Time/Timeout have no enforcement producer, and guard elapsed merely reports duration.
- **Guard check / repair:** Other resource/rate limits do not cover time. At minimum reject over-budget declared claims; explicitly define any actual deadline/check mechanism rather than imply a guard cancels execution. No deployed timeout failure asserted.

### POL-07 — Quota rollback subtracts an old acquisition from a new window

- **Severity / status:** Medium standalone admission availability/ownership concern; source-established cross-window race.
- **Source:** `behavior/safety.rs:603-620,813-820,1348-1369`.
- **Schedule / impact:** Acquire global RPM just before reset. Token acquisition crosses boundary, runs maybe_reset and fails, e.g. positive claim against zero token budget. rollback_global_rpm subtracts from the new zero counter and wraps to u64::MAX, rejecting requests until reset. Source rollback similarly uses current_floor rather than acquired floor and can erase another request's new-window count.
- **Guard check / repair:** Individual atomic increments do not establish reservation epoch. Carry epoch-bearing receipts through rollback. Historical #8 check/add and #125 bucket-reset repairs do not close this ownership boundary.

### POL-08 — Actual-token updates do not reconcile minute-rate accounting

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium standalone accounting contract; no production update caller beyond units established.
- **Source:** `behavior/safety.rs:395-417,1348-1356,1604-1608`.
- **Input / impact:** Under 100-token minute cap, acquire estimated ten, update_tokens(100), then acquire 90. Update changes usage.tokens/guard claim only; minute limiter still sees ten, admitting more after actual usage reached 100. Downward updates conversely retain the original minute debit and can over-reject.
- **Guard check / repair:** Estimated-token reservation is enforced correctly; no retroactive prevention of consumed work is implied. Define actual-versus-reserved accounting and reconcile within the correct acquisition epoch/overage policy. Different from historical subtraction-underflow repair.

### POL-09 — Zero-execution rule budget permits its first execution each window

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium standalone edge case; source-established accepted zero configuration.
- **Source:** `behavior/rules.rs:1236-1261,1268-1302`.
- **Input / impact:** Always-matching action with with_rate_limit(0,300) executes initially because absent state returns true, and again after each expired window because comparison only runs within an unexpired window.
- **Guard check / repair:** Setter/registration do not reject zero; evaluate/evaluate_first share the gate. Enforce zero before shortcuts or reject unsupported zero budgets. Historical #118 positive-budget reset-to-one is not this edge.

### POL-10 — Stale source-bucket refresh bypasses an effective zero cap

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium standalone rate-enforcement edge; source-established.
- **Source:** `behavior/safety.rs:482-495,740-778`.
- **Input / impact:** Configure per_source_rpm zero with permissive other limits. A failed acquisition leaves a bucket; once stale, next acquire resets it to count one and succeeds without checking zero cap. check would reject the same request.
- **Guard check / repair:** Fresh-bucket zero check works. acquire is independently public, so requiring preflight is not its enforcement contract. Validate the first increment of every window or consistently reject zero configuration. New boundary in historical #125 packed-bucket repair, not original lost-write/reset race.

### POL-11 — Strict inference default checks the wrong model tag key

- **Severity / status:** Medium supported placement/discovery correctness; source-established producer/requirement mismatch.
- **Source:** `behavior/required_capability.rs:71-74`; `behavior/placement.rs:750-767,1372-1387`; `behavior/tag_codec.rs:543-550`; `behavior/capability.rs:1582-1588`; `behavior/fold/capability_bridge.rs:773-793`.
- **Input / impact:** With GPU-presence requirement satisfied, advertise models through canonical set_models tags and score an inference artifact under Strict intent matching/default registry. Producer emits software.model.0.id etc.; default AxisKey requires exact software.model, rejecting the correctly populated model host despite its comment promising any software.model.*.
- **Guard check / repair:** Fold preserves tags without inventing parent marker. Manual software.model or disabling intent masks it. Fix the default to recognize the indexed collection; do not broaden every exact AxisKey predicate. Different from TSDK-02 first-value matching.

Policy known residuals/design limits, not additional numbered entries:

- Disabled-mode ResourceGuard owns no counted resources but Drop still decrements (`behavior/safety.rs:1174-1179,1436-1482`). Hold it across Disabled→Enforce, acquire a real guard, then drop old one: saturating release removes the live guard's counters and admits excess work. Historical #102 prevented wrapping only; its test drops the guard before switching (`2159-2201`). Record actual acquisition ownership, not merely saturation.
- AuditOnly non-rate violations remain suppressed (`safety.rs:1690-1699`), and acquire skips rather than observes/logs resource checks (`1187-1288`). Historical #20 corrected rate logging, not these branches.
- require parsing still treats operators inside valid ordinary tag values as expressions (`behavior/required_capability.rs:152-186`): software.id:release>=1 becomes a numeric expression rather than that tag value. Tag splitting accepts it (`behavior/tag.rs:425-454`). Residual of the operator-inside-value/equality-order root, not a new parser class.
- Hourly cost is refunded on guard drop (`safety.rs:1477-1482`) so sequential completed requests do not accumulate against “max total cost per hour.” Tests deliberately pin refunding (`2204-2239`): contract/design conflict, not unambiguously accidental behavior.
- Safety “regex” patterns are substring matches (`safety.rs:1751-1777`), acknowledged in `RELEASE_v0.24_MONEY_FOR_NOTHING.md:51`; Redact logs without modifying content and is tested that way (`1714-1723,2790-2803`); Custom is an explicit placeholder (`1779-1784`). These are known advertised limits, not newly discovered deployed enforcement claims.

Policy exclusions: Rust value predicates scan all matching value tags, reject JavaScript-only numeric forms, and exclude presence tags from value predicates; TSDK-02/03 were not copied onto Rust. Documented floating precision/special values and deliberate semver policies are not new defects. Match-based stop_on_match includes cooldown/rate-suppressed actions by contract, not another AGG-07. Partial RuleSet load has no established transaction guarantee/affected supported consumer. acquire separates resource acquisition from content preflight. Query has no current reference-index implementation; inspected aggregation handles multiple values/nonfinite maxima. No builds/tests/runtime/exploit executions were performed.

## Eleventh audit pass

Four source audits are running: typed task/memory views, durable device/standalone enrollment, Deck snapshots/streams/actions, and ACME/TLS bootstrap. The parent is inspecting SDK member views. Findings will be appended incrementally with exact evidence, supported-consumer/contract qualifications and historical overlap. No production edits or runtime/network executions are planned.

### MVIEW-01 — Signed subnet observations silently truncate live member results

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium inventory-completeness concern; source-established truncation, scale condition explicit.
- **Source:** `net/crates/net/sdk/src/members.rs:24-26,280-317,428-451`; core live view `net/crates/net/src/adapter/net/mesh.rs:20960-20981`.
- **Trigger / impact — [INFERENCE]:** A verifier has more than 1,024 live matching subnet contexts. answer_members takes the first MAX_SUBJECTS and signs an Observed response without a truncation flag, total count or continuation. The underlying peer map is unordered, so the root cannot distinguish a complete local observation from an arbitrary subset, nor deterministically page through the rest.
- **Guard check / repair:** Request authority/signature, named verifier, freshness, live session/credential epoch/expiry and response count bounds are checked. “One node at one time, never a roster” limits global authority, but does not communicate this additional local result omission. Return signed partial/continuation evidence or explicitly reject over-cap views rather than imply a complete local inventory. No exact historical member-view match found.

Member-view exclusions: Observation verification binds the expected entity key and exact nonce-bearing signed request; no stale-response substitution against a fresh request was established. Core admitted-subnet reads recheck current session, epochs and expiry. Unary calls advertise large-response support (`src/adapter/net/mesh_rpc.rs:8241`), so response size alone was not promoted as another one-packet failure. No runtime/member-scale scenario was executed.

TLS paths use crate root `net/crates/net/`. Source-derived deployment/cancellation scenarios were not executed; pinned dependency behavior was checked locally without ACME/HTTPS connections.

### TLS-01 — ACME domain extraction includes the HTTPS port

- **Severity / status:** Medium normal CLI deployment correctness; source-established.
- **Source:** `cli/src/commands/anchor.rs:1271-1277`; `sdk/src/rtc_bootstrap_acme.rs:526-529`.
- **Input / impact:** Valid URL https://anchor.example.com:8443 becomes domain anchor.example.com:8443 through prefix removal/slash splitting, then enters the DNS identifier for ordering. Explicit :443 fails likewise; the configured listener default is 8443.
- **Guard check / repair:** No intervening URL-host extraction strips authority components. Operator PEM bypasses this path. Parse URL and use DNS hostname only. No exact historical match located.

### TLS-02 — Staging and production reuse the same domain certificate cache

- **Severity / status:** Medium certificate trust/availability; source-established cache namespace mismatch.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/rtc_bootstrap_acme.rs:72-75,96-110,118-127,175-199`.
- **Input / impact:** Obtain staging certificate, then switch directory_url to production with same domain/cache. Cache hit returns staging certificate before account selection; hostname/key/time qualification accepts it, so ordinary browsers reject the served anchor. Normal renewal timing can defer replacement for roughly 60 days of a 90-day certificate.
- **Guard check / repair:** Accounts are directory-scoped, certificates are not. Changing/deleting cache mitigates. Include issuing-directory identity in certificate and renewal namespaces. Different from historical R4 wrong-domain cache repair.

### TLS-03 — Stalled ACME I/O can block startup or the sole renewal task indefinitely

- **Severity / status:** High availability concern; source-established missing operation deadline, unexecuted I/O schedule.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/rtc_bootstrap.rs:1690-1691`; `sdk/src/rtc_bootstrap_acme.rs:467-469,575-578`.
- **Trigger / impact:** ACME HTTPS peer completes TLS then stalls headers or response body. Startup never finishes; during renewal the one task never reaches error backoff/another attempt, and the currently served certificate can expire.
- **Guard check / repair:** Inbound bootstrap handshake/idle limits do not bound outbound issuance. Pinned instant-acme 0.8.5 `src/lib.rs:60-75,199-225` directly awaits HTTP/body without a complete deadline; `src/order.rs:169-181` awaits refresh outside RetryPolicy's wait budget. Custom-root Hyper client has no complete deadline either. Bound account/authorization/finalization/retrieval as one operation; retry policy alone is not I/O cancellation.

### TLS-04 — Default-trust ACME branch unexpectedly selects a global crypto provider

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium embedding/configuration concern; dual-provider panic not present in current Ring-only lockfile.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/rtc_bootstrap_acme.rs:421-469`; pinned instant-acme 0.8.5 `src/lib.rs:202-208`, hyper-rustls 0.27.10 `src/connector/builder.rs:89-97`, rustls 0.23.46 `src/crypto/mod.rs:243-285`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Empty directory_roots uses Account::builder→platform verifier→ClientConfig::builder, installing a process-global provider contrary to explicit-provider intent. An embedding dependency set enabling both providers without a preinstalled default panics during issuance/renewal instead of returning BootstrapError.
- **Guard check / repair:** Custom-root branch explicitly selects Ring; the no-global-provider test exercises only that branch. Current Ring-only workspace avoids ambiguous selection, not ordinary global installation. Use explicit provider with platform verification too.

### TLS-05 — Cancelled ACME startup detaches challenge listeners without returning an owner

- **Severity / status:** Medium SDK cancellation/port retention; source-established ownership gap.
- **Source:** `sdk/src/rtc_bootstrap.rs:1938-1978`, particularly `1960-1964`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Startup binds challenge ingress tasks, then certificate acquisition waits on network. SDK timeout/select drops startup future, dropping JoinHandles rather than aborting/joining tasks. Challenge sockets remain bound with no BootstrapHandle returned; retry on same address fails.
- **Guard check / repair:** Explicit Err cleanup and completed-handle shutdown own listeners correctly. Token RAII clears tokens, not tasks. Give tasks cancellation-safe ownership until transfer. Examined CLI callers await directly; this is an SDK case. Distinct cancellation exit adjacent to R4 explicit-error repair, not accepted-HTTP-connection shutdown.

### TLS-06 — Predictable shared temporary cache trusts another local user's certificate

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium local-adversary availability concern; multi-user Unix/shared-temp condition, not a Windows runtime reproduction.
- **Source:** `cli/src/commands/anchor.rs:1284-1287`; `sdk/src/rtc_bootstrap_acme.rs:72-75,96-110,175-199`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Another user precreates /tmp/net-mesh-acme and domain directory with matching self-signed key/certificate, correct SAN and current validity. Default startup reuses it, skips issuance and presents a certificate browsers reject.
- **Guard check / repair:** Existing cache reuse checks no directory ownership/symlink/path permissions; newly written key mode does not authenticate preexisting cache. Requires shared temporary defaults/local adversary; private per-user paths mitigate. Default to private persistent user storage and validate cache ownership/path conditions. Different from historical R5 new-key-mode window; no key-theft/remote cache-access claim made.

TLS exclusions: Prior accepted-HTTP-task shutdown root was not recounted. Historical R4 guards for cold plaintext ingress, matching key/leaf, SAN precedence, full validity window, token cleanup and explicit-error listener cleanup are present. Single configured certificate/HTTP1 routing has no independent SNI/ALPN defect established. Operator PEM reload requires documented restart. Custom roots replace trust without disabling hostname verification; missing CLI custom-root switches alone are not a bug. Publication tears/short-lived renewal pacing are acknowledged limits. Game-anchor credential roots were excluded. No production changes or runtime/network executions were made.

Typed-view paths use crate root `net/crates/net/`. No query/watch scenario was executed.

### TVIEW-01 — ASCII needle optimization loses valid Unicode lowercase matches

- **Severity / status:** Medium supported task/memory filter correctness; source-established.
- **Source:** `src/adapter/net/cortex/needle.rs:29-56`; consumers `cortex/tasks/query.rs:85-89` and `cortex/memories/query.rs:69-73` under `src/adapter/net/`.
- **Input / impact:** Searching k in Kelvin-sign K returns false although lowercasing K produces k. Searching K itself also fails because needle construction lowers it to ASCII k. i against İ similarly differs from the promised lowercase-and-contains behavior. Task titles/memory contents are silently omitted from queries and shared-filter watches, including native list/watch consumers.
- **Guard check / repair:** ASCII haystacks and needles remaining non-ASCII use correct existing paths. ASCII-only decision checks the needle, not haystack's Unicode mapping. Restrict byte-only fast path to ASCII haystacks too, or preserve equivalent Unicode semantics. Existing regression omits these mappings; no exact historical match found.

### TVIEW-02 — Closing an idle native task/memory iterator retains its core watch

- **Severity / status:** Medium supported native/TS resource lifecycle; source-established.
- **Source:** `bindings/node/src/cortex.rs:1633-1670,1831-1834`; `bindings/python/src/cortex.rs:1854-1856,1893-1896,2442-2444,2479-2482`; `src/adapter/net/cortex/tasks/watch.rs:154-183`, `memories/watch.rs:177-226`; `sdk-ts/src/watch-iter.ts:23-31,40-47`.
- **Schedule / impact:** Consume initial watch batch, close while no next is pending, retain iterator. close only sets flag/notifies; stream remains stored. A later next returns before taking/removing it. Receiver/worker/materialized results remain owned and may continue filtering/enqueueing until backpressure or destruction, although iteration is closed. TS loop-return cleanup reaches this ineffective native close when its WatchIterable remains retained.
- **Guard check / repair:** Pending next observing shutdown clears stream; object destruction and FFI cursor free release it. Close must drop stored stream independently of another reader, while canceling any active read. Existing tests check termination/idempotence, not resource release. Different from CTX-03 failed adapter open/drop; earlier SDK cleanup exclusion proved close was called, not that it released native state.

Typed-view known residual: Historical #160 skips seq_or_ts==MAX in `cortex/watermark.rs:133-137`, but MAX−1 still advances app_seq to MAX; next typed fetch_add wraps (`tasks/adapter.rs:637`, `memories/adapter.rs:586`). Requires extreme same-origin raw event/crafted snapshot, not normal typed traffic; retain as counter-overflow residual, not another numbered root.

Typed-view exclusions: Watch coalescing/intermediate-state omission is deliberate/tested. Inclusive timestamp-boundary duplication is historical CR-20. No tie-order/cursor promise established another equal-timestamp defect. Foreign task/memory restore resets destination replay coverage, so WFLOW-01 does not apply. Re-store preserves pin/creation time; delete/reinsert has no promised tombstone-generation semantics. All task/memory dispatch values have consuming folds. CTX-03/04/07 and NETDB lifecycle roots were not recounted.

Enrollment notation: `enroll/` means `net/crates/net/sdk/src/enrollment/`; other crate paths use `net/crates/net/`. Scenarios are source-derived, not reproduced.

### ENR-01 — Expired channel credentials make intact durable memberships unreadable

- **Severity / status:** High supported restart/offline-management correctness; source-established.
- **Source:** `enroll/standalone.rs:855-862`; `enroll/device.rs:357-358`; `enroll/bundle.rs:563-575,774-806`; CLI `cli/src/commands/lifecycle.rs:564-590,1837-1855,1939-1949,3476-3483,3901`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Install channel-bearing join/standalone membership, stop, reopen after chain expiry plus 60-second skew. Durable decoding calls channel_chain_matches→current-time verify_authorizes, classifying intact signed historical state as Corrupt. Ordinary up fails; one expired standalone entry can fail all membership loading. Offline leave opens those stores first and cannot normally erase it.
- **Guard check / repair:** Runtime explicitly supports reporting expired credentials without use (`channel_link.rs:185-211`); core separately checks current validity (`src/adapter/net/identity/token.rs:429-442,945-950`). Separate durable structural/signature/offer integrity from present admission validity, retaining expiry gates at install/use. Different from CLINK cached readiness/racing leave.

### ENR-02 — Writer persists issuance timestamps its own decoder rejects after clock rollback

- **Severity / status:** Medium issuer-ledger restart correctness; source-established invariant mismatch.
- **Source:** `enroll/store.rs:670-690,1159-1163`; `enroll/policy.rs:116-123`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Approval-required claim at T, wall clock regresses to T−1 while invitation remains valid, then approve/redeem. Issue checks invitation window but not issued_at>=claimed_at, persists success. Reopen rejects that record and consequently the whole ledger, affecting unrelated invitations too.
- **Guard check / repair:** Policy lower bound is invitation creation, not claim time. Checksums/durable replacement preserve rather than prevent semantically invalid data. Enforce decode invariant before mutation and return resumable time/clock error. No exact historical match located.

### ENR-03 — Final issuance check reuses time sampled before durable claim work

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium first-issuance timing/authorization concern; source-established stale timestamp, boundary interpretation explicit.
- **Source:** `enroll/service.rs:342-351`; `enroll/standalone.rs:343,383-397`; `enroll/store.rs:686`; `enroll/bundle.rs:844-846`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Preauthorized redemption starts one second before expiry; durable claim/bundle preparation takes two seconds within the normal session timeout. Service then calls issue with the old time, accepting an unissued invitation after its first-issuance window. Standard issuer can sign the accompanying receipt with actual post-expiry time. Standalone path can sample before ledger-lock wait too.
- **Guard check / repair:** Ledger rechecks policy against its supplied time, not current time; session deadline is not the invitation boundary. Resample after durable claim/credential preparation before final authorization. If claim-time linearization is intended instead, specify it and keep receipt/ledger semantics consistent. Different from expressly permitted recovery of already committed issuance after expiry; no executed verifier bypass claimed.

### ENR-04 — Receipt compaction also deletes continuing subnet renewal authorization

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium public SDK composition; no automatic production compaction caller established.
- **Source:** `enroll/store.rs:791-802`; `enroll/renew.rs:155-165`.
- **Trigger / impact:** Issue subnet membership, compact after invitation expiry plus receipt-recovery deadline while issuer grant remains valid. Compaction deletes entire record; later genuine signed renewal fails invite-digest/issued-subject lookup, eventually losing ongoing admission despite invitation TTL not being membership lifetime.
- **Guard check / repair:** Device signature/floor verification cannot recover deleted authorization. Generic deletion is documented; the missing relationship is renewal lifetime versus secret receipt retention. Retain compact membership authorization independently. Tests cover deletion or retained-record renewal separately, not their composition.

### ENR-05 — SDK accepts Mesh-less subnet-plus-org invitations no service can redeem

- **Severity / status:** Qualified medium SDK-only irredeemable input; source-established accepted/consumer mismatch.
- **Source:** `enroll/invite.rs:624-630,729-731`; `enroll/service.rs:322-324`; `enroll/standalone.rs:333-337`; CLI guard `cli/src/commands/enrollment.rs:913-921`.
- **Input / impact:** Sign canonical Subnet+Org relations with matching offers/approval policy but no Mesh. Noise service rejects all Mesh-less invitations; standalone dispatch accepts only one exact standalone relation. Public signing/recording can therefore produce an invitation neither supported consumer redeems.
- **Guard check / repair:** CLI already rejects multiple standalone relations, so no CLI failure asserted. Reject this SDK shape at signing/decoding or implement its explicit dispatch. Related to old Mesh-less PSK-disclosure boundary, not that fixed disclosure root.

Enrollment exclusions: NAT-14/ANCHOR-01–06 were not recounted. Standalone subnet retries deliberately mint fresh leaves; channel retries recover committed chain; fixed recovery expiry is explicit. Renewal replay still produces credentials for the genuine signed subject, not a demonstrated unauthorized enrollment. Core validates subnet signatures/windows/envelopes/floors independently, so SDK field-only checks do not establish a verifier bypass. Storage has exclusive ownership, protected bounded reads and uncertainty fencing; no generic rename-durability repeat. Derived standalone Debug was not promoted without a production logging consumer. No production edits, builds/tests or runtime/network executions were performed.
